339 — Supply Chain Security Scanner

Project Number: 339

Project Name: Supply Chain Security Scanner

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

Goal: Implement, from scratch in Rust, a software supply-chain security scanner: multi-ecosystem manifest/lockfile parsers (npm, Cargo, PyPI, Go, Maven) producing Package-URL components, transitive dependency-graph resolution, SPDX/CycloneDX SBOM generation + ingestion, an advisory engine that syncs OSV/NVD/GHSA feeds and matches components by per-ecosystem version-range satisfiability, provenance + signature verification (Sigstore/cosign, in-toto, SLSA, Rekor inclusion proofs), malicious-package heuristics (typosquatting, dependency confusion, suspicious install scripts), and a license + security policy engine with VEX/waivers that gates CI via SARIF/JSON reports (crates scs-core/manifest/ resolve/sbom/vulndb/match/provenance/threat/policy/report). It scans and analyzes — it never installs or builds packages, is not a runtime sandbox, and is not itself an advisory database. Validated by differential tests against OSV-Scanner/Grype on fixture projects and manifest-parser fuzzing.

1. Goal

1.1 Engineering Definition

Supply Chain Security Scanner (SCS): a static-analysis tool that ingests project manifests/lockfiles, constructs the transitive dependency graph, generates or ingests SBOMs, matches components against vulnerability advisories via version-range satisfiability, verifies provenance/signatures, detects malicious-package patterns, and enforces policy — all without installing, building, or executing any code.

1.2 System Boundaries

Boundary Inside Outside
Input Manifests, lockfiles, SBOMs, advisory feeds, attestations Package registry network calls during scan (feeds pre-synced)
Computation Parse → resolve → match → verify → policy → report Package installation, build, runtime execution
Advisory DB Feed ingestion + local index (OSV/NVD/GHSA) Hosting an advisory database
Provenance Sigstore/cosign verification, in-toto validation, Rekor log queries Key generation, signing, certificate issuance
Licensing SPDX expression evaluation against policy Legal advice, transitive license inference beyond declared SPDX

1.3 Explicit Non-Goals

  • NOT a package manager; never resolves version constraints by downloading packages
  • NOT a runtime sandbox; no execution-based analysis
  • NOT a first-party SAST engine; does not analyze application source semantics
  • NOT an advisory database; consumes OSV/NVD/GHSA feeds, never hosts or edits them
  • NOT a CI/CD platform; integrates into pipelines but does not orchestrate them
  • NOT a container scanner (future phase); manifest-level only in Phase 0–5
  • NOT a patch application tool; reports fixed versions, never applies them

1.4 Deployment Model

  • Local CLI: scs scan <project-dir> — developer workstation, single-process
  • CI mode: non-zero exit on policy failure, SARIF + JSON output to artifact store
  • Offline mode: feed snapshot bundled or pre-synced; no mandatory internet during scan
  • Server/API mode (PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register")): daemon with REST API for IDE integration
  • Container image: single static binary, <20 MB compressed

1.5 Target Users

User Context Key Need
Developer Pre-commit / local scan Fast feedback, minimal FP, fix suggestions
Security engineer Policy authoring + triage VEX, waiver, severity tuning
Platform/DevOps CI gate Deterministic exit codes, SARIF, PR annotations
Auditor / compliance SBOM generation + policy audit SPDX/CycloneDX output, feed-snapshot provenance

1.6 Phase-0 Deliverables — Crate Inventory

Numbered locked deliverables, each a Rust crate (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78):

  1. scs-core — canonical types: Purl, PackageId, Version, VersionReq, Component, DependencyGraph, Advisory, AffectedRange, Finding, Severity/Cvss, Sbom, Provenance, License, Policy, ScsError
  2. scs-manifest — per-ecosystem parsers (npm/Cargo/PyPI/Go/Maven/Gemfile), purl generation, lockfile-vs-manifest reconciliation, hash extraction
  3. scs-resolve — transitive graph construction, constraint solving, dedup, cycle detection, dev/prod/optional scopes
  4. scs-sbom — SBOM emit + parse: SPDX 2.3 + CycloneDX 1.5/1.6, component inventory, relationships, round-trip fidelity
  5. scs-vulndb — OSV/NVD-CVE/GHSA feed sync, normalization, per-ecosystem version-range index, CPE + purl mapping
  6. scs-match — version-range satisfiability per ecosystem, CVE↔GHSA↔OSV correlation, fixed/withdrawn/disputed handling
  7. scs-provenance — Sigstore/cosign signature verification, in-toto attestations, SLSA level classification, Rekor inclusion proofs, hash pinning
  8. scs-threat — typosquatting/dependency-confusion detection, suspicious install-script heuristics, entropy/obfuscation scoring, maintainer/age signals
  9. scs-policy — allow/deny rules, severity thresholds, SPDX license compliance, VEX consumption, waivers/ignores, gate decision
  10. scs-report — SARIF 2.1, JSON, table, SBOM diff, exit codes, CI/PR annotations
  11. scs-cli — CLI driver: argument parsing (clap), configuration loading, orchestration of scan pipeline
  12. scs-bench — fixture corpus + differential regression + performance harness

1.7 Phase Blueprint Summary

Phase Unlock
0 Crate scaffold, core types, Cargo + npm parsers, OSV feed, basic match + report
1 All 6 ecosystem parsers, full graph resolution, SBOM emit/parse
2 NVD/GHSA feeds, CPE matching, CVSS/EPSS scoring, VEX
3 Sigstore/cosign, in-toto, Rekor, SLSA level detection
4 Threat heuristics (typosquatting, dep confusion, install scripts)
5 Policy engine: license compliance, waivers, gating; CI integration
6 SARIF + PR annotations, SBOM diff, dashboard JSON
7 Container image layer scanning (PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register"))
8 Server/API mode, IDE plugin integration (PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register"))
9 ML-based anomaly scoring (PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register"))

1.8 Deferred Features

  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — REST API / daemon mode
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — OCI container layer + image manifest scanning
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — ML-based maintainer behavior anomaly scoring
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — eBPF runtime dependency tracing (reachability)
  • PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") — Registry mirror / private artifact repository support

1.9 Timeline

  • Phase 0 scaffold + core types + 2 ecosystems + OSV match: 6–14 weeks
  • Full Phase 0–6 implementation (all ecosystems, all advisory feeds, provenance, threat, policy, reporting): 6–12 months
  • Each subsequent deferred phase: 4–8 weeks incremental

1.10 Language Rationale

Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78) — mandatory, not negotiable:

  • Safe parsing of dozens of untrusted manifest/lockfile formats (memory-safety without GC)
  • Fast graph algorithms over large transitive trees (Petgraph, zero-copy adjacency)
  • Native crypto primitives: ring/RustCrypto, signature verification without FFI risk
  • Sigstore Rust SDK (sigstore-rs), cosign verification, Rekor TLS log queries
  • Deterministic builds → reproducible scan results (no GC non-determinism)
  • SemVer crate (semver 1.x), PEP 440 implementation (pep440-rs), Maven version ordering
  • Strong type system encodes ecosystem-specific version semantics at compile time
  • Rejected Go: Trivy/Grype precedent (portfolio differentiation), GC pauses in large graph traversal, less safe parse-error handling
  • Rejected Python: parsing performance, type-safety for security-critical matching logic

1.11 Prerequisites

Domain Required Knowledge
Systems Memory layout, zero-copy parsing, owned vs. borrowed data (Rust lifetimes)
Concurrency Rayon work-stealing, Tokio async I/O, Arc<RwLock<T>> advisory index
Graph algorithms DAG construction, DFS/BFS, cycle detection, topological sort, shortest-path (dep chain)
Cryptography ECDSA/Ed25519 signature verification, X.509 cert chains, TLS transparency logs, SHA-256/512
Networking TLS/HTTPS feed fetching, rate-limiting, retry with backoff, Rekor gRPC/REST
Package ecosystems SemVer, PEP 440, Maven version ordering, Go pseudo-versions, lockfile semantics
Security CVSS v3/v4, EPSS, VEX, SLSA levels, in-toto link metadata, supply-chain threat taxonomy

2. Why Build This

2.1 Historical + Architectural Significance

  • SolarWinds (2020): build-system compromise injected malicious code into signed binaries — SLSA/provenance verification directly addresses this class
  • event-stream (2018): malicious maintainer takeover of npm package with 2M weekly downloads — maintainer signal + behavioral heuristics directly address this
  • log4shell (2021): critical CVE in transitive Java dependency, unknown to most consumers — transitive graph resolution + advisory matching is the direct technical counter
  • xz-utils backdoor (2024): sophisticated multi-year social engineering + backdoor in compression library — build-provenance verification + SLSA enforcements are the mitigation layer
  • Supply chain attacks are the primary attack vector escalation since 2020; OSS ecosystem growth (~4M+ npm packages) makes manual review impossible
  • SLSA, in-toto, Sigstore are the industry consensus standards; this project implements all three

2.2 Unique Systems Knowledge Developed

Muscle Mechanic
Multi-format parser engineering Parsing 6+ manifest/lockfile formats safely in Rust; handling malformed/adversarial inputs
Graph algorithm application Transitive closure, cycle detection, shortest-path dep chain in real-world DAGs (10k+ nodes)
Version algebra Implementing SemVer, PEP 440, Maven ordering + range intersection; soundness proofs
Cryptographic protocol integration X.509 chain validation, ECDSA/Ed25519 verification, Rekor transparency log inclusion proofs
Feed synchronization OSV/NVD/GHSA delta sync, normalization across incompatible schemas, index consistency
Policy DSL design Expressing security + license policy as a composable rule engine; VEX interaction
CI/CD integration SARIF 2.1, exit-code semantics, PR annotation protocols, deterministic artifact output
Threat modeling from heuristics Operationalizing typosquatting detection, dependency confusion, entropy scoring

2.3 Engineering Muscles Exercised

  • Correctness under adversarial input: manifest parsers must never panic or leak on malformed files
  • Soundness vs. completeness tradeoff: version-range matching must be sound (no false-negatives) at cost of some FP; documented, not hidden
  • Determinism as a first-class invariant: identical inputs → identical output across runs, platforms, and parallelism modes
  • Fail-closed security properties: provenance failure = scan failure, not silent pass-through
  • Information-dense data modeling: Component, Advisory, Finding, AffectedRange encode all matching semantics without ambiguity
  • Cross-ecosystem abstraction: single Version trait dispatching to semver/pep440/maven/go pseudo-version backends

2.4 Production Engineering Concepts

Concept Where Exercised
Observability tracing spans per phase, structured JSON logs, metrics (components scanned, advisories matched, latency)
Feed consistency snapshot timestamps on every scan; stale-feed detection + warning
Incremental computation dep-graph diffing between lockfile revisions; advisory index incremental update
Error propagation ScsError hierarchy with context chain; no unwrap() in library code
Test harness design Fixture corpus, differential regression vs. Grype/Trivy, property-based version-range tests
Performance profiling scs-bench micro + macro benchmarks; cargo flamegraph integration

2.5 Cross-Portfolio Links

Related Project Connection
Compiler/Parser (language front-ends) Same safe-parsing discipline; Rust combinator approach vs. recursive descent
Consensus / distributed KV Feed sync consistency mirrors log-structured KV; Rekor is a transparency log (append-only Merkle)
Storage engine Advisory index design parallels LSM/B-tree index; range query patterns identical
Task scheduler Scan pipeline parallelism (rayon + tokio) mirrors work-stealing scheduler design
Networking stack TLS feed fetching, gRPC to Rekor, HTTP advisory APIs — direct networking stack integration
Vector DB Advisory semantic similarity (future phase) parallels vector embedding search patterns
Package manager (hypothetical) Complement: where a PM resolves + installs, this tool analyzes the resolved graph

2.6 Calibration Value

  • Demonstrates ability to reason about multi-ecosystem version algebra (SemVer ≠ PEP440 ≠ Maven)
  • Shows cryptographic protocol integration beyond "call library" — transparency log inclusion proofs require Merkle tree verification
  • Evidences security-engineering mindset: fail-closed defaults, sound matching, no silent downgrade
  • Graph algorithms at production scale (petgraph over real npm/Maven transitive trees: 10k–100k nodes)
  • Policy engine design: composable rules, VEX override semantics, waiver audit trail
  • Portfolio signal: mirrors capabilities of Grype, Trivy, OSV-Scanner but purpose-built in Rust

2.7 Why Not Extend an Existing Tool

  • Grype/Syft (Go): portfolio value requires ownership of every algorithmic layer
  • OSV-Scanner (Go): excellent reference but no provenance, no threat heuristics, no policy engine
  • Trivy (Go): monolithic; doesn't compose as a library; no Sigstore integration in core
  • Building from scratch forces deep understanding of version-range algebra, graph construction, and advisory normalization that using a library hides

2.8 What Mastery Looks Like

After completing all 14 phases the practitioner will have gained:

  • Version-algebra fluency (Phases 0-2): ability to implement from memory the half-open range semantics [introduced, fixed) for SemVer, PEP 440, and Maven independently and explain where each ecosystem's model diverges from pure SemVer
  • Graph construction and traversal (Phase 2): ownership of petgraph-based transitive dependency resolution including cycle detection via Tarjan SCC, scope propagation (prod/dev/optional), and canonical deduplication under version constraints
  • Advisory index design (Phase 4): ability to design and implement an inverted index over per-ecosystem version ranges that supports incremental feed sync, ETag-aware deduplication, and multi-source alias unification (OSV ↔ CVE ↔ GHSA)
  • Provenance chain verification (Phase 7): hands-on command of Sigstore keyless signing, cosign bundle verification, Rekor Merkle inclusion proofs, and in-toto attestation policy evaluation at the level needed to implement fail-closed verification without a library abstraction
  • Supply-chain threat detection (Phase 8): ability to enumerate and detect typosquatting, dependency confusion, malicious install scripts, and entropy anomalies at the signal level, and to reason about which signals produce actionable findings vs noise
  • Policy engine and VEX (Phase 9): ability to design a rule evaluation engine that correctly applies CVSS/EPSS thresholds, SPDX license expression algebra, OpenVEX suppression status, and waiver expiry to produce a deterministic gate decision
  • SBOM and SARIF ecosystem fluency (Phase 3, Phase 10): ability to produce and consume conformant SPDX 2.3 and CycloneDX 1.5/1.6 output, explain the relationship and property mapping differences between the two formats, and author SARIF 2.1.0 results that integrate cleanly with GitHub/GitLab CI annotation APIs

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical Data-Flow Diagram

    project manifests/lockfiles (package.json, Cargo.lock, go.sum, pom.xml, requirements.txt)
            |
      [scs-manifest] parse per-ecosystem -> Components (purl, version, hashes)
            |
      [scs-resolve] transitive dependency graph (constraint solve, dedup, cycles, dev/prod scopes)
            |
            +--> [scs-sbom] emit/ingest SPDX / CycloneDX inventory
            |
      [scs-match] <---- [scs-vulndb] (OSV / NVD / GHSA feeds, per-ecosystem version-range index)
            |  version-range satisfiability -> Findings (component x advisory + dep path)
            |
      [scs-provenance] verify Sigstore/cosign signatures, in-toto/SLSA attestations, Rekor inclusion, hash pinning
      [scs-threat]     typosquatting / dependency confusion / install-script + obfuscation heuristics
            |
      [scs-policy] severity thresholds + SPDX license + VEX + waivers -> gate (pass/fail)
            |
      [scs-report] SARIF / JSON / table, SBOM diff, CI exit codes

3.2 scs-manifest — Parse Boundary

  • Responsibility: ingest project root (or explicit paths), detect manifest/lockfile types, parse per-ecosystem into Vec<Component>
  • Ecosystems: npm (package.json + package-lock.json/yarn.lock/pnpm-lock.yaml), Cargo (Cargo.toml + Cargo.lock), PyPI (requirements.txt, Pipfile.lock, poetry.lock, pyproject.toml), Go (go.mod + go.sum), Maven (pom.xml), Ruby (Gemfile + Gemfile.lock)
  • Trust boundary: all inputs treated as adversarial; parsers use serde-based deserializers, never eval/exec; malformed inputs return ScsError::ParseError, never panic
  • Purl generation: deterministic mapping from (ecosystem, name, version) → canonical pkg:<ecosystem>/<name>@<version>
  • Lockfile-vs-manifest reconciliation: detect lockfile staleness (manifest constraint not satisfiable by locked version); flag as Finding::LockfileStale
  • Hash extraction: SHA-256/SHA-512 from lockfiles where available; forwarded to scs-provenance for pinning checks
  • Concurrency: parsers run in parallel via rayon across detected manifests (bounded thread pool, MAX_PARSE_THREADS=8)
  • Output: Vec<Component> + ManifestMeta { ecosystem, manifest_path, lockfile_path, parsed_at }

3.3 scs-resolve — Graph Construction

  • Responsibility: build DependencyGraph (DAG) from Vec<Component> + manifest-declared constraints; compute transitive closure; classify scope
  • Algorithm: BFS from root components; version-constraint resolution against declared ranges; dedup by (purl, version) identity
  • Cycle detection: DFS with grey/black coloring; cycles in DAG are ScsError::CyclicDependency (some ecosystems permit cycles in dev deps)
  • Scope classification: Scope::Prod / Scope::Dev / Scope::Optional; scopes propagate transitively (prod dep of dev dep → dev)
  • Dedup: multiple paths to same (purl, version) → single Component node, all inbound edges retained for path completeness
  • Output: DependencyGraph { roots: Vec<Purl>, nodes: HashMap<Purl, Component>, edges: Vec<(Purl, Purl, Scope)> }
  • Invariant: graph is a DAG post-cycle-removal; every node reachable from at least one root; path to root calculable via reverse-BFS

3.4 scs-sbom — SBOM Boundary

  • Responsibility: emit DependencyGraph as SPDX 2.3 or CycloneDX 1.5/1.6; parse external SBOMs into Vec<Component> for downstream matching
  • SPDX emit: SPDXDocument { packages, relationships (DESCRIBES/DEPENDS_ON/CONTAINS), snippets, annotations }
  • CycloneDX emit: BOM { metadata, components, dependencies, compositions }
  • Round-trip invariant: emit-then-parse preserves all Component fields + relationship topology (tested by scs-bench fixture corpus)
  • Protocol boundary: SBOM files are the inter-process boundary in pipelines; format versioning enforced at parse
  • SBOM ingestion: accept external SBOMs as primary input (bypasses scs-manifest + scs-resolve); feeds directly into scs-match
  • Hashes: SHA-256 and SHA-512 per component; required for provenance cross-check

3.5 scs-vulndb — Advisory Index

  • Responsibility: sync OSV JSON feed, NVD CVE API v2, GHSA GraphQL API; normalize to Advisory; build per-ecosystem range index
  • Feed sources: OSV (osv.dev/v1/...), NVD (services.nvd.nist.gov/rest/json/cves/2.0), GHSA (api.github.com/graphql)
  • Normalization: map all to Advisory { id, aliases, affected: Vec<AffectedRange>, severity: Cvss, fixed_versions, withdrawn }
  • AffectedRange semantics: half-open [introduced, fixed) — introduced is the first vulnerable version, fixed is the first non-vulnerable
  • Per-ecosystem index: HashMap<Ecosystem, BTreeMap<PackageId, Vec<Advisory>>> — O(log n) lookup by name
  • CVE↔GHSA↔OSV correlation: alias graph; a single vulnerability may have entries in all three; deduplicated by primary ID
  • Feed freshness: every sync records FeedSnapshot { source, fetched_at, entry_count, digest } — stored alongside index
  • Persistence: SQLite (via rusqlite) or flat JSON files; configurable; advisory index is append-only per snapshot
  • Concurrency: feed sync is async (tokio); reads are concurrent; writes are single-writer per source

3.6 scs-match — Matching Engine

  • Responsibility: for each Component in DependencyGraph, query scs-vulndb index, evaluate version-range satisfiability, emit Vec<Finding>
  • Version-range satisfiability: per-ecosystem dispatch — SemVer ranges, PEP 440 specifiers, Maven version ranges, Go pseudo-version ordering
  • Half-open range check: introduced <= version < fixed; inclusive last_affected semantics handled explicitly
  • Soundness invariant: any component version inside an AffectedRange is ALWAYS reported; no suppression except via VEX/waiver
  • Path inclusion: every Finding carries path: Vec<Purl> — shortest dependency chain from component to a graph root (BFS reverse)
  • Withdrawn/disputed: Advisory.withdrawn → Finding.vex_status = Disputed; still reported, never silently dropped
  • EPSS integration: if EPSS score available, attached to Finding.epss; used in severity ranking, never for suppression
  • Output: Vec<Finding> sorted by (severity DESC, purl ASC) for deterministic ordering

3.7 scs-provenance — Signature Verification

  • Responsibility: verify Sigstore/cosign signatures, in-toto link/layout attestations, SLSA provenance predicates, Rekor transparency log inclusion, hash pinning
  • Sigstore: verify ECDSA-P256/Ed25519 signatures against Fulcio-issued certificates; check Rekor inclusion proof (Merkle path verification)
  • in-toto: parse link.json and layout.json; verify step signatures + threshold; validate material/product hashes
  • SLSA: extract provenance predicate from in-toto attestation; classify as SLSA Level 0–3 per spec
  • Rekor: gRPC or REST query to rekor.sigstore.dev; verify inclusion proof (Merkle consistency proof against checkpoint)
  • Hash pinning: compare Component.hashes (from lockfile) against registry-fetched hashes and/or attestation hashes; mismatch → Finding::HashMismatch
  • Fail-closed invariant: any verification failure → Provenance.verified = false → Finding::ProvenanceFailed; never treated as verified
  • Trust boundary: external: Rekor TLS + certificate transparency; internal: verified attestation objects only

3.8 scs-threat — Heuristics Layer

  • Responsibility: statically detect supply-chain attack patterns without executing code
  • Typosquatting: edit-distance (Levenshtein ≤ 2) + homoglyph substitution against popular-package name list per ecosystem
  • Dependency confusion: detect internal/scoped package names (e.g., @company/pkg) published on public registries at higher versions
  • Install-script detection: parse package.json.scripts.{preinstall,install,postinstall}; flag shell commands, network calls, eval patterns
  • Obfuscation/entropy: Shannon entropy of install-script content; base64-encoded payloads; Buffer.from(...,'hex') patterns
  • Maintainer/age signals: package age < 7 days + first publish + install scripts = high suspicion; requires registry metadata (fetched at feed sync, not scan time)
  • Output: Vec<Finding> with FindingKind::ThreatHeuristic { signal, confidence }; confidence score 0.0–1.0

3.9 scs-policy — Gate Layer

  • Responsibility: apply user-defined policy to Vec<Finding> → PolicyDecision { pass: bool, blocked: Vec<Finding>, warnings: Vec<Finding> }
  • Policy inputs: Policy { severity_threshold, license_deny: Vec<SpdxExpr>, license_allow, vex_file: Option<Path>, waivers: Vec<Waiver> }
  • Severity threshold: findings at or above severity_threshold (CVSS 7.0 default) → block
  • SPDX license compliance: evaluate component License expression against deny/allow policy; copyleft detection
  • VEX consumption: OpenVEX or CSAF VEX; not_affected + justification → suppresses finding; affected + action_statement overrides
  • Waivers: Waiver { purl_pattern, advisory_id, expires, reason }; expired waivers → ScsError::WaiverExpired
  • Audit trail: every suppressed finding records suppression reason + waiver/VEX reference
  • Output: PolicyDecision + PolicyAuditLog { applied_rules, suppressions, gate_result }

3.10 scs-report — Output Layer

  • Responsibility: serialize (PolicyDecision, Vec<Finding>, DependencyGraph, Sbom) into target formats; set process exit code
  • SARIF 2.1: SarifLog { runs: [{ tool, results, artifacts }] }; location = manifest file + line where possible
  • JSON: canonical ScsReport { meta, findings, policy_decision, sbom_ref, feed_snapshot }
  • Table: human-readable terminal output via comfy-table; severity color-coding
  • SBOM diff: compare two SBOMs (e.g., before/after PR); emit added/removed/changed components
  • CI exit codes: 0 = pass, 1 = policy failure (blocked findings), 2 = scan error, 3 = stale feed warning
  • PR annotations: GitHub Actions / GitLab CI annotation format; maps findings to manifest file locations
  • Determinism: output ordering is stable (purl ASC, advisory_id ASC); timestamps excluded from hashed output

3.11 scs-cli — Orchestration Driver

  • Entry point: clap-based CLI; subcommands scan, sbom, db sync, db status, report diff, policy check
  • Configuration loading: scs.toml (project), ~/.config/scs/config.toml (user), env vars (SCS_*), CLI flags (precedence: CLI > env > project > user)
  • Pipeline orchestration: sequential phase execution with tokio for async I/O (feed sync, Rekor queries) and rayon for CPU-bound parsing/matching
  • Concurrency budget: --jobs flag controls rayon thread count; default = logical CPU count, max 16
  • Feed snapshot pinning: --snapshot <id> for reproducible offline scans; default = latest synced

4. System Constraints

4.1 Constraints Table

Constraint Target Hard Limit Notes
Memory — scan process ≤ 512 MB RSS 1 GB RSS Full transitive graph in memory; large Maven trees worst case
Memory — advisory index ≤ 256 MB resident 512 MB SQLite page cache for NVD/GHSA; evictable
Startup latency ≤ 200 ms 500 ms Time to first manifest parse; excludes feed sync
Scan latency p50 (medium project, 500 deps) ≤ 2 s 5 s Parse + resolve + match; no network; local index
Scan latency p95 (large project, 5k deps) ≤ 15 s 30 s Full npm transitive tree; matching against 250k advisories
Scan latency p99 (monorepo, 50k deps) ≤ 120 s 300 s Multi-manifest parallel; graph merge + full match
Feed sync latency (OSV full) ≤ 5 min 15 min Initial full sync ~500k entries; incremental < 30 s
Feed sync latency (NVD delta) ≤ 60 s 5 min NVD API rate limit: 5 req/30s without API key; 50 req/30s with
Throughput (findings/s) ≥ 50k component-advisory pairs/s — Rayon parallel match; in-memory BTreeMap index
Max dependency graph nodes 100k nodes 500k nodes Petgraph adjacency list; ~800 bytes/node at 100k = 80 MB
Max advisory index entries 500k advisories 2M advisories NVD ~230k + GHSA ~15k + OSV ~700k as of 2025
Concurrency — rayon logical CPU count 16 threads --jobs override; manifest parse + match parallelized
Concurrency — async I/O tokio multi-thread 4 worker threads Feed sync + Rekor queries; bounded by network, not CPU
Network — Rekor RTT p50 ≤ 300 ms p99 ≤ 2 s TLS handshake + inclusion proof query per component
Network — feed sync ≤ 10 MB/s inbound — Rate-limited; retry with exponential backoff (max 5 retries)
File descriptors ≤ 128 per scan 512 total Manifest files + SQLite + Rekor TLS sockets
Disk — advisory index ≤ 2 GB 5 GB SQLite + JSON snapshots; configurable retention
Disk — report output ≤ 10 MB 100 MB SARIF/JSON; SBOM full inventory
Shutdown ≤ 1 s clean 5 s forced Drop all open handles; no partial write to report
Availability (CLI) N/A (stateless) — No SLA; deterministic binary
Feed freshness SLA ≤ 24 h stale 72 h (warn) Stale feed → Finding::StaleFeed warning in report
SBOM round-trip fidelity 100% component + relationship preservation — Invariant; tested by scs-bench
Match soundness 0 false-negatives for in-range versions — Hard invariant; property-based tested
Determinism bit-identical output for identical inputs + snapshot — No timestamps, no random ordering in output

4.2 CPU + Cache Assumptions

  • Advisory BTreeMap lookups: L2/L3 cache-resident for hot ecosystems (npm/PyPI top-1000 packages hit repeatedly)
  • Graph traversal: petgraph adjacency list is pointer-chased; L3 misses dominate for >10k node graphs; rayon partition by root for cache locality
  • Parser parallelism: each manifest parser operates on independent data; no shared mutable state; zero lock contention
  • SIMD: aho-corasick for install-script pattern matching; uses SIMD automatically on x86-64/ARM

4.3 Scaling Model

Axis Vertical Horizontal
Scan throughput More rayon threads (--jobs) Parallel scan per project in CI matrix
Advisory index Larger SQLite page cache Shared NFS/network advisory index (PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register"))
Feed sync Faster disk I/O Separate sync process / pre-built snapshots
Monorepo --workspace flag parallel manifest parse CI shards by workspace member

4.4 Failure Budget

Failure Class Acceptable Rate Action
Feed sync failure ≤ 1 failure/day Retry + stale-feed warning; never block scan
Parse error (malformed manifest) ≤ 1% of manifests Log + skip; report as ScsError::ParseError
Rekor timeout ≤ 5% of provenance checks Fail-closed: Provenance.verified = false
Advisory index corruption 0 tolerance Detect via digest check; re-sync on corruption
Scan crash (panic) 0 tolerance in lib code All panics are bugs; catch_unwind at CLI boundary

4.5 Resource Ceilings — Enforcement

  • RSS ceiling enforced via rlimit in CI mode (SCS_MAX_RSS_MB); scan aborts with ScsError::ResourceExhausted if exceeded
  • Feed sync is interruptible; partial sync is committed atomically (SQLite transaction) or rolled back
  • All file handles closed before report write; report is written atomically (temp file + rename)
