6. Design Principles

6.1 Scan Determinism — Identical Input → Identical Output

  • Every internal map/set sorted before serialization; all collections use BTreeMap/BTreeSet where order is observable.
  • Advisory feed identified by (source_url, snapshot_id, fetched_at); snapshot id stored in every scan result.
  • Random/time-dependent state forbidden in matching logic; seeds to any RNG must be fixed or absent.
  • Tradeoff: BTreeMap ~10–20 % slower than HashMap for large graphs; acceptable — correctness > perf.
  • Operational: two scans on same commit + same feed snapshot must produce bit-identical JSON; CI diffing is meaningful.
  • Violation consequence: non-deterministic findings break diff-based triage; engineers distrust results; suppressed false alerts accumulate.

6.2 Match Soundness — No False Negatives on Version-Range Queries

  • AffectedRange semantics: half-open [introduced, fixed) — a component at exactly fixed is NOT affected.
  • Off-by-one on exclusive bound is a false negative, a safety defect.
  • Match logic uses per-ecosystem version comparators (SemVer, PEP440, Maven) — no string comparison shortcuts.
  • Withdrawn advisories: always reported with withdrawn timestamp; scs-policy may waive, but scs-match never silently skips.
  • Tradeoff: soundness over precision; some false positives are preferable to missed vulnerabilities.
  • Operational: property-based tests with proptest cover entire version-space lattice per comparator.
  • Violation consequence: a critical CVE silently skipped becomes a breach; legal/compliance liability.

6.3 Provenance Fail-Closed — Verification Failures Are Failures

  • Any error in Sigstore/cosign/Rekor/in-toto verification → Provenance { verified: false } + Finding of severity ≥ Info.
  • Network timeout fetching Rekor → explicit ScsError::RekorUnavailable; scan continues but provenance section marked unverified.
  • No default-to-trusted fallback; partial verification (cert valid, inclusion proof missing) = unverified.
  • Tradeoff: may generate noise when Rekor is transiently unavailable; operators use --offline flag + cached state.
  • Operational: configurable timeout per verification step; structured error in JSON report with retry hint.
  • Violation consequence: attacker-controlled artifact with stripped signature treated as verified; integrity bypass.

6.4 Path Completeness — Every Finding Carries Full Dependency Chain

  • Finding.path: Vec<Purl> = ordered chain from affected component to a graph root (direct dependency of the project).
  • For diamonds/merges, report the shortest root path; if multiple shortest paths exist, pick lexicographically first (determinism).
  • Path reconstruction: BFS from each affected node during scs-resolve graph traversal; stored in Finding at match time.
  • Tradeoff: memory: storing paths for large graphs with many findings can be significant; cap path storage at 64 hops; log warning if truncated.
  • Operational: path enables triage ("is this reachable?"); without it, engineers cannot determine upgrade target.
  • Violation consequence: path-less finding forces engineers to manually trace graph; triage time multiplies; misassignment of fix ownership.

6.5 Bounded Resource Usage — Memory and CPU Limits Are Enforced Inline

  • DependencyGraph node count cap: configurable, default 50 000 nodes; hard error if exceeded.
  • Advisory index memory cap: configurable; feed ingestion uses streaming deserialization (serde Deserializer), never slurps full feed JSON.
  • Regex / typosquatting heuristics: regex crate with size_limit + dfa_size_limit set; exponential-backtrack patterns forbidden.
  • File-size limits on every manifest/lockfile input (default 64 MiB); reject with ScsError::InputTooLarge.
  • Tradeoff: limits may reject pathologically large monorepos; configurable with audit log when overridden.
  • Operational: scs_resource_limit_triggered counter metric; alert on repeated triggers.
  • Violation consequence: malicious/malformed manifest causes OOM; CI runner crash; denial of service of the scanner itself.

6.6 Ecosystem Isolation — Per-Ecosystem Logic Has No Cross-Ecosystem Coupling

  • Each ecosystem parser in scs-manifest is a self-contained module; version comparator in scs-match dispatched by Ecosystem enum variant.
  • No ecosystem shares version-comparison code unless via an explicit shared trait implementation (e.g., VersionComparator).
  • Adding a new ecosystem requires: new parser, new comparator impl, new advisory-range mapping — nothing else changes.
  • Tradeoff: some duplication (e.g., similar range-algebra for SemVer-like ecosystems); prefer explicit duplication over implicit coupling.
  • Operational: ecosystem-specific test fixtures in scs-bench/fixtures/<ecosystem>/; each independently runnable.
  • Violation consequence: a bug in npm SemVer comparator silently affects PyPI matching if code is shared without guard.

6.7 Observability by Default — Every Operation Emits Structured Telemetry

  • tracing spans wrap every phase: manifest parse, resolve, sbom emit, vulndb query, match, provenance verify, policy eval.
  • Every span carries: ecosystem, purl, advisory_id, duration_ms, finding_count where applicable.
  • Metrics (via metrics crate): scs_findings_total{severity, ecosystem}, scs_match_latency_ms, scs_feed_age_seconds, scs_graph_nodes, scs_provenance_verified{result}.
  • Structured JSON logs at RUST_LOG=info sufficient to reconstruct scan timeline; DEBUG adds per-component trace.
  • Tradeoff: adds ~5 % overhead in hot match loops; acceptable per priorities ordering (observability > perf).
  • Operational: OpenTelemetry export via opentelemetry-otlp; Prometheus scrape endpoint optional.
  • Violation consequence: silent scan failures with no signal; operators cannot distinguish "no vulns found" from "scan crashed halfway".

6.8 Feed Freshness Auditability — Every Scan Records Its Advisory Snapshot

  • ScanReport.feed_snapshot: Vec<FeedRef { source, snapshot_id, fetched_at }> — one entry per advisory source.
  • Snapshot id = SHA-256 of feed content or provider-supplied ETag/version; fetched_at = RFC3339 UTC.
  • Offline mode: scan proceeds with last cached snapshot; report records cache age; policy may gate on max_feed_age.
  • Tradeoff: snapshot ids increase report size minimally (~200 bytes); mandatory for compliance audit trails.
  • Operational: scs_feed_age_seconds metric; alert if feed > 24 h stale.
  • Violation consequence: scan with 6-month-old advisory data appears current; newly disclosed CVEs silently missed.

6.9 SBOM Round-Trip Fidelity — Emit-Then-Parse Preserves All Components and Relationships

  • SPDX/CycloneDX emitter and parser are tested with round-trip property: parse(emit(sbom)) == sbom for all non-lossy fields.
  • Lossy fields (e.g., SPDX documentDescribes flattening) documented with explicit schema commentary; never silently dropped.
  • Relationships: DEPENDS_ON, DESCRIBES, CONTAINS preserved in both directions.
  • Tradeoff: strict round-trip tests increase test suite size; necessary for SBOM-as-artifact correctness.
  • Operational: round-trip test in scs-bench for each schema version; run on every CI push.
  • Violation consequence: SBOM produced by scanner cannot be re-ingested for subsequent diff; downstream tooling breaks.

6.10 Crash-Only / Idempotent Design — Scans Are Safe to Retry

  • No scan writes mutable state outside its output directory; advisory cache writes are atomic (write-temp + rename).
  • Feed sync is idempotent: re-fetching same ETag is a no-op; partial download leaves no corrupt state.
  • Interrupted scan leaves no partial result file; output file written only on success (temp + rename pattern).
  • Tradeoff: two-phase write adds one syscall; trivially worth it.
  • Operational: CI retry on transient network failure is safe; no manual cleanup required.
  • Violation consequence: interrupted scan leaves partial report.json; CI reads stale partial result; false pass.

6.11 Input Distrust — All External Data Is Adversarial Until Validated

  • Manifests, lockfiles, advisory feeds, attestations, SBOMs from stdin/file are parsed with strict schema validation before use.
  • JSON/TOML/YAML parsers configured with depth limits; recursive structures rejected at depth > 128.
  • Advisory feed: validate schema version + required fields before inserting into index; malformed record logged + skipped, not panic.
  • Purl strings validated against spec regex before accepting; reject on parse error with structured diagnostic.
  • Tradeoff: validation overhead is O(input size) but sub-millisecond for typical inputs.
  • Violation consequence: attacker-controlled lockfile triggers parser panic; scan crashes; CI falsely passes.

6.12 Separation of Mechanism and Policy — Scanners Detect; Policy Engine Decides

  • scs-match reports all version-range hits regardless of severity or waiver status.
  • scs-policy applies thresholds, VEX waivers, allowlists, license rules, and produces gate decision.
  • Waivers/ignores stored in policy file (scs-policy.toml) under version control; auditable.
  • Tradeoff: two-pass approach means policy cannot short-circuit matching (all matches computed); ensures no findings hidden before policy evaluation.
  • Operational: --policy none runs scanner in report-only mode; useful for onboarding without blocking CI.
  • Violation consequence: baking waiver logic into matcher means a misconfigured waiver silently suppresses real findings; no audit trail.

6.13 Minimal Privilege on External Calls — Network Access Is Scoped and Auditable

  • Advisory feed sync and Rekor queries are the only outbound network calls; all others are local file I/O.
  • --offline flag disables all network; scan uses cached feeds only; --no-provenance disables Rekor/Sigstore.
  • TLS certificate validation always enabled; no danger_accept_invalid_certs flag exposed.
  • Tradeoff: offline mode reduces security posture (stale feeds); explicit operator choice logged in report.
  • Violation consequence: scanner making unexpected outbound calls leaks project metadata; violates airgapped-environment requirements.

6.14 Explicit Over Implicit Dependency Resolution

  • If a lockfile is present, it is authoritative; manifest version ranges are supplemental context only.
  • When both manifest and lockfile present but disagree, emit ScsError::ManifestLockfileMismatch warning; use lockfile for matching.
  • Dev/optional dependencies tracked in separate scope buckets; policy decides whether to gate on them.
  • Tradeoff: lockfile-first means scanner reflects actual deployed versions, not theoretical ranges.
  • Violation consequence: matching against manifest ranges instead of resolved versions produces false positives/negatives for pinned deployments.

6.15 Defense in Depth on Heuristics — Threat Signals Are Scored, Not Binary

  • Typosquatting, confusion, install-script signals each produce a numeric confidence score [0.0, 1.0].
  • No single heuristic gates on its own; scs-policy aggregates score with configurable thresholds.
  • Heuristics are additive evidence, not proof; Finding includes evidence list for analyst review.
  • Tradeoff: more complex policy evaluation; avoids high false-positive rate from over-eager single-signal blocking.
  • Violation consequence: single-signal blocking rejects legitimate packages with unusual names; blocks CI for benign projects.

7. System Invariants

7.1 Match Soundness — No False Negatives Within Advisory Feed Coverage

  • Statement: for every (component, advisory) pair where component.version ∈ advisory.affected_range, a Finding MUST be emitted.
  • Why: missing a critical CVE in a deployed dependency is a safety/compliance defect.
  • Enforced in: scs-match version-range satisfiability check; per-ecosystem comparator; regression test suite.
  • Violation detection: property-based tests (proptest) generate random versions and check match against known-affected ranges; golden-file tests for all major CVEs.
  • Telemetry: scs_match_soundness_check{ecosystem, advisory_id} counter; alert on test failure in CI.
  • Recovery: comparator bug → hotfix + re-scan; no silent retry — always surface the mismatch.
  • Debugging: enable RUST_LOG=scs_match=debug to trace per-comparison inputs/outputs; dump version + range as structured log.

7.2 AffectedRange Half-Open Semantics

  • Statement: version ∈ [introduced, fixed) means introduced ≤ version < fixed; version == fixed is NOT affected.
  • Why: off-by-one on exclusive upper bound = false negative on exactly the patched version.
  • Enforced in: AffectedRange::contains(&self, v: &Version) -> bool; unit tests with boundary cases v == introduced, v == fixed, v == fixed - epsilon.
  • Violation detection: boundary-condition unit tests required for every comparator; fuzz target on range parser input.
  • Telemetry: none (pure computation); violations caught in test.
  • Recovery: fix comparator; bump MSRV-compatible version of comparator crate if upstream bug.
  • Debugging: AffectedRange::explain(v) method returns string "in range [x, y)" / "fixed: version >= y" for diagnostic output.

7.3 Provenance Fail-Closed

  • Statement: any verification error in Sigstore/cosign/Rekor/in-toto yields Provenance { verified: false }; never defaults to verified: true on error path.
  • Why: an error swallowed as "verified" turns a network hiccup or attacker-crafted response into a security bypass.
  • Enforced in: scs-provenance; every ? propagation from verification returns ScsError, not Ok(verified=true).
  • Violation detection: mutation testing: inject error at each verification step, assert verified == false in output.
  • Telemetry: scs_provenance_verified{result=true|false|error} counter; alert if error rate spikes.
  • Recovery: transient Rekor unavailability → --offline-provenance flag; provenance section marked unverified with reason.
  • Debugging: structured log {rekor_url, entry_uuid, error_type, component_purl} at WARN level for every failure.

7.4 Scan Determinism

  • Statement: scan(inputs, feed_snapshot) → report is a pure function; same inputs + same snapshot → bit-identical JSON output.
  • Why: non-deterministic output makes diff-based triage meaningless; engineers lose trust in "no new findings" result.
  • Enforced in: all collections sorted before output; no HashMap in serialization paths (use BTreeMap); no Instant::now() in finding content (only in feed_snapshot.fetched_at).
  • Violation detection: run scan twice on same input; diff output JSON; CI test asserts zero diff.
  • Telemetry: scs_determinism_check{result=pass|fail} in CI benchmark; alert on fail.
  • Recovery: locate non-deterministic collection → replace with BTreeMap/sorted Vec; re-run test.
  • Debugging: binary-search over phases: emit intermediate JSON per phase, diff phase-by-phase to isolate non-determinism source.

7.5 Path Completeness

  • Statement: every Finding.path is a non-empty Vec<Purl> from the affected component to a project root; the last element is always a direct dependency of the scanned project.
  • Why: path-less findings force manual graph traversal; triage SLA increases; fix ownership ambiguous.
  • Enforced in: scs-resolve BFS stores predecessor map; scs-match extracts path at Finding construction; assertion finding.path.last() == root in tests.
  • Violation detection: unit test: construct graph with transitive dep, assert finding path length ≥ 2 and terminal element is root.
  • Telemetry: scs_finding_path_length_p99 histogram; alert if p99 > 64 (truncation happening).
  • Recovery: path truncated at 64 hops (configurable); log {component_purl, path_len=64, truncated=true} at WARN.
  • Debugging: scs resolve --dump-graph --dot > graph.dot → visualize with graphviz; trace BFS predecessor map.

7.6 SBOM Round-Trip Fidelity

  • Statement: parse(emit(sbom)) == sbom for all components, relationships, and non-lossy metadata fields.
  • Why: SBOMs are artifacts handed to compliance/legal/downstream tools; silent field loss invalidates them.
  • Enforced in: scs-sbom; round-trip property test per schema version (SPDX 2.3, CycloneDX 1.5/1.6).
  • Violation detection: proptest-driven round-trip: generate random Sbom, emit, parse, assert equality; golden-file tests for known SBOM samples.
  • Telemetry: none (pure in-process); violations caught in CI test.
  • Recovery: fix emitter or parser; schema field added → add test case; lossy field → document in code commentary.
  • Debugging: diff emit(sbom) vs emit(parse(emit(sbom))) as JSON/text; field mismatch pinpoints loss site.

7.7 Feed Freshness Recording

  • Statement: every ScanReport includes feed_snapshot: Vec<FeedRef> covering all advisory sources used; no scan produces a report with empty feed_snapshot (even in offline mode).
  • Why: without snapshot metadata, a report cannot be reproduced or audited; compliance requires "scan dated X used feed version Y".
  • Enforced in: scs-vulndb returns FeedRef with every query; scs-report asserts feed_snapshot.len() > 0 before writing output.
  • Violation detection: integration test: run scan, assert report.feed_snapshot non-empty; assert fetched_at parses as RFC3339.
  • Telemetry: scs_feed_age_seconds{source} gauge; alert policy: WARN if > 24 h, ERROR if > 72 h.
  • Recovery: expired cache → re-fetch; network unavailable → proceed with cached, log age warning; --require-fresh-feed flag rejects stale cache and exits non-zero.
  • Debugging: scs feed status subcommand prints feed refs, ages, and cache hit/miss per source.

7.8 No Silent Skipping of Withdrawn Advisories

  • Statement: a withdrawn advisory (Advisory.withdrawn.is_some()) is NEVER silently excluded from matching; it is matched and reported with vex_status = Withdrawn + withdrawn timestamp.
  • Why: silent exclusion means a previously reported CVE disappears without explanation; analysts lose audit trail.
  • Enforced in: scs-match match loop includes withdrawn advisories; Finding.vex_status set to Withdrawn; scs-policy may suppress display but never before match.
  • Violation detection: test: insert withdrawn advisory, run match, assert finding emitted with vex_status=Withdrawn.
  • Telemetry: scs_findings_total{vex_status=withdrawn} counter; unexpected drop → investigation.
  • Recovery: if advisory incorrectly flagged withdrawn → update feed; finding reappears on next scan.
  • Debugging: --include-withdrawn flag (default on) explicit; log advisory id + withdrawn timestamp at DEBUG.

7.9 Lockfile Primacy

  • Statement: when a lockfile is present, resolved versions come from the lockfile; manifest version ranges are never used as the sole source for matching.
  • Why: lockfile reflects actual deployed versions; manifest ranges are theoretical; matching against ranges produces false positives/negatives.
  • Enforced in: scs-manifest returns source: LockfileDerived vs source: ManifestDerived on each Component; scs-match emits WARN if matching against ManifestDerived versions.
  • Violation detection: integration test: project with both package.json and package-lock.json; assert all components have source = LockfileDerived.
  • Telemetry: scs_manifest_derived_components_total counter; high count → missing lockfile warning.
  • Recovery: instruct user to commit lockfile; --require-lockfile flag exits non-zero if lockfile absent.
  • Debugging: scs manifest --dump-components --format=json shows source field per component.

7.10 No Cross-Ecosystem Version Comparison

  • Statement: version comparisons are ALWAYS dispatched by Ecosystem enum; no comparison crosses ecosystem boundaries (e.g., no SemVer comparator applied to PEP 440 versions).
  • Why: SemVer pre-release ordering differs from PEP 440; cross-application silently produces wrong range results.
  • Enforced in: VersionComparator trait; match dispatch in scs-match uses advisory.ecosystem to select comparator; compile-time enum exhaustiveness.
  • Violation detection: unit tests: known cross-ecosystem confusions (e.g., 1.0.0-alpha ordering in SemVer vs PEP440) with expected outcome per ecosystem.
  • Telemetry: none (pure type-checked dispatch); violations caught at compile time + test.
  • Recovery: add new ecosystem arm to comparator dispatch; existing arms unchanged.
  • Debugging: scs match --explain --purl <purl> --advisory <id> prints which comparator was selected and the comparison result.

7.11 Input Size Enforcement

  • Statement: all file inputs (manifests, lockfiles, advisory feeds, SBOMs) are rejected with ScsError::InputTooLarge if they exceed configured limits (default 64 MiB per file).
  • Why: unbounded input enables OOM DoS of the scanner in CI; adversarial lockfile can exhaust runner memory.
  • Enforced in: scs-manifest and scs-sbom readers wrap file handles with take(max_bytes); scs-vulndb feed reader uses streaming parse with byte counter.
  • Violation detection: test: feed a 65 MiB synthetic lockfile; assert ScsError::InputTooLarge returned, not panic.
  • Telemetry: scs_input_size_bytes{type=manifest|lockfile|feed|sbom} histogram; scs_input_rejected_total counter.
  • Recovery: operator increases limit via scs.toml config [limits] max_input_bytes; audit log records override.
  • Debugging: error includes {file_path, actual_bytes, limit_bytes} in structured error payload.

7.12 Dependency Graph Acyclicity After Dedup

  • Statement: after scs-resolve deduplication and version unification, the DependencyGraph MUST be a DAG (no cycles in the resolved graph); cycles in the raw constraint graph are detected and reported before resolution.
  • Why: cycle in resolved graph breaks topological sort used for BFS path finding; Tarjan's algorithm hangs on true cycles.
  • Enforced in: scs-resolve runs Tarjan SCC post-dedup; SCC size > 1 → ScsError::CycleDetected { members: Vec<Purl> }; resolution aborts.
  • Violation detection: test: construct synthetic circular dependency; assert CycleDetected error; assert no Finding emitted for cycle members.
  • Telemetry: scs_cycle_detected_total{ecosystem} counter; alert (cycles are unusual; indicate malformed lockfile or resolver bug).
  • Recovery: report cycle to user with member purls; user must fix lockfile or exclude cyclic workspace member.
  • Debugging: scs resolve --dump-graph --dot + graphviz to visualize SCC; cycle members highlighted.

7.13 Policy Gate Atomicity

  • Statement: the policy gate decision (pass / fail) is computed from the complete set of Findings; it is never emitted until all phases (manifest, resolve, sbom, match, provenance, threat) are complete.
  • Why: partial gate decision on incomplete Findings could produce false pass if a later phase would have produced fail.
  • Enforced in: scs-cli pipeline: all phases run to completion (or controlled error); scs-policy is the final stage; early-exit only on unrecoverable errors, never on partial completion.
  • Violation detection: integration test: inject advisory matching only a transitive dep discovered late in resolve; assert gate decision reflects it.
  • Telemetry: scs_gate_decision{result=pass|fail|error} counter; scs_phase_completion{phase} gauge.
  • Recovery: phase error → gate emits error decision (non-zero exit 2); never silently emits pass on incomplete scan.
  • Debugging: --verbose flag prints phase completion timestamps; partial scans show which phase errored.

7.14 Purl Canonicalization Stability

  • Statement: Purl::from_component(c) produces the same string for the same logical package across all scan runs and all ecosystems; no two distinct logical packages map to the same purl.
  • Why: non-canonical purl breaks cross-scan deduplication, advisory correlation, and SBOM component identity.
  • Enforced in: scs-core Purl constructor validates against purl-spec regex; lowercase-normalizes scheme + type; encodes qualifiers in sorted order.
  • Violation detection: unit tests for each ecosystem purl generator; round-trip test parse(purl.to_string()) == purl.
  • Telemetry: scs_purl_parse_error_total{ecosystem} counter; spike indicates parser regression.
  • Recovery: purl format bug → fix generator; existing SBOMs may need re-emission; log migration warning.
  • Debugging: scs manifest --dump-purls prints canonical purl per component; diff against expected for regression.

8. Domain Model — Software Supply Chain Security

8.1 Core Entity Hierarchy

Project (workspace root)
  └─ Manifest (package.json, Cargo.toml, pom.xml, go.mod, requirements.txt, Gemfile)
       └─ Lockfile (package-lock.json, Cargo.lock, go.sum, poetry.lock, Gemfile.lock)
            └─ Component { purl, version, hashes, licenses, direct, scope }
                 └─ DependencyGraph { roots, edges: DAG<Purl> }
                      ├─ Finding { component, advisory, path, severity, vex_status }
                      │    └─ Advisory { id, aliases, affected: Vec<AffectedRange>, cvss }
                      ├─ Sbom (SPDX | CycloneDX)
                      └─ Provenance { attestation, slsa_level, rekor_inclusion, verified }

Entity invariants:

  • Every Component has exactly one canonical Purl; purl namespace + name are lowercase-normalized.
  • DependencyGraph is a DAG (cycles detected and broken during resolve; reported as warnings).
  • Every Finding has a non-empty path: Vec<Purl> from affected component to a root; min length = 1 (direct dep).
  • Advisory.withdrawn set → findings still emitted with vex_status: Disputed; never silently dropped.
  • AffectedRange uses half-open semantics [introduced, fixed); last_affected only used when fixed is absent.

8.2 Purl Canonical Form

EBNF:
  purl       = "pkg:" type "/" [namespace "/"] name ["@" version] ["?" qualifiers] ["#" subpath]
  type       = "npm" | "cargo" | "pypi" | "golang" | "maven" | "gem"
  namespace  = *(unreserved / pct-encoded / "/")
  name       = *(unreserved / pct-encoded)
  version    = *(unreserved / pct-encoded)

Normalization rules (enforced in scs-core::Purl::canonicalize):

Field Rule
type lowercase
namespace lowercase; empty string for npm unscoped, cargo, pypi
name npm: preserve case; cargo/pypi/gem: lowercase; maven: groupId/artifactId split
version strip leading v/V; pypi: normalize via PEP 440
qualifiers sorted by key, url-encoded values
subpath drop trailing /

Worked examples:

  • pkg:npm/%40types/node@20.1.0 — scoped npm package; namespace = @types
  • pkg:cargo/serde@1.0.190 — no namespace for Cargo
  • pkg:pypi/Django@4.2.7 — normalized to pkg:pypi/django@4.2.7
  • pkg:maven/org.springframework.boot/spring-boot-starter@3.1.0
  • pkg:golang/github.com/gin-gonic/gin@v1.9.1
  • pkg:gem/rails@7.1.0

Failure: malformed purl (missing pkg: scheme, empty name) → ScsError::InvalidPurl; reject at parse boundary; never propagate.

8.3 Version Model and Range Semantics

Three distinct version algebras used in matching:

Ecosystem Algebra Crate Example Range
npm, Cargo, Go (modules) SemVer (semver 1.0) semver >=1.2.3, <2.0.0
PyPI PEP 440 pep440 >=1.0.0,!=1.0.1,<2.0
Maven ComparableVersion custom [1.0,2.0)
Gem RubyGems (semver-like) rubygems-version ~> 1.2

Half-open range [introduced, fixed) mapping:

introduced=1.0.0, fixed=1.0.5:
  affected = {v | v >= 1.0.0 AND v < 1.0.5}
  edge case: v = 1.0.5 -> NOT affected (strictly less)
  edge case: introduced absent -> range starts at -∞ (any version)
  edge case: fixed absent, last_affected=1.0.4 -> affected = {v | v >= introduced AND v <= 1.0.4}

Multiple-range advisory (disjunction):

affected = range_A ∪ range_B
version v is affected iff ANY range in affected[] satisfies

OSV affected[].ranges schema fields → AffectedRange:

OSV field AffectedRange field Notes
introduced introduced "0" = beginning of time
fixed fixed exclusive upper bound
last_affected last_affected inclusive; only when fixed absent
limit (ignored) redundant with fixed in practice

PEP 440 exclusion clauses (!=1.0.1): materialized as gap in range; requires interval-difference logic.

Maven bracket notation: [1.0,2.0) → introduced=1.0, fixed=2.0; (,2.0] → no introduced, last_affected=2.0.

8.4 DependencyGraph State Machine

States a component traverses during scs-resolve:

          ┌─────────┐
  parse   │ PENDING │  component seen in manifest; version not yet resolved
  ───────>│         │
          └────┬────┘
               │ constraint solve
               ▼
          ┌──────────┐
          │ RESOLVED │  concrete version selected; purl finalized
          └────┬─────┘
               │ fetch metadata (hashes, licenses)
               ▼
          ┌──────────────┐
          │  ENRICHED    │  hashes + licenses populated
          └────┬─────────┘
               │ all transitive deps resolved
               ▼
          ┌──────────────┐
          │  GRAPH_BUILT │  DAG complete; topological order stable
          └──────────────┘

Cycle detection:

  • DFS with WHITE/GRAY/BLACK coloring during graph construction.
  • GRAY back-edge → cycle; break by treating target as already-RESOLVED; emit Finding::Diagnostic(CycleDetected).
  • Rust crates with [patch] sections can create virtual cycles; treat patch targets as roots.

Scope resolution rules:

Scope Included in prod scan Included in dev scan
Prod yes yes
Dev no yes
Optional no (unless --include-optional) yes

Deduplication: components with same purl but different resolved versions (duplicate subtree) → keep all, tag as duplicate: true; emit warning; report each finding independently (do not deduplicate findings).

8.5 Advisory Lifecycle and State

State machine for Advisory:
  ACTIVE     — published, affects active versions
  WITHDRAWN  — advisory retracted; findings still emitted, vex_status=Disputed
  DISPUTED   — CVE rejected/disputed; still emitted
  FIXED      — all affected ranges have `fixed` version; still matched for un-upgraded components

Feed ingestion state per advisory ID:

Event State transition
First fetch from OSV/NVD/GHSA NEW → INDEXED
Re-fetch, no change INDEXED (no-op)
Re-fetch, fields changed INDEXED → UPDATED; old snapshot retained
withdrawn timestamp set ACTIVE → WITHDRAWN
Alias mapping received ID linked to canonical ID

Advisory deduplication (aliasing):

  • OSV aliases[] field links CVE ↔ GHSA ↔ OSV IDs.
  • Canonical ID selection: CVE-YYYY-NNNNN preferred; GHSA-* if no CVE; OSV ID as fallback.
  • Match against canonical ID only; alias lookup returns same Advisory object.
  • Pitfall: NVD and GHSA may have different affected ranges for same CVE; merge strategy = union of ranges (conservative).

8.6 Vulnerability Matching Sequence

scs-match matching pipeline:

  DependencyGraph (GRAPH_BUILT)
       |
  [1] for each Component c in graph:
       |   ecosystem = c.purl.type
       |   purl_key  = (ecosystem, name, namespace)
       |   advisories = scs-vulndb::lookup_by_purl(purl_key)
       |
  [2] for each Advisory a in advisories:
       |   for each AffectedRange r in a.affected:
       |     if r.ecosystem == ecosystem:
       |       if version_satisfies(c.version, r):
       |         emit Finding { component=c, advisory=a, path=shortest_path(c) }
       |
  [3] apply VEX status from scs-policy::vex_lookup(c.purl, a.id)
       |   AFFECTED / NOT_AFFECTED / FIXED / UNDER_INVESTIGATION
       |
  [4] de-alias findings: merge findings with same canonical advisory ID
       |
  [5] rank by CVSS score (descending), then by path depth (ascending = closer to root first)

Sequence diagram — single component match:

scs-match          scs-vulndb          scs-policy
   |                   |                   |
   |-- lookup(purl) -->|                   |
   |<- [Advisory...] --|                   |
   | version_satisfies(v, range)?          |
   |-- vex_lookup(purl, cve_id) ---------->|
   |<- VexStatus ----------------------------
   | build Finding { vex_status }
   | emit via mpsc channel -> scs-report

Edge cases in matching:

  • Version 0.0.0 (unversioned): matches any range starting from introduced="0"; always flag.
  • Pre-release versions (e.g., 1.0.0-beta.1): SemVer pre-release < stable; >=1.0.0-alpha,<1.0.0 matches beta; verify semver crate pre-release semantics.
  • Yanked Cargo crates: resolved version is yanked → emit Finding::Diagnostic(YankedVersion) in addition to vuln findings.
  • CPE matching fallback: if purl lookup returns nothing, attempt CPE-based NVD lookup; lower confidence; tag matched_by: Cpe.

Malformed advisory examples that must not crash:

  • fixed before introduced (invalid range) → skip range, emit parse warning, continue.
  • Unknown ecosystem in affected → skip range for that ecosystem, continue matching other ranges.
  • Null CVSS score → severity = Unknown; still report finding.

8.7 Finding Model and Severity Ranking

// signatures only — not implementation
struct Finding {
    component: Component,
    advisory:  Advisory,
    path:      Vec<Purl>,      // dep chain: affected → ... → root
    severity:  Severity,
    fixed:     Option<Version>,
    vex_status: VexStatus,
    matched_by: MatchSource,   // Purl | Cpe | Heuristic
}

enum Severity { Critical, High, Medium, Low, Unknown }
enum VexStatus { Affected, NotAffected, Fixed, UnderInvestigation, None }
enum MatchSource { Purl, Cpe, Heuristic }

Severity mapping:

CVSS v3 Score Severity
9.0–10.0 Critical
7.0–8.9 High
4.0–6.9 Medium
0.1–3.9 Low
none/unavailable Unknown

EPSS integration: Finding carries optional epss_score: Option<f32> (0.0–1.0); policy engine can gate on EPSS > threshold.

Path computation:

  • BFS from affected component outward in reverse-dependency graph.
  • Shortest path selected for Finding.path; all paths available via --all-paths flag.
  • Path = [affected_purl, intermediate..., root_purl]; root = direct dep of project.
  • Direct dep: path length = 1; path = [affected_purl].

8.8 SBOM Domain Model

SPDX 2.3 / CycloneDX 1.5 both supported for emit and parse.

SPDX package record fields → Component mapping:

SPDX Field Component field Notes
SPDXID internal id format: SPDXRef-<purl-hash>
PackageName purl.name
PackageVersion version
ExternalRef: PACKAGE-MANAGER purl purl canonical purl
PackageChecksum hashes SHA-256 preferred
PackageLicenseConcluded licenses[0] SPDX expression
Relationship: DEPENDS_ON graph edge

CycloneDX component fields → Component:

CDX Field Component field
bom-ref internal id
purl purl
version version
hashes[].content hashes
licenses[].expression licenses
dependencies[].ref graph edges

Round-trip invariant: parse(emit(sbom)) == sbom for all components and relationships. Test fixture: reference SBOMs for known projects (lodash, numpy, serde) committed in scs-bench/fixtures/sbom/.

SBOM diff (scs-report):

  • Added components: in new SBOM, not in baseline.
  • Removed components: in baseline, not in new SBOM.
  • Version-changed: same purl name, different version.
  • New findings: findings for added/changed components not present in baseline findings.

8.9 Provenance and Sigstore Domain Model

Sigstore verification pipeline:

  Artifact (OCI image digest or source tarball hash)
       |
  [1] fetch Rekor transparency log entry by artifact hash
       |   Rekor entry: { logID, logIndex, body{ kind, spec }, integratedTime }
       |
  [2] verify Rekor inclusion proof (Merkle path from leaf to STH)
       |   failure → Provenance { verified: false, reason: RekorInclusionFailed }
       |
  [3] decode cosign bundle: { Signature, Certificate }
       |   Certificate: subject (email/OIDC), issuer (Fulcio CA), SAN
       |
  [4] verify certificate chain to Fulcio root CA
       |   failure → Provenance { verified: false, reason: CertChainFailed }
       |
  [5] verify signature over artifact digest using cert public key
       |   failure → Provenance { verified: false, reason: SignatureInvalid }
       |
  [6] verify certificate validity window ⊇ Rekor integratedTime
       |   (short-lived cert; keyless signing)
       |
  [7] check build OIDC claims: issuer = github-actions, repo = expected_repo
       |
  Provenance { attestation, slsa_level, rekor_inclusion: Some(entry), verified: true }

in-toto attestation fields → Provenance:

Field Meaning
_type https://in-toto.io/Statement/v0.1
subject[].digest.sha256 matches Component.hashes
predicateType SLSA provenance URI
predicate.builder.id CI identity (GitHub Actions, etc.)
predicate.buildConfig source of build inputs
predicate.materials[].uri upstream source refs

SLSA level determination:

Level Requirements
L0 no provenance
L1 provenance exists, not verified
L2 provenance hosted service, verified build
L3 hardened build, verified + Rekor inclusion

Failure modes:

  • Rekor network unreachable → fail-closed: verified=false, reason=RekorUnreachable; never assume verified.
  • Clock skew > 5 min between cert validity and integratedTime → fail; log skew delta.
  • OIDC issuer mismatch → fail; policy engine receives slsa_level=L0.
  • Missing cosign bundle → verified=false, reason=NoBundleFound.

Hash pinning (non-Sigstore provenance):

  • Lockfile hash (sha256/sha512) compared to Component.hashes.
  • Mismatch → Finding::Diagnostic(HashMismatch) with severity Critical.
  • Go go.sum dual hash (h1:sha256-of-zip + sha256-of-dir) → both verified.

8.10 Malicious Package Threat Model

Heuristics evaluated by scs-threat, each producing a ThreatSignal:

struct ThreatSignal {
    kind: ThreatKind,
    score: f32,       // 0.0–1.0 confidence
    evidence: String,
}

enum ThreatKind {
    Typosquatting,
    DependencyConfusion,
    SuspiciousInstallScript,
    ObfuscatedCode,
    MaintainerAnomaly,
    PackageAgeTooNew,
    UnusuallyHighDownloadSpike,
}

Typosquatting detection algorithm:

  1. Compute edit distance (Damerau-Levenshtein, threshold ≤ 2) between candidate package name and all packages in popular-package corpus.
  2. Also check: homoglyph substitution (0/O, 1/l, rn/m), hyphen/underscore swap, prefix/suffix addition (-util, -js, -py).
  3. Corpus: top-10,000 packages per ecosystem from download stats, updated weekly.
  4. Score = f(edit_distance, download_ratio_delta, age_delta).
  5. Pitfall: legitimate forks with similar names; output as signal, not automatic block; policy engine decides.

Dependency confusion detection:

  • Package exists in both public registry and private registry → confusion risk.
  • Detection: compare package name against internal namespace list (from --private-namespace config).
  • Version in lockfile > latest public version → strong signal (classic Birsan attack vector).
  • published_by different from expected org → signal.

Install script analysis:

  • Parse package.json::scripts.{preinstall,install,postinstall} for npm.
  • Parse setup.py cmdclass for PyPI.
  • Flag: shell exec (exec, eval, subprocess, child_process), network calls, file writes outside package dir, base64-decoded payloads.
  • High entropy string detection: Shannon entropy > 4.5 bits/char in string literals → flag.
  • Regex patterns for common obfuscation: String.fromCharCode, atob, eval(Buffer.from(...,'base64')).

Maintainer/age signals:

  • Package age < 7 days + direct dependency + no prior versions → high-risk signal.
  • Maintainer account age < 30 days + recent ownership transfer → signal.
  • Single-maintainer package suddenly transferring ownership → signal.
  • Source: registry metadata API (npm registry JSON, PyPI JSON API); fetched during enrichment phase.

Combination scoring (ensemble):

final_threat_score = weighted_sum(signals) / normalization_factor
threshold > 0.7 → ThreatKind::HighRisk finding
threshold 0.4–0.7 → ThreatKind::MediumRisk finding

8.11 Policy Engine Domain Model

Policy file schema (TOML, evaluated by scs-policy):

[policy]
fail_on_severity = "High"          # Critical | High | Medium | Low
fail_on_threat   = true
license_allow    = ["MIT", "Apache-2.0", "BSD-2-Clause", "BSD-3-Clause"]
license_deny     = ["GPL-3.0-only", "AGPL-3.0-only"]
max_epss         = 0.7             # gate if EPSS > 70%

[[ignore]]
advisory_id  = "GHSA-xxxx-yyyy-zzzz"
package      = "pkg:npm/lodash@4.17.21"
expires      = "2024-12-31"
reason       = "mitigated by WAF rule"

[[vex]]
advisory_id  = "CVE-2023-12345"
package      = "pkg:cargo/openssl@0.10.55"
status       = "not_affected"
justification = "vulnerable_code_not_in_execute_path"

Policy evaluation order (short-circuit):

1. VEX status check → if NotAffected, skip further evaluation (not a failure)
2. Waiver/ignore check → if active waiver, mark ignored, continue (no failure)
3. Severity threshold → if Finding.severity >= fail_on_severity → FAIL
4. EPSS threshold → if epss_score > max_epss → FAIL
5. License deny list → if any Component.license ∈ license_deny → FAIL
6. Threat score → if threat_score > threshold AND fail_on_threat → FAIL
7. All checks pass → PASS

Gate decision state machine:

GateState: PASS | FAIL | WARN
- Start: PASS
- Any FAIL trigger → FAIL (sticky; does not revert)
- Ignored finding → WARN (does not cause FAIL unless all findings ignored and --strict-ignores)
- Final: GateState determines exit code (0=PASS, 1=FAIL, 2=ERROR)

License compliance:

  • SPDX expression parsing: MIT AND Apache-2.0, GPL-2.0-or-later OR MIT, LicenseRef-custom.
  • Copyleft detection: any expression containing GPL/AGPL/EUPL/OSL → flag.
  • Unknown license → configurable: warn or fail.
  • Dual-license: if ANY branch of OR expression is in allow list → allowed (permissive interpretation).

8.12 Report Domain Model

SARIF 2.1.0 output structure → Finding mapping:

SARIF field Finding field
runs[0].tool.driver.name "scs"
results[].ruleId advisory.id (canonical)
results[].level severity → error/warning/note
results[].message.text human description
results[].locations[].logicalLocations[].fullyQualifiedName component.purl
results[].relatedLocations dependency path components
results[].properties.epss finding.epss_score
results[].suppressions VEX/waiver entries

Exit code contract:

Exit Code Meaning
0 PASS — no policy violations
1 FAIL — policy threshold exceeded
2 ERROR — scan could not complete (parse failure, network error)
3 WARN — findings present but all below threshold or waived

CI annotation format (GitHub Actions):

::error file={manifest},line={line},col={col},title={advisory_id}::{component} {version} affected by {advisory_id} (CVSS {score})

SBOM diff report: emitted as JSON array of { op: "added"|"removed"|"changed", component: Purl, details: ... }.

8.13 Feed Snapshot and Determinism Model

Feed snapshot: every scan records:

struct FeedSnapshot {
    osv_fetched_at:  DateTime<Utc>,
    nvd_fetched_at:  DateTime<Utc>,
    ghsa_fetched_at: DateTime<Utc>,
    osv_schema_version: String,
    total_advisories: u64,
    snapshot_hash: [u8; 32],  // SHA-256 of sorted advisory IDs + modified timestamps
}

Determinism invariant:

  • scan(inputs, snapshot_A) == scan(inputs, snapshot_A) always (same snapshot, same inputs).
  • scan(inputs, snapshot_A) != scan(inputs, snapshot_B) allowed if new advisories added.
  • Output includes snapshot_hash for auditability; CI can pin snapshot for reproducible checks.

Feed sync modes:

Mode Behavior
--offline use local cache only; fail if cache absent
--update sync feeds before scan; update local SQLite
--snapshot <hash> use exact snapshot by hash; fail if not cached
default use latest cached; warn if older than 24h

Local advisory cache: SQLite database at $XDG_CACHE_HOME/scs/advisories.db; schema in scs-vulndb.

8.14 Error Domain

enum ScsError {
    InvalidPurl(String),
    ManifestParse { path: PathBuf, cause: String },
    LockfileNotFound { manifest: PathBuf },
    AdvisoryFeedFetch { url: String, status: u16 },
    AdvisoryFeedParse { source: AdvisorySource, cause: String },
    VersionParse { purl: Purl, raw: String },
    RangeParse { advisory: String, raw: String },
    ProvenanceVerification { purl: Purl, reason: ProvenanceError },
    SbomParse { format: SbomFormat, cause: String },
    PolicyParse { path: PathBuf, cause: String },
    CycleDetected { component: Purl },
    HashMismatch { purl: Purl, expected: String, actual: String },
    RekorUnreachable { url: String },
    Io(std::io::Error),
}

Error propagation:

  • ManifestParse → recoverable; skip that manifest, continue with others; aggregate errors in final report.
  • AdvisoryFeedFetch → if --offline flag absent, treat as ScsError → exit code 2; if --offline, silently use cache.
  • ProvenanceVerification → fail-closed always; never demote to warning; Finding emitted regardless.
  • CycleDetected → non-fatal; graph construction continues; finding emitted as Diagnostic.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-001 Implementation language Rust (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78) Go (Trivy/Grype precedent), Python, Java Safe parsing of untrusted manifests; zero-cost graph algorithms; native crypto; reproducible binaries; no GC pauses in hot matching loops Go wins when matching the Trivy/Grype ecosystem and contributor familiarity outweigh parse-safety; Python wins for a quick prototype reusing pip/packaging internals directly
ADR-002 Build system Cargo workspace (11 crates) Bazel, Buck2, plain Makefile Native Rust toolchain; workspace-level test/bench/lint; no extra toolchain dep; cargo xtask for auxiliary scripts Bazel wins for polyglot monorepos needing hermetic, remote-cached builds across many languages
ADR-003 Async runtime Tokio 1.x (multi-thread scheduler) async-std, smol, blocking threads Feed fetch + registry API calls are I/O-bound; Tokio ecosystem (reqwest, tokio-postgres) widest; multi-thread for parallel matching Blocking threads win for a purely local scan with no network feeds, where a simple thread pool beats async machinery
ADR-004 HTTP client reqwest (Tokio + rustls) hyper directly, ureq, curl-sys Async; rustls avoids OpenSSL linking; connection pooling; redirect handling; used in scs-vulndb + scs-provenance hyper-directly wins when you need fine-grained control of connection lifecycle or HTTP/2 features reqwest abstracts away
ADR-005 TLS stack rustls 0.21+ OpenSSL/openssl-sys, native-tls Pure Rust; no system OpenSSL dep; reproducible; no linking complexity in CI; acceptable perf OpenSSL/native-tls wins when FIPS-validated crypto or the OS system trust store / hardware engines are mandated
ADR-006 CLI framework clap 4.x (derive API) argh, structopt (deprecated), pico-args Rich help generation; subcommand support; shell completions; env-var override integration pico-args wins when binary size and compile time dominate and the CLI surface is tiny
ADR-007 Serialization (JSON/YAML/TOML) serde + serde_json + serde_yaml + toml quick-xml only, nom-based parsers Universal derive macro; zero-copy where possible; TOML for policy files; YAML for SPDX; JSON for SARIF/OSV/CycloneDX A hand-written nom parser wins for one high-throughput binary format where serde reflection overhead is measurable
ADR-008 Advisory cache / local DB SQLite via rusqlite Postgres, DuckDB, flat JSON files, RocksDB Embedded; zero server dep; fast purl-range index queries; acceptable for millions of advisories; FTS5 for text search Postgres wins when one advisory index is shared by many concurrent scanner instances needing a central server; RocksDB wins for write-heavy LSM ingest
ADR-009 SemVer parsing + constraint semver 1.0 crate custom parser, node-semver port Canonical Rust ecosystem SemVer; range satisfiability tested; SemVer npm quirks handled via normalization layer A custom/node-semver port wins when you must reproduce a package manager's non-standard range quirks the crate refuses to model
ADR-010 PEP 440 version handling custom pep440 crate (in-tree) packaging-py via FFI, pyo3 No Python runtime dep; PEP 440 spec complex (epoch, post, dev); own impl avoids FFI overhead + safety boundary pyo3/packaging wins when a Python runtime is already embedded and exact CPython-parity behaviour is required
ADR-011 Crypto (hashing) sha2 + sha3 crates (RustCrypto) ring, openssl-sys Pure Rust; no C FFI; audited by RustCrypto; covers SHA-256/SHA-512/SHA-3 needed for SBOM hashes ring wins when you need its assembly-optimized throughput and constant-time guarantees on a hashing-heavy hot path
ADR-012 Sigstore/cosign verification sigstore-rs (SigStore Rust SDK) reimplementing Rekor/Fulcio client from scratch, calling cosign binary via subprocess Native Rust; avoids subprocess + PATH dep; correctness maintained by upstream SDK; Rekor Merkle proof in-library Subprocess cosign wins when you must track cosign's bleeding-edge features faster than the SDK ships them
ADR-013 in-toto attestation parsing in-toto crate / custom DSSE parser calling in-toto-run binary No binary dep; DSSE envelope parse + verify is tractable in Rust; reduces attack surface The reference in-toto-run binary wins when it is the compliance-required oracle for attestation verification
ADR-014 Graph data structure petgraph 0.6 (DiGraph) custom adjacency list, daggy Well-tested; DFS/BFS/Tarjan SCC built-in; topological sort; supports metadata on edges for scope/depth A custom CSR adjacency layout wins when the dependency graph is huge and cache-optimal traversal matters more than convenience
ADR-015 SPDX parsing + emit spdx 0.10 crate + custom serializer spdx-rs, parsing from scratch License expression parsing; SPDX expression evaluation; supplement with custom SPDX 2.3 TV/JSON emit From-scratch parsing wins when targeting an SPDX version the crate does not yet support
ADR-016 Error handling thiserror (library) + anyhow (CLI/bin) failure (deprecated), custom error enums thiserror for scs-core/libraries (typed errors, no heap); anyhow for scs-cli (ergonomic context) Hand-written enums win when a no_std target or a stable ABI boundary forbids the proc-macro/heap dependency
ADR-017 Logging tracing + tracing-subscriber log + env_logger, slog Structured spans; async-aware; machine-readable JSON output via tracing-json; trace-level for debug; spans for timing log + env_logger wins for a tiny tool that needs only flat logging with minimal dependencies
ADR-018 Metrics (internal) metrics crate + metrics-exporter-prometheus opentelemetry-rust (heavier), statsd Lightweight; Prometheus /metrics endpoint for long-running daemon mode; feed-sync duration, match throughput opentelemetry-rust wins when full distributed-trace export to an OTLP collector is required, not just Prometheus pull
ADR-019 Testing framework Rust built-in #[test] + proptest (property) + cargo-nextest pytest-like frameworks, criterion alone nextest for fast parallel test; proptest for version algebra fuzz; built-in sufficient for unit/integration A pytest-like framework wins when the test corpus is authored by non-Rust QA engineers in a polyglot pipeline
ADR-020 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) on scs-manifest, scs-vulndb, scs-sbom parsers honggfuzz, AFL++ cargo-fuzz integrates natively; libFuzzer coverage-guided; OSS-Fuzz-compatible corpus format AFL++ wins when you need its advanced mutators / persistent mode or are fuzzing a non-libFuzzer target
ADR-021 Benchmarking criterion 0.5 + custom scs-bench crate hyperfine (external), bench built-in criterion statistical rigor; scs-bench holds fixtures + differential (baseline vs current) harness hyperfine wins for end-to-end CLI-binary timing (process startup included) rather than in-process function benchmarks
ADR-022 CI/CD GitHub Actions CircleCI, Jenkins, GitLab CI GitHub native; cargo cache actions; matrix for MSRV 1.78 + stable + nightly; artifact upload for bench results GitLab CI wins when the org's source already lives in self-hosted GitLab; Jenkins wins for complex on-prem pipelines
ADR-023 Container packaging Docker multi-stage (scratch final image) Nix flakes, static musl binary only Scratch image minimizes attack surface; musl target for static binary; no libc in final layer A bare static musl binary (no container) wins for CLI distribution; Nix flakes win when fully hermetic, reproducible images are required
ADR-024 Reproducible builds CARGO_UNSTABLE_SPARSE_REGISTRY, pinned Cargo.lock, SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH nix-based full hermetic Cargo.lock committed; SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH for timestamps; cargo-auditable embeds SBOM in binary Full Nix hermetic builds win when bit-for-bit reproducibility across heterogeneous machines is a hard compliance requirement
ADR-025 Dependency audit cargo-deny (license + advisory) + cargo-audit manual review, Dependabot only cargo-deny enforces deny.toml at CI; cargo-audit checks Cargo.lock against RustSec DB; eats own dog food Dependabot-only wins on GitHub-hosted projects that prefer automated update PRs over hard CI gating
ADR-026 Documentation rustdoc + mdBook for user guide Sphinx, Docusaurus rustdoc native; mdBook lightweight Rust-native; no JS build chain for docs Docusaurus wins when the docs site needs rich interactive web features and a JS ecosystem
ADR-027 Policy file format TOML JSON, YAML, HCL Human-editable; comments supported; serde/toml crate; consistent with Cargo.toml toolchain familiarity YAML wins when policies are generated/consumed by existing YAML tooling (k8s, CI); HCL wins in Terraform-centric orgs
ADR-028 Parallel scan execution Rayon (data parallel) for graph matching + Tokio (I/O) for feed/network pure Tokio threadpool, single-thread Rayon for CPU-bound matching across large graphs; Tokio for I/O-bound feed sync; two-tier concurrency model Pure Tokio wins for an entirely I/O-bound workload with little CPU matching; single-thread wins for tiny scans where parallelism overhead dominates
ADR-029 SBOM format selection Both SPDX 2.3 + CycloneDX 1.5 (configurable default) SPDX only, CycloneDX only Industry split; compliance requires both; round-trip tests ensure fidelity; emit format CLI flag A single format wins when the consuming toolchain is locked to exactly one of SPDX or CycloneDX and dual emit is wasted effort
ADR-030 Vulnerability severity normalization CVSS v3.1 primary; CVSS v2 fallback; NVD v3.1 preferred over GHSA EPSS-only ranking, severity enum without score CVSS v3.1 most widely available; EPSS supplemental only (predictive, not prescriptive); NVD authoritative CVSS source EPSS-only ranking wins when prioritizing by real-world exploitation probability matters more than nominal severity; an enum-only score wins for a coarse pass/fail gate

11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types live in scs-core. Normative API contract; implementations must satisfy every invariant stated here.

11.1 Purl and PackageId

// Canonical Package URL — RFC-compliant, normalized on construction.
struct Purl(String);
// Invariants:
//   - lowercase scheme "pkg"; percent-encoded namespace/name; version URL-encoded
//   - construction via Purl::parse() validates spec; invalid -> ScsError::InvalidPurl
//   - equality: byte-exact on normalized form; no case-folding at comparison time
//   - serialization: Display = inner String; Serde transparent
//   - ownership: owned String; Clone = O(n), cheap for typical lengths (~40 bytes)
//   - concurrency: Send + Sync; immutable after construction
//   - persistence: stored as VARCHAR; deserialization re-validates

enum Ecosystem { Npm, Cargo, PyPI, Go, Maven, RubyGems }

struct PackageId {
    ecosystem: Ecosystem,   // discriminant for version parsing + range semantics
    namespace: Option<String>, // e.g. npm scope "@scope", maven groupId
    name: String,           // bare name, lowercased for Npm/PyPI
}
// Invariants:
//   - (ecosystem, namespace, name) triple is canonical key in graph + index
//   - Eq/Hash by all three fields; namespace=None != namespace=Some("")
//   - From<Purl>: parses purl type + subpath -> PackageId; lossy (drops version/quals)
//   - no mutation after construction; Clone = O(n)

11.2 Version and VersionReq

enum Version {
    Semver(semver::Version),          // npm, Cargo, Go (semver-coerced)
    Pep440(pep440::Version),          // PyPI
    Maven(maven_version::Version),    // Maven (composite numeric segments)
    Opaque(String),                   // Ruby Gems, fallback; compared lexicographically
}
// Invariants:
//   - constructed from (Ecosystem, &str); wrong variant -> ScsError::VersionParse
//   - Ord defined within same variant; cross-variant comparison -> panic (programmer error)
//   - serialization: String representation; round-trips through Display -> parse
//   - concurrency: Send + Sync; immutable

enum VersionReq {
    Semver(semver::VersionReq),       // >=1.2.3, ^1.2.3, ~1.2.3, *
    Pep440Req(pep440::Requirement),   // ~=1.4, >=2.0,<3.0
    MavenRange(maven_range::Range),   // [1.0,2.0), (,1.0]
    Opaque(String),
}
// Invariants:
//   - matches(version: &Version) -> bool; panics on ecosystem mismatch
//   - AffectedRange.satisfies(v) -> bool is the canonical half-open check
//   - no interior mutation; Send + Sync

11.3 Component

enum HashAlgorithm { Sha256, Sha512, Blake3 }

struct Hash {
    algorithm: HashAlgorithm,
    digest: [u8; 64],   // zero-padded; SHA-256 uses first 32 bytes
}

enum Scope { Prod, Dev, Optional, Unknown }

enum Source {
    Lockfile { path: PathBuf },
    Manifest { path: PathBuf },
    Sbom     { path: PathBuf, format: SbomFormat },
}

struct Component {
    purl:     Purl,
    version:  Version,
    hashes:   Vec<Hash>,    // may be empty for manifest-only resolution
    licenses: Vec<License>, // SPDX expressions; empty if unknown
    source:   Source,
    direct:   bool,         // true iff declared in root manifest (not transitive)
    scope:    Scope,
}
// Invariants:
//   - purl.version segment == version.to_string() (enforced at construction)
//   - hashes: at most one entry per HashAlgorithm; deduped on insert
//   - direct=true implies depth==0 in the DependencyGraph from any root
//   - serialization: Serde with purl as primary key; hashes as hex strings
//   - ownership: fully owned; Clone = O(n) proportional to hashes+licenses count
//   - concurrency: Send + Sync; immutable after graph construction

11.4 DependencyGraph

struct Edge {
    from:        Purl,
    to:          Purl,
    req:         VersionReq,   // constraint declared by `from`
    scope:       Scope,
    optional:    bool,
}

struct DependencyGraph {
    roots:     Vec<Purl>,                           // direct roots (workspace members)
    nodes:     IndexMap<Purl, Component>,           // canonical node set; O(1) lookup
    edges:     Vec<Edge>,                           // directed from → to (dependsOn)
    adjacency: HashMap<Purl, Vec<usize>>,           // edge indices by source purl
}
// Invariants:
//   - DAG (no directed cycles); cycle detection run at finalize() -> ScsError::CyclicDependency
//   - every edge.to purl exists in nodes; referential integrity enforced at insert
//   - roots ⊆ nodes; roots are the only nodes with in-degree 0 (after pruning)
//   - deterministic iteration order: IndexMap preserves insertion order; insertion is
//     topological post-sort so output is stable across runs given same inputs
//   - path(purl) -> Vec<Vec<Purl>>: returns all simple paths from purl to any root
//     (used by Finding.path); max path length capped at 64 to prevent pathological O(n!)
//   - serialization: nodes as array, edges as array; adjacency rebuilt on load
//   - concurrency: read-only after finalize(); &DependencyGraph is Send + Sync
//   - mutation only via GraphBuilder (separate type); finalize() -> DependencyGraph is consuming

11.5 Advisory, AffectedRange, Cvss, Epss

enum CvssVersion { V2, V31, V40 }

struct Cvss {
    version:  CvssVersion,
    vector:   String,       // canonical vector string, e.g. "CVSS:3.1/AV:N/..."
    score:    f32,          // 0.0–10.0; base score only
    severity: Severity,     // derived from score per spec thresholds
}

struct Epss {
    probability: f32,       // 0.0–1.0
    percentile:  f32,       // 0.0–1.0
    model_date:  NaiveDate,
}

enum Severity { Critical, High, Medium, Low, None, Unknown }

struct AffectedRange {
    ecosystem:     Ecosystem,
    introduced:    Option<Version>,  // None = beginning of time
    fixed:         Option<Version>,  // None = no fix; half-open [introduced, fixed)
    last_affected: Option<Version>,  // advisory-specified inclusive last; normalized to fixed = last_affected.next()
}
// Invariants:
//   - satisfies(v: &Version) -> bool: introduced <= v && (fixed.is_none() || v < fixed)
//   - last_affected and fixed are mutually exclusive in source; normalization picks fixed
//   - introduced=None matches all versions below fixed
//   - ecosystem must match queried version's variant; mismatch -> always false (never panic)

struct Advisory {
    id:            String,              // canonical form: "CVE-...", "GHSA-...", "OSVA-..."
    aliases:       Vec<String>,         // cross-source aliases (e.g. CVE + GHSA for same vuln)
    summary:       String,
    affected:      Vec<AffectedRange>,  // union of ranges; component affected if any range matches
    severity:      Option<Cvss>,
    epss:          Option<Epss>,
    fixed_versions: Vec<(Ecosystem, Version)>,
    withdrawn:     Option<DateTime<Utc>>,  // None = active; Some = retracted advisory
    source:        AdvisorySource,      // OSV | NVD | GHSA
    snapshot_id:   SnapshotId,          // feed snapshot this advisory was ingested from
}
// Invariants:
//   - withdrawn.is_some() -> advisory surfaced as Finding.vex_status = Withdrawn; NOT suppressed silently
//   - aliases deduplicated; canonical id is the most-specific available (CVE > GHSA > OSV)
//   - affected: empty vec means no versions listed (treated as affects-all unless withdrawn)
//   - serialization: full round-trip via serde; snapshot_id required (feed auditability invariant)

11.6 Finding

enum VexStatus {
    NotAffected,        // vendor-confirmed not affected (via VEX)
    AffectedConfirmed,  // vendor-confirmed affected
    Fixed,              // fixed version available
    UnderInvestigation,
    Withdrawn,          // advisory was retracted
    None,               // no VEX data
}

struct Finding {
    component:  Component,
    advisory:   Advisory,
    path:       Vec<Purl>,          // [affected_purl, ..., root_purl]; len >= 1
    severity:   Severity,           // may be overridden by policy after CVSS adjustment
    fixed:      Option<Version>,    // earliest fixing version for component.purl.ecosystem
    vex_status: VexStatus,
    suppressed: bool,               // true if waiver/ignore rule matched
}
// Invariants:
//   - path[0] == component.purl; path[last] ∈ DependencyGraph.roots (path completeness invariant)
//   - path length capped at 64; longer chains truncated + annotated with truncated=true flag
//   - suppressed findings still emitted in full report (never silently dropped); gating ignores suppressed
//   - severity = advisory.severity.map(|c| c.severity).unwrap_or(Severity::Unknown) before policy override
//   - Finding is value type; Clone used for multi-path duplicates; Eq by (component.purl, advisory.id)
//   - serialization: advisory.id + component.purl as composite key

11.7 Sbom

enum SbomFormat { Spdx23, Spdx30, CycloneDx15, CycloneDx16 }

enum RelationshipKind {
    DependsOn,
    Contains,
    DescribedBy,
    DynamicLink,
    StaticLink,
    Other(String),
}

struct SbomRelationship {
    from:  String,          // element SPDXID or CycloneDX bom-ref
    to:    String,
    kind:  RelationshipKind,
}

struct Sbom {
    format:        SbomFormat,
    spec_version:  String,          // e.g. "SPDX-2.3", "1.5"
    serial_number: Option<String>,  // CycloneDX urn:uuid:...
    name:          String,          // document name
    namespace:     Option<String>,  // SPDX namespace URI
    created:       DateTime<Utc>,
    components:    Vec<Component>,
    relationships: Vec<SbomRelationship>,
    metadata:      HashMap<String, String>,  // tool name, version, authors
}
// Invariants:
//   - round-trip invariant: emit(parse(bytes)) produces structurally equivalent Sbom
//     (component set, relationship set, hashes preserved; timestamp may normalize to UTC)
//   - component.purl is the canonical cross-format identifier; bom-ref/SPDXID are internal
//   - duplicate components (same purl) merged with union of hashes; conflict -> ScsError::SbomConflict
//   - relationships: referential integrity (from + to must reference component in components set)
//   - serialization: lossless for SPDX JSON + CycloneDX JSON; XML supported read-only for CycloneDX

11.8 Provenance

enum SlsaLevel { L0, L1, L2, L3 }

struct RekorInclusion {
    log_id:         String,
    log_index:      u64,
    integrated_time: DateTime<Utc>,
    inclusion_proof: Vec<u8>,   // raw proof bytes; verified separately
}

struct Provenance {
    purl:             Purl,
    attestation_type: String,              // e.g. "https://slsa.dev/provenance/v1"
    attestation_raw:  Option<Vec<u8>>,     // base64-decoded envelope bytes
    slsa_level:       Option<SlsaLevel>,
    rekor_inclusion:  Option<RekorInclusion>,
    verified:         bool,                // ONLY true after all checks pass (fail-closed)
    verification_err: Option<String>,      // populated when verified=false; required for auditability
}
// Invariants:
//   - FAIL-CLOSED: verified=false is the default; must be explicitly set to true by verifier
//   - verified=true requires: sig check passed AND cert chain valid AND Rekor inclusion valid (if present)
//   - verification_err must be Some when verified=false; empty string is not acceptable
//   - attestation_raw stored even on failure (for manual forensics)
//   - serialization: attestation_raw as base64; inclusion_proof as hex

11.9 License

struct License {
    expression: String,     // SPDX expression, e.g. "MIT OR Apache-2.0", "(GPL-2.0-only)"
    source:     LicenseSource,
}

enum LicenseSource {
    Manifest,           // declared in package manifest
    Sbom,               // from ingested SBOM
    InferredFromFile,   // heuristic from LICENSE file scan
    Unknown,
}

// Normative API on License:
//   parse_spdx_expression(s: &str) -> Result<SpdxNode, ScsError>
//   is_copyleft(expr: &SpdxNode) -> bool   // GPL-*, LGPL-*, AGPL-*, MPL-2.0
//   is_permissive(expr: &SpdxNode) -> bool // MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-*
//   satisfies_policy(expr: &SpdxNode, policy: &LicensePolicy) -> PolicyResult
// Invariants:
//   - SPDX expression parser is strict; unknown license IDs -> ScsError::UnknownSpdxId (not silently accepted)
//   - LicenseOrLaterSuffix (+) resolved to OR with unknown future versions; treated as copyleft for safety
//   - LicenseSource::Unknown does not block scanning; surfaced as informational Finding

11.10 Policy, PolicyResult, Waiver

struct Waiver {
    id:          String,            // waiver identifier for audit log
    purl:        Option<Purl>,      // None = applies to all packages
    advisory_id: Option<String>,    // None = applies to all advisories
    reason:      String,            // mandatory human-readable justification
    expires:     Option<DateTime<Utc>>,
    created_by:  String,
}

struct Policy {
    min_severity_gate:  Severity,           // findings at or above this block CI
    license_deny:       Vec<SpdxNode>,      // expressions that trigger deny
    license_allow:      Vec<SpdxNode>,      // allowlist; deny takes precedence
    waivers:            Vec<Waiver>,
    vex_consume:        bool,               // whether VEX statuses suppress findings
    fail_on_unknown_license: bool,
    max_age_days_unfixed: Option<u32>,      // escalate if no fix for N days
}

enum PolicyResult {
    Pass,
    Fail { reasons: Vec<String> },
    Warn { reasons: Vec<String> },
}
// Invariants:
//   - expired waivers are ignored; waiver application logged in report regardless
//   - deny overrides allow for same expression
//   - vex_consume=true: VexStatus::NotAffected -> suppressed=true but Finding still emitted
//   - gating decision: PolicyResult::Fail -> process exit code 1 (CI gate blocks)
//   - deterministic: same Policy + Findings -> same PolicyResult (no randomness in rule eval)

11.11 ScsError

enum ScsError {
    // Parse errors
    InvalidPurl     { input: String, reason: String },
    VersionParse    { input: String, ecosystem: Ecosystem, reason: String },
    ManifestParse   { path: PathBuf, ecosystem: Ecosystem, source: Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync> },
    SbomParse       { path: PathBuf, format: SbomFormat, source: Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync> },
    UnknownSpdxId   { id: String },

    // Graph errors
    CyclicDependency { cycle: Vec<Purl> },
    UnresolvedDep    { from: Purl, requirement: String },

    // Advisory / matching errors
    AdvisoryFeedSync { source_url: String, source: Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync> },
    StaleSnapshot    { snapshot_id: SnapshotId, age: Duration },

    // Provenance errors
    SignatureVerification  { purl: Purl, reason: String },
    RekorVerification      { purl: Purl, reason: String },
    AttestationParse       { purl: Purl, source: Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync> },

    // SBOM errors
    SbomConflict    { purl: Purl, field: String },
    RoundTripFail   { format: SbomFormat, diff: String },

    // Policy / report errors
    PolicyLoad      { path: PathBuf, source: Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync> },
    ReportWrite     { path: PathBuf, source: Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync> },

    // I/O
    Io              { path: PathBuf, source: std::io::Error },

    // Internal invariant violations (programmer errors)
    InvariantViolation { message: String },
}
// Invariants:
//   - all variants are Send + Sync (required for use in async contexts and ? in threads)
//   - InvariantViolation is unrecoverable; triggers panic in debug, structured log + exit(2) in release
//   - SignatureVerification and RekorVerification are NOT mapped to warnings; fail-closed invariant
//   - ScsError implements Display with structured context; never exposes raw OS error strings alone
//   - thiserror derive used; no anyhow in library crates (only in scs-cli for context chaining)

11.12 SnapshotId and Feed Metadata

struct SnapshotId {
    source:    AdvisorySource,   // OSV | NVD | GHSA
    timestamp: DateTime<Utc>,   // fetch time (UTC, second precision)
    etag:      Option<String>,  // HTTP ETag for incremental sync
    sha256:    [u8; 32],        // hash of raw feed bytes (auditability)
}

enum AdvisorySource { Osv, Nvd, Ghsa }

struct FeedSnapshot {
    id:         SnapshotId,
    advisories: Vec<Advisory>,  // normalized, deduped
    indexed_at: DateTime<Utc>,
}
// Invariants:
//   - SnapshotId included in every Finding and every report (feed freshness invariant)
//   - sha256 computed over raw bytes before parsing; validated on load from disk cache
//   - two snapshots with same source + timestamp + sha256 are identical (safe to skip re-index)
//   - FeedSnapshot not persisted as single blob; advisories stored in per-ecosystem inverted index
//     with SnapshotId recorded separately in metadata table

11.13 Key Traits

// Manifest parsing trait — implemented by each ecosystem parser in scs-manifest
trait ManifestParser {
    fn ecosystem(&self) -> Ecosystem;
    fn can_parse(&self, path: &Path) -> bool;              // filename/extension check
    fn parse(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<Component>, ScsError>;
    fn parse_lockfile(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<Component>, ScsError>;
}
// Invariant: parse() never installs/executes; reads file bytes only

// Version satisfiability — implemented per AffectedRange
trait VersionSatisfies {
    fn satisfies(&self, version: &Version) -> bool;
}
// Invariant: half-open semantics [introduced, fixed); panics on ecosystem mismatch

// Policy evaluation trait
trait PolicyEvaluator {
    fn evaluate(&self, findings: &[Finding], policy: &Policy) -> PolicyResult;
}
// Invariant: deterministic; same inputs -> same output; no side effects

// Report emitter trait
trait ReportEmitter {
    fn emit(&self, findings: &[Finding], policy_result: &PolicyResult, writer: &mut dyn Write)
        -> Result<(), ScsError>;
}

// Provenance verifier trait
trait ProvenanceVerifier {
    fn verify(&self, purl: &Purl, hints: &Provenance) -> Result<Provenance, ScsError>;
}
// Invariant: fail-closed — error return or verified=false, never silently verified=true

11.14 Concurrency and State Model

// Scan lifecycle state machine (scs-cli / runner.rs):
//   Init -> Parsed -> Resolved -> VulnMatched -> ProvenanceChecked -> PolicyApplied -> Reported

// Concurrency model:
//   - Parsing (scs-manifest): embarrassingly parallel per-file; rayon par_iter over discovered files
//   - Graph construction (scs-resolve): single-threaded builder; finalize() produces immutable graph
//   - Advisory matching (scs-match): read-only graph + read-only index; rayon par_iter over nodes
//   - Provenance (scs-provenance): async (tokio); network I/O to Rekor/Fulcio; bounded concurrency (semaphore)
//   - Threat heuristics (scs-threat): CPU-bound; rayon par_iter; no shared mutable state
//   - Policy (scs-policy): single-threaded; O(findings) deterministic pass
//   - Reporting (scs-report): single-threaded writer; output is ordered by (severity desc, purl asc)

// Shared state rules:
//   - DependencyGraph: immutable after finalize(); Arc<DependencyGraph> shared across threads
//   - FeedSnapshot / advisory index: Arc<RwLock<Index>>; writers hold lock only during sync window
//   - No global mutable state; all configuration passed via function arguments
//   - Cancellation: tokio CancellationToken propagated to all async tasks; drop cleans up

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton — crates, core types (Purl/Component), parse ONE manifest (package.json) into a flat component list and print it (no resolution, no vulns)

0.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Establish Cargo workspace with all 12 crates declared No circular deps; each crate compiles independently
Implement scs-core canonical types: Purl, PackageId, Version, Component, ScsError Types must be Clone + Debug + Serialize + Deserialize + Eq + Hash
Implement scs-manifest: parse package.json → Vec<Component> (flat, no transitive resolution) Untrusted input; all parse errors propagate as ScsError::Parse
CLI (scs-cli): scs scan --manifest package.json prints table of direct deps Exit 0 on success, non-zero on parse error
Gate: zero unsafe, pass cargo clippy --deny warnings MSRV 1.78 enforced via rust-version in workspace Cargo.toml

Architectural scope: scs-core, scs-manifest, scs-cli wired; all other crates present but empty (lib.rs with #![forbid(unsafe_code)] only); no network calls, no advisory DB, no resolution.

0.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Workspace (Cargo.toml):

[workspace]
resolver = "2"
members = ["crates/scs-core", "crates/scs-manifest", ..., "crates/scs-cli", "crates/scs-bench"]
[workspace.package]
rust-version = "1.78"
edition = "2021"

scs-core (src/lib.rs + sub-modules, all re-exported, #![forbid(unsafe_code)]):

  • types/purl.rs: Purl(String) newtype; ctor validates pkg:<ecosystem>/<name>@<ver> via once_cell::sync::Lazy<Regex>; .ecosystem()/.name()/.version() accessors; FromStr + round-tripping Display; canonical grammar per Package URL Spec (P13/S1).
  • types/version.rs: Version(String)/VersionReq(String) opaque; ecosystem-specific parsing deferred to scs-manifest/scs-resolve.
  • types/component.rs: Component { purl: Purl, version: Version, hashes: Vec<Hash>, licenses: Vec<License>, source: SourceLocation, direct: bool, scope: Scope }.
  • types/hash.rs: Hash { algorithm: HashAlg, value: String }, HashAlg = enum { Sha256, Sha512, Md5, Blake2b }; types/scope.rs: enum Scope { Prod, Dev, Optional }; types/license.rs: License(String) SPDX expression, length-validated only at phase 0.
  • types/error.rs: ScsError (thiserror) — Parse { path: PathBuf, detail: String }, Io(std::io::Error), Unsupported(String).

scs-manifest::npm — parse_package_json(path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<Component>, ScsError>: read via std::fs::read_to_string; parse via serde_json::from_str::<Value>; extract dependencies + devDependencies (skip peerDependencies). Per (name, version_req): sanitize name (reject ../, absolute paths, NUL); build Purl = pkg:npm/<name>@<version_req> (verbatim at phase 0, satisfiability deferred to scs-resolve); direct: true; scope = Prod/Dev by source map; hashes: vec![], licenses: vec![] (no lock/metadata yet). Return sorted by purl string (determinism). Missing dependencies key → empty vec, not error; malformed JSON → ScsError::Parse.

scs-manifest::lib.rs — pub fn parse(path) -> Result<Vec<Component>, ScsError> dispatches on filename stem: package.json → npm::parse_package_json; else ScsError::Unsupported.

scs-cli::main.rs — clap subcommand scan, flags --manifest <PATH>, --format <table|json> (default table); calls scs_manifest::parse, prints via comfy-table/tabled, or serde_json::to_writer_pretty for JSON; tracing_subscriber::fmt at INFO (--verbose → DEBUG).

scs-bench — placeholder src/lib.rs, one #[test] fn compiles() {}.

Crate deps (Phase 0): scs-cli → scs-manifest → scs-core; scs-bench → scs-core; all others → scs-core only (stubs).

Observability (minimal): tracing::debug!(purl = %purl, scope = ?scope, "component parsed") per component; tracing::warn!(path = ?path, "no dependencies key in package.json").

0.3 Deliverable

  • cargo build --workspace succeeds, zero warnings.
  • scs scan --manifest fixtures/npm/package.json prints table: PURL | VERSION | SCOPE | DIRECT.
  • scs scan --manifest fixtures/npm/package.json --format json emits valid JSON array of Component.
  • All 12 crate stubs present, cargo test --workspace passes (zero-test stubs pass).
  • cargo clippy --workspace --deny warnings clean.

0.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/npm/package.json Standard react-app manifest, 10 direct prod + 5 dev 15 components, correct scopes
fixtures/npm/empty_deps.json {} — no dependencies key Empty vec, no error
