Replay: re-running any fixture produces bit-identical stdout (sorted purl order enforced).

0.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Purl validation too strict Legitimate scoped npm names @org/pkg rejected Regex missing @ prefix Unit test with scoped fixture ScsError::Parse on valid input Regex: pkg:npm/%40<scope>/<name> or allow @ before percent-encode
version_req stored as Version Range ^1.0.0 confused with exact version Phase 0 conflation — Version is opaque Type mismatch surfaces in phase 2 resolve Wrong purl version field Separate VersionReq from Version in Component from day 0
Missing serde feature flags serde_json compiles but Serialize not derived Forgot features = ["derive"] Compile error Build failure Add serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
File encoding not UTF-8 read_to_string panics on latin-1 manifests npm allows non-UTF-8 historically Test with binary fixture Io error wrapping InvalidData Use read + lossy UTF-8 decode, log warning
comfy-table not in workspace deps Link error at CLI build Forgot [workspace.dependencies] entry Cargo error message Build failure Declare in workspace; use workspace = true in scs-cli
Determinism broken by HashMap iteration Output order changes across runs HashMap non-deterministic iteration Run fixture twice, diff output Non-idempotent stdout Sort Vec<Component> by purl.0 before return

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • read_to_string: single syscall for <1 MB manifests, acceptable; >10 MB manifest → log warning.
  • serde_json::from_str allocates per string field (~500 String allocs for 500-dep manifest); use serde_json::from_reader + BufReader to avoid double-buffer.
  • Purl validation regex: compile once via once_cell::sync::Lazy, never recompile per call.
  • Vec<Component> sort: O(n log n) on purl.0 string, negligible for n<10k.
  • tracing::debug! per component: gate behind enabled!(Level::DEBUG) to avoid format overhead in production.

0.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
package.json is symlink Follow symlink; log resolved path at DEBUG
version_req is "" (empty string) Store as-is; warn; phase-2 resolver handles
name key missing in top-level manifest Not required for dep parsing; proceed
Dep name is null in JSON serde_json gives Value::Null → ScsError::Parse
dependencies value is array (malformed) serde_json type mismatch → ScsError::Parse
File >100 MB Read returns Io error if OOM; add size check before read
Concurrent CLI invocations on same file Each process reads independently; no shared state; safe

0.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
scs-core type change breaks scs-manifest ABI Compile error All downstream crates fail to build Workspace compile CI gate Revert type change or migrate all callers
Regex panic on malformed purl input unwrap on Regex::new at startup Process crash once_cell init fallible; use expect with message Fix regex string
ScsError::Parse swallowed by caller Silent empty output User sees no components, no error Propagate ? everywhere; CLI maps Err to non-zero exit Audit ? chain
serde_json stack overflow on deeply-nested JSON Thread stack overflow, SIGSEGV Process crash Depth limit via serde_json::Deserializer::from_str + custom limit Reject input >100 nesting depth

Operator visibility: CLI always prints error to stderr, exit code 1 on any Err; RUST_LOG=debug exposes per-component parse trace.

0.10 Operational Notes

  • Logging: tracing_subscriber::fmt to stderr; RUST_LOG env var controls level.
  • Metrics: none at phase 0; stub scs_core::telemetry module for later.
  • No network: phase 0 fully offline; no HTTP client dependency introduced.
  • Binary size: expect ~5–8 MB stripped; acceptable for CLI tool.
  • Reproducibility: Cargo.lock committed; cargo vendor compatible.
  • Compatibility: scs-core types marked #[non_exhaustive] where extension expected (HashAlg, Scope).
  • Migration: phase 0→1 adds fields to Component (hashes populated); #[serde(default)] on new fields keeps backward compat with serialized phase-0 output.

13. Phase 1 — Manifest + lockfile parsers — multi-ecosystem (npm, Cargo, PyPI, Go, Maven), purl generation, lockfile-vs-manifest reconciliation, content hashes

1.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Parse manifests + lockfiles for 5 ecosystems: npm, Cargo, PyPI, Go, Maven Each parser isolated in its own scs-manifest submodule
Generate canonical Purl per ecosystem spec npm: pkg:npm/%40scope/name@ver; Cargo: pkg:cargo/name@ver; PyPI: pkg:pypi/name@ver (normalized); Go: pkg:golang/module@ver; Maven: pkg:maven/group/artifact@ver
Populate Component.hashes from lockfiles (SHA-256/SHA-512) Hash values copied verbatim from lock; validated as hex
Lockfile-vs-manifest reconciliation: detect deps declared in manifest but absent from lockfile Emit ScsError::Reconciliation (new variant) with list of missing names
scs-manifest::parse_dir(dir: &Path) discovers + parses all manifests in a project root Processes multiple ecosystems in one call
MSRV 1.78; no unsafe; all parsers fuzz-safe (bounded allocation)
Scope: scs-manifest complete; scs-core gains Reconciliation error variant + SourceLocation enum; scs-cli extended to accept directory scan.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

scs-core additions: ScsError::Reconciliation { missing: Vec<String>, ecosystem: String }; SourceLocation: enum { ManifestOnly, LockfileResolved, DirectRegistry }; Component.source: SourceLocation set by each parser. scs-manifest module layout:

scs-manifest/src/
  lib.rs          — parse_dir, parse_file dispatch
  npm.rs          — package.json + package-lock.json v1/v2/v3
  cargo.rs        — Cargo.toml + Cargo.lock
  pypi.rs         — requirements.txt, pyproject.toml, Pipfile.lock, poetry.lock
  golang.rs       — go.mod + go.sum
  maven.rs        — pom.xml + (no standard lockfile; use dep:tree output if present)
  purl.rs         — ecosystem-specific purl construction helpers
  hash.rs         — hex validation, HashAlg detection from lockfile field names
  reconcile.rs    — manifest-vs-lockfile reconciliation logic

npm (npm.rs): parse_package_json (phase 0, extended) also reads peerDependencies (scope=Optional). parse_package_lock(path, components) supports v1 (dependencies flat map) and v2/v3 (packages map, "node_modules/<name>" keys); per entry extract version (exact) + integrity (SRI sha512-<base64>) → decode to Hash{Sha512, hex} (RustCrypto sha2/sha3 per ADR-011). Reconciliation: package.json dep names minus package-lock.json names → Reconciliation error if non-empty. Scoped names @scope/pkg → purl pkg:npm/%40scope%2Fpkg@ver (percent-encode @//). Cargo (cargo.rs): Parse Cargo.toml via toml: [dependencies]/[dev-dependencies]/[build-dependencies]. Parse Cargo.lock: [[package]] entries name/version/checksum (SHA-256 hex) → Hash{Sha256, checksum}. Workspace Cargo.toml ([workspace]) → scan member paths, recurse. Purl pkg:cargo/<name>@<version>; reconciliation: manifest dep names vs lockfile package names. PyPI (pypi.rs): requirements.txt line-by-line, skip comments/options (-r, --index-url), regex name[extras]==version (extras stripped from purl name); purl pkg:pypi/<normalized_name>@<version> (lowercase, _→-, PEP 503). pyproject.toml (PEP 621 [project.dependencies]) via toml, PEP 440 specifiers → VersionReq. Pipfile.lock JSON: default+develop sections, version (==version), hash (sha256:<hex>). poetry.lock TOML: [[package]] with name/version/files[].hash. Go (golang.rs): go.mod line-by-line (no crate needed); require block → module version; replace directives → ScsError::Unsupported warning (not error). go.sum lines <module> <version>/go.mod <hash> + <module> <version> <hash>, format h1:<base64-sha256> decoded to Hash{Sha256, hex}. Purl pkg:golang/<module_path>@<version> (path may contain /). Maven (maven.rs): pom.xml via quick-xml+serde: <dependencies> block, fields groupId/artifactId/version/scope (compile→Prod, test→Dev, provided→Optional). No standard lockfile; dependency-tree.txt (Maven Dependency Plugin) parsed for transitive hint only. Purl pkg:maven/<groupId>/<artifactId>@<version>; property version ${project.version} stored verbatim + ScsError::Unsupported("unresolved property") warning. purl.rs helpers: npm_purl(name, version) (percent-encode scoped names); pypi_normalize(name) (lowercase, [._-]+→-); golang_purl(module, version); maven_purl(group, artifact, version). reconcile.rs: reconcile(manifest_names: &HashSet<String>, lockfile_names: &HashSet<String>, ecosystem: &str) -> Result<(), ScsError> — manifest_names - lockfile_names non-empty → Err(Reconciliation{missing, ecosystem}); called after each ecosystem parse when lockfile present. parse_dir(dir) -> Result<Vec<Component>, Vec<ScsError>>: walk dir (one level, non-recursive at root); detect manifests by name pattern; parallel parse per ecosystem via rayon::scope (each parser Send); collect Vec<Component> deduped by purl + collect errors; dedup merge: lockfile version wins, hashes merged.

1.3 Deliverable

  • scs scan --dir <project-root> discovers all manifests, prints unified table ECOSYSTEM | PURL | VERSION | SCOPE | HASHES | SOURCE.
  • scs scan --manifest Cargo.lock prints resolved Cargo deps with SHA-256 hashes.
  • Reconciliation warnings printed to stderr per ecosystem.
  • cargo test -p scs-manifest runs fixture corpus: all 5 ecosystems pass.
  • cargo fuzz target fuzz_parse compiles (no corpus required yet).

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
All ecosystem unit tests pass 100%
PyPI name normalization: My_Package → my-package Tested via fixture
npm scoped purl round-trip: @babel/core → pkg:npm/%40babel%2Fcore@7.x Unit test
Cargo.lock SHA-256 hashes populated in Component.hashes Assert non-empty
Reconciliation error on missing lockfile entry Fixture: missing_lock.json
Parse 1000-dep package-lock.json v2 <100 ms, <20 MB RSS
Parse Cargo.lock with 400 packages <50 ms
Parse go.sum with 600 entries <80 ms
No unsafe cargo geiger
Fuzz target compiles cargo fuzz build fuzz_parse

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
npm scoped package percent-encoding @scope/pkg stored as pkg:npm/@scope/pkg (invalid purl) Missing percent-encode of @ and / purl validation rejects at Purl::from_str ScsError::Parse on valid npm package purl.rs helper must encode: %40 and %2F
package-lock v1 vs v2/v3 format confusion Wrong field paths; empty component list v1 uses dependencies, v2/v3 use packages Compare lockfileVersion field Silent empty output Read lockfileVersion int; branch parser
PyPI extras in dependency spec requests[security]==2.28.0 — name parsed as requests[security] Bracket not stripped Purl contains [ → validation failure ScsError::Parse Strip [...] via regex before name normalization
Poetry.lock TOML array of inline tables toml crate misparses some inline table arrays Toml spec edge case Compare with taplo CLI output Parse failure on valid poetry.lock Use toml_edit which handles inline tables more robustly
go.sum h1: base64 → hex decode length mismatch Hash stored as wrong-length hex Base64 padding error Assert hex.len() == 64 Wrong hash length → assertion or silent truncation Decode base64, verify 32 bytes, hex-encode
Maven property version ${...} in dep Version stored as literal ${project.version} POM properties not evaluated Debug log shows ${} in purl Warn-level log; ScsError::Unsupported Log warning, store verbatim, skip in vulnerability matching
rayon parallelism data race on shared Vec<Component> Compile error or UB Passing &mut Vec into rayon scope Compiler rejects &mut across threads Compile error Use Mutex<Vec<Component>> or rayon::collect() pattern
Git submodule Cargo.lock in subdirectory Duplicate package entries merged incorrectly Workspace recursion descends into submodule Check is_symlink + .gitmodules Duplicate purls Skip submodule directories

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • quick-xml stream parsing for Maven avoids loading full DOM; bounded memory for large pom.xml.
  • Pipfile.lock/package-lock.json JSON via serde_json::from_reader(BufReader::new(file)) — avoid double-buffering.
  • go.sum line-by-line via BufReader::lines(); zero-copy where possible with &str slices.
  • rayon: parse_dir spawns one task per detected ecosystem — 5× speedup for 5-ecosystem monorepos; I/O-bound on spinning disk, CPU-bound on SSD.
  • Dedup hash map in parse_dir: HashMap<Purl, Component> via FxHashMap (rustc-hash), ~20% faster string hashing.
  • Avoid cloning String in hot paths: Arc<str> for Purl.0 when same purl referenced across multiple Components.
  • rayon thread pool defaults to num_cpus; SCS_THREADS env var overrides for CI resource limits.

1.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
package.json + package-lock.json both absent parse_dir returns empty vec, no error
Cargo.toml present, Cargo.lock absent Components from manifest with source: ManifestOnly; no hashes; warn
go.mod replace pointing to local path Record as ScsError::Unsupported; continue; log warn
pom.xml with BOM (UTF-8 BOM) Strip BOM before XML parse; quick-xml may fail on BOM
requirements.txt with Windows CRLF lines() handles CRLF correctly in Rust std
Circular require in requirements.txt (-r other.txt) Track visited files; break cycle; emit warn
poetry.lock with source section (private registry) Parse version/hash; source URL stored in SourceLocation
Maven <dependencyManagement> versions Not expanded at phase 1; version may be absent → ScsError::Unsupported
npm workspaces field Detect; recurse into each workspace dir; dedup by purl

1.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Parser panic on crafted malformed input cargo fuzz finds; CI fuzz step Single ecosystem parse fails Each parser wrapped in catch_unwind (fuzz target only; production uses Result) Fix parser; re-fuzz
go.sum hash corruption undetected Wrong Hash stored Downstream provenance check trusts wrong hash Phase 6 (scs-provenance) re-verifies; mismatch surfaces there Flag component as unverified
rayon task panic in parse_dir Thread panic propagates as Err from scope Whole parse_dir fails Wrap each rayon task result; collect errors; partial success returned Operator sees per-ecosystem error in output
TOML parser OOM on adversarial deeply-nested TOML RSS grows unbounded Process OOM Pre-read size check: reject >50 MB TOML Emit ScsError::Parse("file too large")
npm package-lock.json v3 undocumented field breaks parser serde_json deserialize into Value — unknown fields ignored None (Value-based parsing is schema-agnostic) N/A N/A (resilient by design)

1.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (stub): scs_manifest_parse_duration_ms{ecosystem} histogram via metrics crate facade (no-op backend at phase 1, wired phase 8+).
  • Tracing: tracing::info_span!("parse_ecosystem", ecosystem) wrapping each parser call; duration logged at span exit.
  • Log fields: ecosystem, path, component_count, error_count at INFO per parse call.
  • Fuzz: crates/scs-bench/fuzz/fuzz_targets/fuzz_parse.rs feeds random bytes to each parser; assert no panic (only Err).
  • Compatibility: Component gains hashes/source fields; #[serde(default)] keeps phase-0-serialized JSON deserializable at phase 1.
  • Deprecation: phase-0 parse(path) remains; parse_dir(dir) added alongside.
  • CI gate: cargo test -p scs-manifest --test fixtures must pass; fixture corpus committed to repo.

14. Phase 2 — Dependency resolution — transitive dependency graph, version-constraint solving, dedup, cycle detection, dev/prod/optional scopes

2.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Build DependencyGraph (DAG) from lockfile components + edges Lockfile already encodes resolved versions; graph construction = structural, not SAT solving
For ecosystems WITH lockfiles (npm, Cargo, PyPI/poetry, Go): read resolved edges from lockfile No re-solving; lockfile is ground truth
For ecosystems WITHOUT lockfiles (requirements.txt, Maven): construct dependency tree from manifest constraints + advisory metadata (best-effort; warn when incomplete) Phase 2 does NOT download packages
Implement scs-resolve: fn build_graph(components: Vec<Component>, edges: Vec<(Purl, Purl)>) -> Result<DependencyGraph, ScsError>
Cycle detection: report cycles as ScsError::CyclicDependency with cycle path Directed graphs can have cycles in non-lockfile ecosystems
Dedup: same purl at multiple versions → ScsError::VersionConflict (warn, keep all)
Scope propagation: a Dev-scoped direct dep's transitive deps inherit Dev unless also reachable via Prod path
Path<Purl> from each node to graph root(s) — required for Finding.path
Architectural scope: scs-resolve crate fully implemented; scs-core gains DependencyGraph; CLI adds --graph flag dumping DOT format.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

scs-core additions:

DependencyGraph {
  roots: Vec<Purl>,           // direct deps (direct=true components)
  nodes: HashMap<Purl, Component>,
  edges: Vec<(Purl, Purl)>,   // (parent, child) directed edges
  scopes: HashMap<Purl, Scope>,  // effective scope after propagation
}
impl DependencyGraph {
  fn successors(&self, p: &Purl) -> impl Iterator<Item=&Purl>;
  fn ancestors(&self, p: &Purl) -> impl Iterator<Item=&Purl>;
  fn paths_to_roots(&self, p: &Purl) -> Vec<Vec<Purl>>;  // BFS
  fn depth(&self, p: &Purl) -> usize;
  fn node_count(&self) -> usize;
  fn edge_count(&self) -> usize;
}
ScsError::CyclicDependency { cycle: Vec<Purl> }
ScsError::VersionConflict { name: String, versions: Vec<Version> }

scs-resolve/src/lib.rs: pub fn build_graph(components: Vec<Component>, raw_edges: Vec<(Purl, Purl)>) -> Result<DependencyGraph, ScsError>. Steps: 1) insert all components into nodes: HashMap<Purl, Component>; 2) validate edges — both endpoints must exist, unknown endpoint → ScsError::Parse("edge references unknown purl"); 3) cycle detection via DFS with coloring (white/gray/black), back-edge found → collect cycle path via parent-pointer stack → CyclicDependency (Tarjan SCC / Kahn toposort theory, B4); 4) scope propagation: BFS from roots, Prod scope propagates along Prod-edge paths, Dev-only-reachable node gets Dev, both-reachable → Prod wins; 5) dedup check: group by PackageId{ecosystem, name}, >1 version → VersionConflict (non-fatal, added to error list); 6) paths_to_roots: reverse-edge BFS from each non-root node, collect shortest paths, computed lazily on demand in scs-match. scs-resolve/src/lockfile_edges.rs: per-ecosystem edge extraction — npm v2/v3: packages["node_modules/a"].dependencies map → edges (a, dep); Cargo.lock: [[package]] dependencies = ["name version"] → edges; poetry.lock: [[package]] [package.dependencies] map → edges; go.sum: no direct edge info, go.mod require gives direct deps only, transitive edges NOT available → ScsError::Unsupported("go transitive edges unavailable without go list"); Maven: no standard lockfile edge format → same Unsupported warning. scs-resolve/src/constraint.rs (stub for phase 2; full solving deferred to phase 3+): fn satisfies(version: &Version, req: &VersionReq, ecosystem: &str) -> bool stub returns true (no false-negatives at this phase); real SemVer/PEP440 impl in phase 3; documented as "constraint solving not yet implemented." scs-resolve/src/graph.rs: DFS cycle detection iterative (not recursive) to avoid stack overflow on deep trees (npm graphs can be 200+ levels); BFS for paths_to_roots via VecDeque, path length bounded at 50 (configurable); scope propagation single BFS pass, scope_map: HashMap<Purl, Scope>, Prod dominates Dev. scs-cli additions: scs graph --dir <path> emits DOT to stdout; --format json emits DependencyGraph JSON; --scope prod filter excludes Dev+Optional nodes. Observability: tracing::info!(node_count, edge_count, root_count, cycle_count, conflict_count, "graph built"); tracing::warn!(cycle = ?cycle_path, "cyclic dependency detected") per cycle; tracing::warn!(name, versions = ?versions, "version conflict") per conflict.

2.3 Deliverable

  • scs graph --dir fixtures/npm/react-app/ emits valid DOT; dot -Tpng renders without error.
  • DependencyGraph::paths_to_roots(purl) returns non-empty vec for every non-root node.
  • Cycle in fixture → ScsError::CyclicDependency with exact cycle purl list.
  • Scope propagation: dev-only dep reachable via prod path → promoted to Prod.
  • cargo test -p scs-resolve passes all fixture tests.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p scs-resolve 100% pass
build_graph on react-app (1200 nodes, 1800 edges) <200 ms, <50 MB RSS
Cycle detection: fixture with 3-node cycle Cycle path reported exactly
Scope propagation: dev-dep reachable via prod Scope=Prod in scopes map
paths_to_roots for deepest node Non-empty, length ≤ actual depth
Version conflict: two versions of lodash ScsError::VersionConflict in error list
DependencyGraph JSON round-trips serde round-trip test
DOT output is valid graphviz dot -Tsvg exits 0 on fixture output
Iterative DFS: 200-deep npm graph No stack overflow

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/graphs/npm_react_app/ Full react-app with package-lock v2 1200+ nodes, correct scopes
fixtures/graphs/cargo_tokio/ Tokio workspace Cargo.lock ~400 nodes, no cycles
fixtures/graphs/cycle_3node.json Pre-built edge list with A→B→C→A cycle CyclicDependency { cycle: [A,B,C] }
fixtures/graphs/diamond_dep.json A→B, A→C, B→D, C→D (diamond) D appears once; both paths preserved
fixtures/graphs/version_conflict.json Two versions of lodash VersionConflict in errors
fixtures/graphs/scope_promotion.json dev→X→Y; prod→X X,Y scope=Prod

fixtures/graphs/go_mod_only.json go.mod with no go.sum edges Unsupported warning; direct deps only

2.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Recursive DFS stack overflow on deep npm graph SIGSEGV or thread stack overflow npm hoisting creates 200+ deep trees Test with deep_200 fixture Stack overflow signal Rewrite DFS as explicit Vec-based stack (iterative)
Diamond dep counted twice in paths_to_roots Path list explodes exponentially BFS without visited set Profile: paths_to_roots takes seconds Time spike Add visited: HashSet<Purl> in BFS; return shortest paths only
Edge referencing purl not in nodes ScsError::Parse on valid graph Lockfile edge refers to dep not in component list Print missing purl in error Parse error on valid lock Pre-scan lockfile to ensure all deps emit a component
Scope propagation incorrect for optional deps Optional dep incorrectly promoted to Prod BFS visits optional edge as prod edge Add optional edge type to (Purl, Purl, Scope) tuples Wrong scope in output Encode edge scope in edges: Vec<(Purl, Purl, EdgeScope)>
Cycle detection reports wrong cycle path Cycle path starts mid-cycle Parent-pointer stack not reset on backtrack Add unit test asserting cycle contains back-edge start Wrong path in error Track discovery order; cycle = path from back-edge target to back-edge source
npm node_modules hoisting creates phantom edges Edge (parent, dep) where dep is hoisted to top-level npm v2 package paths flatten hoisting Compare with npm ls output Wrong graph structure Use node_modules/<full-path> key in v2 parser, not just name
HashMap<Purl, Component> collisions on case-different purls PyPI Requests vs requests treated as different Case-sensitive HashMap key Normalize purl before insert Duplicate nodes Normalize purl string at construction (lowercase name for pypi)

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • DependencyGraph node map: FxHashMap<Purl, Component>, faster than std::HashMap for string keys; pre-allocate with with_capacity(component_count).
  • Edge vec Vec<(Purl, Purl)>; Purl is String newtype — consider Arc<str> to share purl strings across nodes/edges without cloning.
  • DFS coloring: HashMap<Purl, Color> pre-allocated; alternatively petgraph::algo::toposort if petgraph adopted (evaluate at phase 2).
  • paths_to_roots: potentially O(paths × depth) — bound max 10 paths/node (SCS_MAX_PATHS); truncate with warning.
  • Scope propagation BFS: single pass O(V+E); no contention (single-threaded).
  • DOT serialization: stream-write to BufWriter; avoid building full string in memory for large graphs (>10k nodes).
  • petgraph evaluation: if adopted, petgraph::stable_graph::StableDiGraph<Component, EdgeScope> for O(1) neighbor access; tradeoff is heap allocation per node.

2.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
Graph with zero edges (all direct, no transitive) Valid; paths_to_roots returns [root] for each root
Self-loop A → A Cycle detection catches immediately; CyclicDependency { cycle: [A] }
Root node with no incoming edges but no direct=true flag Heuristic: nodes with in-degree 0 treated as roots; log warning
Two roots with same name, different purl Both in roots; correct
Optional dep transitively depends on Prod dep Existing Prod node not overwritten; Optional node scoped correctly
Graph with 0 nodes (empty component list) DependencyGraph { roots: [], nodes: {}, edges: [] }; no error
npm workspace with 50 sub-packages All sub-packages' deps merged into one graph; dedup by purl
Edge list references purl with trailing whitespace Purl validation strips+rejects; ScsError::Parse

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
build_graph OOM on massive monorepo (50k nodes) RSS exceeds container limit; OOM kill Complete scan failure Pre-flight component count; reject >20k with ScsError::Unsupported("graph too large") Raise limit via SCS_MAX_GRAPH_NODES env
Incorrect scope propagation → prod vuln reported as dev Finding suppressed by policy (dev vulns may be ignored) False negative (missed prod vuln) Test: assert prod-reachable node has Scope::Prod Fix BFS propagation; add regression fixture
Cycle causes infinite loop in paths_to_roots Scan hangs indefinitely Scan never completes paths_to_roots must check visited set; cycle → return partial path with warning Iterative BFS with visited set is loop-safe
Edge encoding mismatch between npm v2 and v3 parsers Wrong edges → wrong transitive set → missed vulns False negatives Compare DependencyGraph.edge_count against npm ls --all count Fix lockfile edge extractor; add v3 fixture
petgraph dependency introduces MSRV regression cargo build fails on 1.78 All phases fail to build Check petgraph MSRV before adoption; pin version Use only stdlib graph if petgraph MSRV > 1.78

2.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: scs_graph_nodes{ecosystem} gauge, scs_graph_edges{ecosystem} gauge, scs_graph_build_ms histogram, emitted per build_graph call.
  • Tracing: info_span!("build_graph", ecosystem) wrapping build_graph; child spans for DFS, scope propagation.
  • Logging: INFO node/edge/cycle/conflict counts; WARN cycles, conflicts, Unsupported warnings; DEBUG per-edge insertion.
  • DOT export: scs graph --dir . > graph.dot && dot -Tsvg graph.dot > graph.svg — operator debugging tool.
  • Snapshot: DependencyGraph serialized to JSON as scan artifact; stored alongside SBOM for audit trail.
  • Compatibility: DependencyGraph JSON schema versioned ("schema_version": 1); future field additions use #[serde(default)].
  • Rollback: build_graph failure → CLI falls back to flat component list (phase 0/1 output) with warning "graph unavailable; vulnerability paths will be missing."
  • CI implications: --scope prod flag gates CI on prod-scope findings only; dev-scope reported but non-blocking.

15. Phase 3 — SBOM generation + ingestion — SPDX + CycloneDX emit, component inventory, hashes/licenses; scan an existing third-party SBOM

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement scs-sbom: emit SPDX 2.3 (tag-value + JSON) and CycloneDX 1.6 (JSON + XML) from a resolved DependencyGraph.
  • Ingest third-party SBOMs (JSON/XML/tag-value) into normalized Component + relationship sets for downstream matching.
  • Round-trip invariant: emit → parse → emit produces byte-for-byte identical component inventory (modulo timestamp field).
  • Compute + verify per-component hashes (SHA-256, SHA-512, MD5 when present in lockfile); populate SPDX LicenseInfoInFile / CycloneDX licenses from Component.licenses: Vec<License> (SPDX expression strings).
  • Relationship graph: DESCRIBES/CONTAINS/DEPENDS_ON edges in SPDX, dependency tree in CycloneDX; partial SBOMs (component list only, no relationships) are valid ingestion input.
  • Constraints: no network I/O in this crate; no manifest parsing (consume scs-resolve output only); all string allocations interned via scs-core::intern.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Module layout (scs-sbom/src/):

Module Responsibility
emit/spdx.rs Serialize Sbom → SPDX 2.3 tag-value + JSON
emit/cyclonedx.rs Serialize Sbom → CycloneDX 1.6 JSON + XML
ingest/spdx.rs Parse SPDX tag-value + JSON → Sbom
ingest/cyclonedx.rs Parse CycloneDX JSON + XML → Sbom
hash.rs HashSet builder; hex-decode/encode; multi-algo
license.rs SPDX expression tokenizer; License::parse(expr: &str) -> Result<License>
diff.rs sbom_diff(a: &Sbom, b: &Sbom) -> SbomDiff (added/removed/changed components)
roundtrip.rs roundtrip_check(sbom: &Sbom, format: SbomFormat) -> Result<()>
Key data structures (in scs-core): 
Sbom {
  format: SbomFormat,          // Spdx23 | CycloneDx16
  doc_namespace: String,       // SPDX: unique URI; CDX: serialNumber
  components: Vec<Component>,
  relationships: Vec<Relationship>,
  snapshot_id: SnapshotId,     // feed snapshot when SBOM was created
}
Relationship { from: Purl, to: Purl, kind: RelKind }
RelKind { DependsOn | Contains | Describes | DevDependsOn }

Emit pipeline: 1) GraphToSbom::build(graph) -> Sbom — topological iteration, one Component per unique purl, dedup by canonical purl string; 2) SPDX: SPDXID = SPDXRef-{sha256(purl)[..8]}, emit PackageVersion/PackageChecksum/PackageLicenseDeclared; 3) CycloneDX: bom-ref=purl, version, hashes[], licenses[], dependencies mirroring graph edges (SPDX spec P14; CycloneDX spec P15); 4) XML emit via quick-xml streaming writer (no DOM), JSON via serde_json; 5) tag-value SPDX: line-oriented writer, spec-mandated field order, ##--- separator between packages. Ingest pipeline: 1) auto-detect format by sniffing first 512 bytes (SPDXVersion:, "spdxVersion", "bomFormat":"CycloneDX", XML root <bom); 2) SPDX JSON via serde_json::from_reader (deny_unknown_fields=false), extract packages/relationships/snippets (snippets ignored); 3) CycloneDX XML via quick-xml event parser, state machine BomRoot → Component → Hashes → Licenses → Dependencies; 4) normalize: construct Purl from SPDX ExternalRef: PACKAGE-MANAGER purl2 pkg:... or CycloneDX purl field, fallback synthesize from name@version@ecosystem; 5) populate Component.hashes from PackageChecksum/hashes[], licenses via license.rs::parse; 6) tracing::warn! per component missing a purl (SBOM_MISSING_PURL counter). Observability: metrics::counter!("sbom.components_emitted", ecosystem) per emit; metrics::counter!("sbom.ingest_unknown_purl") per synthesized-purl fallback; tracing::info_span!("sbom_emit", format=?fmt, component_count=n) and info_span!("sbom_ingest", format=?fmt, source=path). Test harness: tests/roundtrip.rs emits SPDX+CycloneDX → parses → compares component sets (components_in == components_out); tests/ingest_third_party.rs parses scs-bench/fixtures/sbom/ fixtures, asserts purl+hash+license fields; tests/diff.rs two SBOMs with controlled add/remove/version-change, asserts SbomDiff fields; property test via proptest: random DependencyGraph → emit → ingest → assert component count preserved.

3.3 Deliverable

  • scs-sbom crate compiles; scs-cli sbom --emit spdx-json | cyclonedx-json | cyclonedx-xml | spdx-tv produces valid documents (validated by spdx-tools Java validator + cyclonedx-cli validate).
  • scs-cli sbom --ingest <path> reads a third-party SBOM, prints component count + unmatched-purl count.
  • scs-cli sbom --diff <old.sbom> <new.sbom> prints added/removed/changed components.
  • Round-trip test suite green; no panics on any fixture from scs-bench/fixtures/sbom/.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Round-trip correctness 100% component+relationship preservation across all 4 formats
SPDX validation Zero errors from spdx-tools-java --validate on emitted documents
CycloneDX validation Zero errors from cyclonedx-cli validate on emitted JSON + XML
Ingest coverage Parse all 8 SBOM fixtures without panic; missing-purl rate ≤ 2% on realistic fixture
Emit throughput 10 000-component graph → SPDX JSON in ≤ 400 ms on reference hardware
Memory ceiling Emit peak RSS ≤ 3× serialized output size
License parse 100% of SPDX FOSS expression fixtures parse without error
Diff correctness 100% of controlled diff fixtures produce expected SbomDiff output

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fixtures/sbom/spdx-json-minimal.json Minimal SPDX 2.3 JSON (1 component) 1 component, purl present
fixtures/sbom/spdx-tv-complex.spdx Multi-package SPDX tag-value with relationships Component + RelKind roundtrip
fixtures/sbom/cyclonedx-json-16.json Full CycloneDX 1.6 JSON incl. services + formulation Components only extracted (services ignored)
fixtures/sbom/cyclonedx-xml-nested.xml CycloneDX XML with nested <components> Flat component list; parent-child edges
fixtures/sbom/sbom-no-purl.json CycloneDX with no purl fields Synthesized purl; SBOM_MISSING_PURL incremented
fixtures/sbom/sbom-malformed-license.json Invalid SPDX license expression License::Unknown(raw) fallback; no panic
fixtures/sbom/sbom-duplicate-purls.json Two components with identical purl Dedup to one; emit count matches
fixtures/sbom/sbom-empty.json Zero-component CycloneDX Empty Sbom; no panic
fixtures/sbom/sbom-large-5k.json 5 000-component CycloneDX Throughput + memory benchmark
fixtures/sbom/diff-old.json + diff-new.json Controlled add/remove/version delta SbomDiff {added:[X], removed:[Y], changed:[Z]}

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Purl namespace collision — two SPDX packages share ExternalRef purl value with different case; ingest deduplicates by case-insensitive purl; signal: SBOM_MISSING_PURL stays 0 but component count wrong. Debug: RUST_LOG=scs_sbom=debug logs each purl normalization.
  • CycloneDX nested <components> — spec allows components within components (firmware); naive flat parser misses children. Root cause: state machine only handles top-level. Signal: component_count metric lower than fixture expectation. Fix: push/pop component stack on <components> open/close events.
  • SPDX doc namespace uniqueness — two parallel scans emit docs with same namespace URI; downstream tooling may reject. Root cause: namespace generated from project name only, not scan timestamp+uuid. Fix: append SnapshotId UUID to namespace.
  • XML namespace prefix drift — quick-xml may emit ns0: prefix instead of default namespace; some validators reject. Debug: compare raw output with xmllint --format; fix: explicit namespace binding in writer.
  • SPDX LicenseRef- custom identifiers — packages with custom license strings generate LicenseRef-scancode-X; round-trip breaks if ID not preserved verbatim. Signal: roundtrip test fails on license field. Fix: store raw string in License::Ref(String) variant.
  • Timestamp field in round-trip — SPDX Created: timestamp changes on each emit; round-trip byte-comparison fails. Fix: pin timestamp to SnapshotId timestamp; strip from comparison in test.
  • CycloneDX bomFormat field case — some third-party tools emit "bomFormat": "cyclonedx" (lowercase); auto-detect fails. Fix: case-insensitive comparison in sniff routine.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Allocation per component: each Component emitted allocates purl + hash strings; intern via scs-core::intern to reuse across emit + ingest paths.
  • XML streaming vs. DOM: quick-xml streaming avoids O(N) DOM allocation; do not switch to roxmltree for large files.
  • JSON serialization: use serde_json::to_writer (streaming) not to_string to avoid double-buffer allocation for large SBOMs.
  • Relationship edge explosion: dense graphs (10k nodes, 50k edges) serialize all edges; cap relationship emit at MAX_EDGES = 500_000; emit warning if exceeded.
  • Hash hex encoding: avoid format!("{:x}", ...) per-byte loop; use hex::encode which is SIMD-optimized.
  • Tag-value line buffering: write to BufWriter<File> with 64 KB buffer; each package is ~15 lines; one write! per field.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty graph: zero-component DependencyGraph → emit valid SPDX/CycloneDX with document header only; ingest returns empty Sbom.
  • Component with no hashes: emit omits PackageChecksum / hashes section; ingest sets Component.hashes = vec![].
  • Non-UTF-8 in lockfile path: purl percent-encodes non-ASCII; SPDX PackageName escapes per spec §3.3.
  • Circular relationship edges: A DEPENDS_ON B, B DEPENDS_ON A; emit both; ingest without infinite loop (visited set).
  • SBOM with 64-bit component count overflow: usize component index; u32 bom-ref counter; panic on >4 billion components (unreachable in practice; assert len < u32::MAX).
  • Malformed XML entity: &amp; in package name; quick-xml decodes automatically; re-encode on emit.
  • Missing SPDXID field: ingest sets synthetic ID = SPDXRef-{sha256(purl)[..12]}; logs warning.

3.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Emit panics on large graph process crash, non-zero exit scan aborted add catch_unwind in CLI driver; retry with --no-sbom flag
Ingest silently drops components component_count metric < expected false-negative matches re-run with RUST_LOG=scs_sbom=debug; compare raw + parsed count
Invalid SPDX emitted CI validator step fails SBOM unusable downstream fix schema bug; no data loss (re-emit from same graph)
CycloneDX XML parse panic Result::Err propagated scan exits non-zero report parse error with byte offset; skip SBOM ingest, proceed with manifest scan
Round-trip relationship loss roundtrip_check returns Err relationship metadata missing log discrepancy; emit warning in report; relationships non-critical for finding generation
Postmortem guidance: on SBOM validation failure, capture emitted file + scs-bench fixture used; reproduce with cargo test --features ci-fixtures; diff against known-good fixture.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: sbom.components_emitted{format,ecosystem}, sbom.ingest_unknown_purl, sbom.emit_duration_ms{format}, sbom.roundtrip_check_failures.
  • Tracing: spans sbom_emit + sbom_ingest at INFO; per-component warn at DEBUG.
  • Log fields: format, component_count, relationship_count, snapshot_id, source_path.
  • CI integration: scs-cli sbom --emit cyclonedx-json --output bom.json; artifact upload; downstream cyclonedx-cli validate bom.json.
  • Compatibility: SPDX 2.3 backward-compatible with 2.2 consumers (no new required fields added); CycloneDX 1.6 incompatible with 1.4 consumers on formulation field — emit version header clearly.
  • Migration: if SBOM format version bumped (e.g., CycloneDX 1.7), add new emit/cyclonedx_17.rs without removing 1.6 variant; SbomFormat enum gets new variant; --sbom-format flag selects version.

16. Phase 4 — Advisory database ingestion — OSV / NVD-CVE / GHSA feed sync, normalization, per-ecosystem version-range indexing, incremental update

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement scs-vulndb: ingest OSV JSON schema v1, NVD CVE 2.0 JSON API, GHSA GraphQL API into normalized Advisory records.
  • Persist advisory index to a local SQLite database (vulndb.sqlite) with WAL mode; support incremental sync (delta since last fetch).
  • Build per-ecosystem version-range index: EcosystemIndex { ecosystem: Ecosystem, ranges: BTreeMap<PackageId, Vec<AffectedRange>> }.
  • Record SnapshotId (feed name + commit/etag + timestamp) for every sync; every scan references the snapshot used (feed freshness invariant).
  • Normalize advisory IDs: canonical form is Advisory.id = CVE-XXXX-NNNNN (preferred) or GHSA-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX or OSID-XXXX; populate Advisory.aliases.
  • Support offline mode: if network unavailable and local DB exists, scan proceeds against stale DB; stale age recorded in report.
  • Constraints: all network I/O isolated to scs-vulndb::feed module; no network in matching phase; DB path configurable; feed creds (NVD API key, GHSA token) via env vars only.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Module layout (scs-vulndb/src/):

Module Responsibility
feed/osv.rs Fetch OSV batch JSON + ecosystem zips; parse OSV schema v1
feed/nvd.rs NVD CVE 2.0 REST API pagination; rate-limit (50 req/30s without key, 2000/30s with key)
feed/ghsa.rs GHSA GraphQL (securityAdvisories + securityVulnerabilities queries); cursor pagination
store/db.rs SQLite schema, migrations, upsert/query ops; rusqlite with WAL
store/index.rs In-memory EcosystemIndex built from DB; range lookup API
normalize.rs Advisory normalization: ID canonicalization, alias dedup, range coercion
snapshot.rs SnapshotId generation, recording, lookup
sync.rs Orchestrate incremental sync: compare etag/cursor, delta fetch, upsert, rebuild index
SQLite schema (DDL, not source — illustrative): 
advisories(id TEXT PK, canonical_id TEXT, aliases JSON,
           ecosystem TEXT, package_name TEXT,
           introduced TEXT, fixed TEXT, last_affected TEXT,
           cvss_score REAL, cvss_vector TEXT, epss_score REAL,
           withdrawn_at INTEGER, raw_json TEXT,
           snapshot_id TEXT, updated_at INTEGER)
snapshots(id TEXT PK, feed TEXT, etag TEXT, cursor TEXT,
          fetched_at INTEGER, record_count INTEGER)
idx_advisories_pkg: (ecosystem, package_name, introduced, fixed)

OSV ingest: 1) fetch osv.dev/ecosystems.json, per ecosystem fetch bulk zip or paginate osv.dev/v1/query; 2) serde_json::from_reader::<OsvEntry> with #[serde(default)] on optional fields; 3) map affected[].ranges[].type → AffectedRange, only ECOSYSTEM+SEMVER types, skip GIT ranges; 4) map severity → Cvss{score,vector}, prefer v3.1 > v3.0 > v2; 5) upsert into advisories, store raw JSON for audit, emit vulndb.osv_ingested counter (OSV schema P6; NVD schema P5). NVD ingest: 1) GET /rest/json/cves/2.0?pubStartDate=X&resultsPerPage=2000, paginate startIndex against totalResults; 2) respect Retry-After on 403/429, exponential backoff+jitter (30s→300s max); 3) map configurations[].nodes[].cpeMatch[].criteria CPE 2.3 → purl via CPE→purl translation table; 4) extract metrics.cvssMetricV31[0] → Cvss, fallback V30/V2; 5) set aliases from GHSA refs in references if present; 6) incremental: store lastModStartDate cursor in snapshots, fetch only modified CVEs since last sync. GHSA ingest: 1) GraphQL securityAdvisories(after: $cursor, updatedSince: $since), paginate until hasNextPage=false; 2) extract vulnerabilities[], map ecosystem+package.name+vulnerableVersionRange → AffectedRange; 3) range syntax ">= 1.0, < 2.0" parsed via normalize::parse_ghsa_range(s); 4) map GHSA ID → canonical CVE if identifiers includes CVE, store both in aliases; 5) cursor stored in snapshots.cursor, subsequent syncs pass updatedSince = snapshots.fetched_at. Version-range index: 1) SELECT ecosystem, package_name, introduced, fixed, last_affected FROM advisories WHERE withdrawn_at IS NULL; 2) group by (ecosystem, package_name) → Vec<AffectedRange>, sort by introduced ascending; 3) store in Arc<EcosystemIndex>, rebuilt atomically after each sync (swap Arc under RwLock); 4) lookup API fn lookup(idx, pkg) -> &[AffectedRange] — O(log N) BTreeMap lookup. Sync orchestration (sync.rs): async fn sync_all(cfg) -> Result<SnapshotId> parallel-fetches OSV+NVD+GHSA via tokio::join!; async fn sync_incremental(feed, cursor) -> Result<SyncStats> per-feed; writes all rows in a single SQLite transaction, commits atomically, rolls back on failure; after commit rebuilds index and emits vulndb.sync_completed{feed} + vulndb.advisory_count{ecosystem}.

4.3 Deliverable

  • scs-cli vulndb sync fetches all three feeds; populates ~/.scs/vulndb.sqlite; prints record counts per ecosystem + snapshot ID.
  • scs-cli vulndb stats prints: record count, snapshot IDs per feed, age of each feed, largest ecosystems.
  • scs-cli vulndb lookup pkg:npm/lodash returns matching advisories from local index.
  • In-memory EcosystemIndex rebuilt in ≤ 5 s for 500k advisories; lookup latency ≤ 1 μs.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
OSV ingest coverage ≥ 99% of osv.dev published advisories ingested without error
NVD ingest accuracy CVSS score matches nvd.nist.gov UI for 100 sampled CVEs
GHSA alias resolution ≥ 95% of GHSA entries linked to CVE ID where one exists
Incremental sync Re-sync fetches only delta; ≤ 10% of full-sync record count on no-change run
Index build time 500k advisories → index in ≤ 5 s single-threaded
Lookup latency p99 ≤ 1 μs for EcosystemIndex::lookup on hot path
DB durability WAL commit; no data loss on SIGKILL mid-sync (test: kill mid-write, re-open, count intact)
Snapshot recorded Every scan log contains snapshot_id with feed + timestamp
Feed stale warning Advisory age > 48 h triggers WARN in report

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fixtures/vulndb/osv-entry-full.json Full OSV v1 entry (GHSA-XXXX with aliases, CVSS, ranges) Advisory with CVE alias, AffectedRange, Cvss
fixtures/vulndb/osv-entry-git-range.json OSV entry with GIT range only AffectedRange empty; advisory recorded with no ranges
fixtures/vulndb/nvd-cve-2.0-page.json NVD CVE 2.0 API page (10 CVEs) 10 advisories; CVSS v3.1 preferred
fixtures/vulndb/nvd-cpe-no-purl.json NVD CVE with CPE but no recognizable package Advisory stored; purl synthesis fails gracefully
fixtures/vulndb/ghsa-graphql-page.json GHSA GraphQL response (cursor + 25 advisories) 25 advisories; cursor extracted for next page
fixtures/vulndb/ghsa-range-complex.json GHSA vulnerableVersionRange: ">= 1.0, < 1.5, >= 1.6, < 1.8" Two AffectedRange records
fixtures/vulndb/advisory-withdrawn.json OSV entry with withdrawn timestamp Advisory.withdrawn = Some(ts); excluded from active index
fixtures/vulndb/advisory-duplicate-ids.json Same CVE from OSV + NVD + GHSA Single canonical advisory; three aliases
fixtures/vulndb/db-integrity-kill.sh Script: start sync, SIGKILL, reopen DB valid; partial sync rolled back
fixtures/vulndb/incremental-delta.json Simulated delta sync (5 new + 3 updated) DB has new count; snapshot updated

4.6 Pitfalls

  • NVD rate-limit without API key — 429 response mid-pagination; naive retry without Retry-After burns remaining quota. Root cause: missing header handling. Debug: vulndb.nvd_rate_limited counter spike. Fix: parse Retry-After: 30 header; sleep exact duration + jitter.
  • OSV GIT ranges silently dropped — GIT-type ranges have no version semantics; dropping them causes false-negatives for source-code consumers. Signal: range count < expected for some advisories. Mitigation: store raw range in advisory.raw_json; future phase can add git-hash matching.
  • GHSA version range parsing edge cases — range "= 1.2.3" (exact) not in original GHSA spec; some entries use it. Root cause: non-standard range syntax. Fix: extend parse_ghsa_range to handle = as >= X, <= X.
  • Alias dedup across feeds — OSV records CVE-2021-44228 as log4j; NVD records same with different CPE; GHSA uses GHSA alias; naive upsert creates three rows. Fix: after each feed sync, run normalize::merge_aliases which groups by canonical CVE ID.
  • SQLite WAL corruption on SIGKILL during checkpoint — checkpoint in progress when process killed; WAL file left open. Fix: on DB open, run PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(RESTART) first; check integrity_check.
  • NVD CVE 2.0 pagination off-by-one — totalResults=2000, resultsPerPage=2000, startIndex=0 → single page; startIndex=2000 returns empty; naive loop fetches empty page twice. Fix: while start_index < total_results condition.
  • EPSS score not in OSV/GHSA — only available from EPSS API separately; omitting it leaves epss_score = None; matching phase must handle None gracefully.

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • NVD full sync: ~250k CVEs, 2 000/page → 125 HTTP requests; each page ~2 MB JSON; total ~250 MB. Stream-parse with serde_json::Deserializer::from_reader; do not buffer full response.
  • OSV bulk zip: per-ecosystem zips up to 100 MB; zip::ZipArchive reads sequentially; decompress into tempdir, parse in parallel with rayon.
  • SQLite upsert throughput: single-row INSERT OR REPLACE at 50k rows/s; batch into 10k-row transactions for 10× speedup. Use BEGIN IMMEDIATE to avoid lock upgrades.
  • Index rebuild allocation: 500k AffectedRange structs × ~80 bytes = ~40 MB; acceptable. Use Vec::with_capacity from SQL COUNT(*) pre-query.
  • HTTP connection reuse: share single reqwest::Client across all feed fetches; enables connection pooling; do not create per-request client.
  • GHSA GraphQL page size: max 100 nodes per page; 100k advisories → 1000 requests; use tokio::time::sleep between pages to respect secondary rate limit (1k req/min authenticated).

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty feed response: OSV ecosystem returns 0 records; upsert 0 rows; snapshot still recorded with record_count=0.
  • Advisory with no affected ranges: valid (informational advisory); store without index entry; lookup returns empty.
  • Timestamp overflow: withdrawn_at stored as INTEGER (Unix seconds); far-future dates safe with i64.
  • DB file locked by another process: rusqlite returns SQLITE_BUSY; retry with 50ms sleep × 10; then return ScsError::DbBusy.
  • Concurrent sync + scan: scan reads index under RwLock::read(); sync rebuilds index under RwLock::write() after commit; no reader starvation (tokio scheduler fairness).
  • Partial OSV zip: zip file truncated mid-archive; zip::read::ZipFile::read_to_end returns Err; log + skip that file; continue other ecosystems.
  • CVSS v2-only advisory: no v3 score; store cvss_score from v2; flag cvss_version: CvssV2 in Cvss struct.

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
NVD API key expired 403 on every NVD request; vulndb.nvd_fetch_errors counter NVD data stale rotate key in env var; re-run sync
OSV feed unavailable timeout on fetch; vulndb.osv_fetch_errors OSV data stale; scan proceeds on old DB stale age in report; re-run when available
DB schema migration fails migration error on open; process exits non-zero all scans blocked restore from backup; or scs-cli vulndb reset --confirm (drops DB)
Index rebuild OOM allocator returns Err or SIGKILL (OOM killer) scans fail reduce index scope; add --ecosystem filter; ulimit increase
Alias merge creates wrong canonical false-negative match (wrong CVE ID) missed finding postmortem: vulndb lookup --raw shows alias chain; re-normalize
Duplicate advisories (race on parallel sync) unique constraint violation in SQLite one feed's data wins; other dropped serialize per-advisory upserts; INSERT OR REPLACE is atomic
Postmortem guidance: on DB corruption, sqlite3 vulndb.sqlite "PRAGMA integrity_check" → if non-OK, delete + re-sync. Always check snapshots table timestamps to identify stale feeds before filing false-negative bugs.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: vulndb.advisory_count{ecosystem}, vulndb.sync_duration_ms{feed}, vulndb.nvd_rate_limited, vulndb.fetch_errors{feed}, vulndb.index_rebuild_ms, vulndb.snapshot_age_hours{feed}.
  • Tracing: info_span!("vulndb_sync", feed, snapshot_id); debug per-page fetch with count.
  • Log fields: feed, record_count, delta_count, snapshot_id, etag, duration_ms.
  • Deployment: DB file at ${SCS_DATA_DIR:-~/.scs}/vulndb.sqlite; configurable via --db-path; CI: pre-populated DB in cache layer (GitHub Actions cache key = vulndb-{date}).
  • Scheduled sync: scs-cli vulndb sync --cron for daemon mode; or systemd timer / cron job (daily at 03:00 UTC recommended); stale threshold 48 h.
  • Rollback: keep last 2 DB snapshots (.sqlite.bak); on corrupt DB, scs-cli vulndb restore --from .sqlite.bak.
  • Environment variables: NVD_API_KEY, GHSA_TOKEN, SCS_DATA_DIR, SCS_OFFLINE=1 (skip fetch, use existing DB).
  • Compatibility: OSV schema v1.x backward-compatible; NVD CVE 2.0 breaks from 1.1 (different JSON structure); version auto-detected from response version field.

17. Phase 5 — Vulnerability matching engine — version-range satisfiability (SemVer/PEP440/Maven), CVE<->GHSA<->OSV correlation, fixed-version computation, withdrawn/disputed handling

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement scs-match: given a DependencyGraph + EcosystemIndex, produce Vec<Finding> with zero false-negatives (match soundness invariant).
  • Version-range satisfiability per ecosystem: SemVer (npm/Cargo), PEP 440 (PyPI), Maven version ranges, RubyGems ~>, Go pseudo-versions.
  • Half-open [introduced, fixed) semantics: version in [introduced, ∞) and NOT in [fixed, ∞) → affected; multi-range advisory: component must satisfy ANY range (union), safe only if it satisfies NONE.
  • Correlate CVE↔GHSA↔OSV IDs: dedupe Finding records so one component × one vulnerability produces one Finding regardless of source feed; compute Finding.fixed as the minimum fixed_version across all matching ranges where a fix exists.
  • Handle withdrawn: Some(ts) advisories: exclude from active findings; emit Finding.vex_status = Withdrawn; handle disputed tag (NVD) as Finding.vex_status = Disputed (still reported, user-visible note).
  • Path completeness: every Finding.path: Vec<Purl> traces from the affected component to a graph root (BFS shortest path).
  • Constraints: pure computation — no I/O; deterministic output (stable sort by purl → advisory_id); no allocation per query in hot path (reuse buffers).

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Module layout (scs-match/src/):

Module Responsibility
matcher.rs match_all(graph: &DependencyGraph, index: &EcosystemIndex) -> Vec<Finding>
satisfiability/semver.rs SemVer range check: semver_satisfies(v: &Version, r: &AffectedRange) -> bool
satisfiability/pep440.rs PEP 440 range check: pep440_satisfies(v: &Version, r: &AffectedRange) -> bool
satisfiability/maven.rs Maven version range: maven_satisfies(v: &Version, r: &AffectedRange) -> bool
satisfiability/{rubygems,go}.rs RubyGems ~> pessimistic constraint operator; Go module pseudo-version comparator
correlation.rs dedup_findings(findings: Vec<Finding>) -> Vec<Finding> — alias-aware dedup
fixed_version.rs compute_fixed(ranges: &[AffectedRange]) -> Option<Version>
path.rs BFS shortest dependency path from component to graph root
vex.rs Apply VEX statements (from scs-policy) to set Finding.vex_status
Core matching algorithm (matcher.rs): 
for component in graph.components():
    ranges = index.lookup(&component.package_id)  // O(log N)
    for range in ranges:
        if satisfies(component.version, range):
            path = bfs_to_root(graph, component.purl)
            fixed = compute_fixed(ranges)
            push Finding { component, advisory, path, fixed, vex_status: Active }
deduplicate by (purl, canonical_advisory_id)
stable_sort by (purl, advisory_id)

SemVer (semver.rs): crate semver; introduced="" → 0.0.0 (beginning of time); fixed="" (no fix) → upper bound ∞; build metadata (+build) stripped before parse; pre-release 1.0.0-alpha < 1.0.0, affected only if range explicitly includes pre-releases; OSV last_affected closed range [introduced, last_affected] mapped to [introduced, next_patch(last_affected)) (SemVer spec S8, ADR-009). PEP 440 (pep440.rs): crate pep440_rs; specifiers ==,!=,<,>,<=,>=,~=,=== parse GHSA vulnerableVersionRange; epoch precedence 1!1.0 > 1.0; post/dev/local releases 1.0.post1 > 1.0, 1.0.dev1 < 1.0; OSV introduced/fixed parsed via pep440_rs::Version::from_str. Maven (maven.rs): range notation [1.0,2.0), (,1.0], [1.5] (exact), comma-delimited multi-range; MavenVersion comparator: integer dot segments, SNAPSHOT suffix, qualifier ordering (alpha<beta<milestone<rc<ga); no external crate, parse_maven_range(s) -> Vec<MavenInterval> (~100 lines); MavenInterval{lo,hi: Bound<MavenVersion>}.contains(v). Go (go.rs): pseudo-version v0.0.0-20060102150405-abcdef123456 compared by timestamp then hash; tagged releases use regular semver (v prefix stripped); OSV Go ranges detected as pseudo-version via regex ^\d{14}-. Dedup (correlation.rs): alias map HashMap<String, CanonicalId> from Advisory.aliases grouped by canonical; two Findings with same (purl, canonical_advisory_id) keep the higher-severity one, merge aliases; emits vulndb.findings_deduped counter (removed count). Fixed-version (fixed_version.rs): compute_fixed(ranges) -> Option<Version> filters ranges where fixed.is_some(), parses each per ecosystem, returns the minimum (earliest fix closes the vulnerability). BFS path (path.rs): reverse-adjacency list built once per DependencyGraph (reused across findings); bfs(start, roots, radj) -> Vec<Purl> to nearest root, O(V+E); direct dependency (component IS a root) → path=[purl]; result is shortest path only (prevents combinatorial explosion in deep graphs). Observability: metrics::histogram!("match.findings_per_component"); metrics::counter!("match.satisfiability_checks{ecosystem}"); tracing::info_span!("match_all", component_count, advisory_count); tracing::debug!("finding", purl, advisory_id, introduced, fixed) per finding. Test harness: tests/semver_satisfiability.rs (50+ parameterized cases from OSV vectors + edge cases); tests/pep440_satisfiability.rs (30+ PEP 440 spec examples); tests/maven_satisfiability.rs (20+ incl. SNAPSHOT/epoch/qualifier ordering); tests/match_all.rs (synthetic graph × index, assert exact Finding set); tests/dedup.rs (multi-feed same CVE → single Finding); tests/path_completeness.rs (deep graph, every path non-empty + terminates at root); property test: random SemVer version+range → no panic, consistent with semver::VersionReq.

5.3 Deliverable

  • scs-match crate compiles; match_all returns deterministic Vec<Finding> on identical inputs.
  • scs-cli scan --path . pipeline complete from manifest parse → resolve → match → findings output.
  • scs-cli scan prints table of findings: purl, CVE/GHSA, severity, installed version, fixed version, dep path.
  • Zero false-negatives on all scs-bench golden fixtures: known-vulnerable component always appears in output.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Match soundness 0 false-negatives on all 20 scs-bench golden fixtures
SemVer correctness 100% pass on 50-case parameterized test suite
PEP 440 correctness 100% pass on 30-case parameterized test suite
Maven correctness 100% pass on 20-case parameterized test suite incl. SNAPSHOT
Deduplication Multi-feed fixtures produce exactly 1 Finding per (purl, CVE)
Path completeness 100% of findings have non-empty path terminating at a root
Fixed-version accuracy Finding.fixed matches OSV fixed field for 100 sampled advisories
Match throughput 10k components × 500k advisory ranges → findings in ≤ 2 s
Determinism Identical inputs → byte-identical sorted Finding JSON on 10 runs

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fixtures/match/semver-ranges.toml 50 (version, introduced, fixed, expected_match) tuples All assertions pass
fixtures/match/pep440-ranges.toml 30 PEP 440 satisfiability cases All assertions pass
fixtures/match/maven-ranges.toml 20 Maven range cases incl. [1.0,), (,1.5] All assertions pass
fixtures/match/go-pseudoversion.toml 10 Go pseudo-version comparison cases Correct ordering
fixtures/match/log4shell-graph.json DependencyGraph with log4j 2.14.1; CVE-2021-44228 advisory Finding with CRITICAL severity, path to root
fixtures/match/event-stream-graph.json npm graph with malicious event-stream 3.3.6 (threat phase) Handled by scs-threat; match produces 0 findings (no advisory)
fixtures/match/multi-feed-same-cve.json OSV + GHSA + NVD records for CVE-2021-44228 Single deduped Finding
fixtures/match/withdrawn-advisory.json Component version in affected range; advisory withdrawn Finding with vex_status: Withdrawn
fixtures/match/disputed-nvd.json NVD CVE with DISPUTED tag Finding with vex_status: Disputed
fixtures/match/no-fix-available.json Advisory with no fixed field Finding.fixed = None
fixtures/match/deeply-transitive.json 5-hop transitive dep chain; vulnerable leaf Finding with path length 5
fixtures/match/last-affected.json OSV entry with last_affected instead of fixed Correct closed-range satisfiability

5.6 Pitfalls

  • Half-open boundary off-by-one — advisory says fixed = "1.2.3"; component has version exactly 1.2.3; should NOT be a finding. Root cause: using <= instead of < for fixed bound. Signal: false-positive on exact fixed version. Debug: log (introduced, fixed, component_version, result) at TRACE; add specific test case.
  • Pre-release version in SemVer range — OSV range [1.0.0, 2.0.0); component is 2.0.0-beta.1; semver spec says 2.0.0-beta.1 < 2.0.0 → component IS in range (false-positive risk if user has upgraded to RC). Fix: document behavior; emit Finding.note = "pre-release version in range".
  • Maven SNAPSHOT version comparison — 1.5-SNAPSHOT treated as between 1.4 and 1.5; OSV range [1.0, 1.5) → 1.5-SNAPSHOT < 1.5 → affected. Some users expect SNAPSHOT excluded. Fix: configurable flag --include-snapshot-versions; default include.
  • Go pseudo-version timestamp comparison — two pseudo-versions with same timestamp, different hash; lexicographic sort on hash is undefined (hash is short SHA, not version). Fix: if timestamps equal, sort is stable but arbitrary; emit debug log.
  • Alias cycle in deduplication — OSV advisory A aliases GHSA-B; GHSA-B aliases CVE-C (canonical); another OSV entry D also aliases CVE-C; correlation.rs must handle transitive aliases without infinite loop. Fix: union-find (disjoint-set) for alias merging; path compression.
  • last_affected vs fixed semantics — advisory has last_affected="1.2.3" but no fixed; means 1.2.3 is last known affected; range is [introduced, 1.2.4) (exclusive of next patch). compute_fixed must return next_patch(last_affected), not last_affected itself.
  • Empty introduced field — OSV entry with introduced = "0" (not semver); SemVer::parse("0") fails. Fix: normalize "0" → "0.0.0" before parse; add normalization table per ecosystem.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Hot path allocation: match_all inner loop runs O(components × ranges_per_pkg) times; avoid allocating Vec<Finding> per component; push into pre-allocated Vec::with_capacity(estimate).
  • BFS reverse-adjacency: build once outside main loop; O(V+E) construction; amortized O(1) per component lookup.
  • SemVer parse in hot path: parse introduced/fixed at index-build time (Phase 4), not at match time; store as ParsedVersion enum in AffectedRange; match phase does integer comparison only.
  • EcosystemIndex BTreeMap lookup: O(log N) per component; for 10k components × log(500k) ≈ 19 comparisons = 190k ops; negligible. Do not optimize to hash map unless profiling shows otherwise.
  • Dedup hash map: HashMap<(Purl, CanonicalId), Finding> built once after match; O(F log F) sort at end; F = finding count (typically ≪ component count).
  • Path BFS per finding: BFS is O(V+E) per finding; cache paths per component (memoize by purl); second finding on same component reuses cached path.
  • Parallel matching: rayon::par_iter() over components; each component independently looks up index; EcosystemIndex behind Arc<_> (read-only); findings collected with par_iter().flat_map().collect().

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Component with no ecosystem in purl — purl parse fails; skip matching for that component; emit match.unknown_ecosystem counter; log warning.
  • Advisory range introduced = fixed — zero-width range [X, X) → empty; never matches. Log debug; no finding.
  • Component with multiple versions — e.g., hoisted + non-hoisted npm; DependencyGraph has two nodes with same name but different purls; match each independently; may produce two findings for same advisory.
  • Advisory with 100+ affected ranges — pathological; iterate all ranges; satisfiability check each; no short-circuit for correctness; but log match.large_range_advisory{count} counter.
  • Version parse failure — component version not parseable as SemVer/PEP440/Maven (e.g., "unknown", ""); skip satisfiability check; emit match.version_parse_error{ecosystem} counter; no panic.
  • Graph with 0 components — empty graph; match_all returns vec![] immediately.
  • Withdrawn advisory still in index — race between index rebuild and scan; AffectedRange present but Advisory.withdrawn.is_some(); satisfy check runs; Finding.vex_status = Withdrawn set correctly.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
False-negative (missed finding) Regression test fails; scs-bench --diff shows new miss Security gap Bisect by comparing (version, range, expected) in semver-ranges.toml; fix satisfiability logic
False-positive (wrong match) User reports non-vulnerable component flagged CI blocks on false alarm --explain-finding flag prints (version, introduced, fixed, satisfies=true); add to suppression or fix logic
match_all OOM on large graph SIGKILL from OOM; non-zero exit Scan fails Profile allocation; enable --streaming-match mode (process components in batches of 1k)
Dedup infinite loop on alias cycle process hangs; match.dedup_duration_ms never finishes Scan hangs Union-find with cycle detection; add RUST_LOG=scs_match=trace to debug alias graph
Wrong fixed-version returned User upgrades to incorrect version; stays vulnerable Persistent vulnerability vulndb lookup --raw <cve> shows raw fixed field; compare against compute_fixed output; fix last_affected → next_patch logic
Path BFS stack overflow (deep graph) stack overflow panic Scan crash Replace recursive BFS with iterative queue; depth limit 500 with truncation marker
Postmortem guidance: on false-negative report, capture (1) DependencyGraph JSON, (2) EcosystemIndex snapshot, (3) advisory raw JSON; reproduce with cargo test -- --nocapture match_regression; check range boundary condition first.

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: match.findings_total{severity,ecosystem}, match.satisfiability_checks{ecosystem}, match.version_parse_errors{ecosystem}, match.dedup_removed, match.duration_ms, match.false_negatives_detected (from regression harness).
  • Tracing: info_span!("match_all", component_count, advisory_count, finding_count) at INFO; per-finding debug! with (purl, advisory_id, introduced, fixed, satisfies).
  • Log fields: snapshot_id (from index), ecosystem, component_count, finding_count, dedup_count, duration_ms.
  • Debugging flags: SCS_MATCH_TRACE=1 enables per-range satisfiability logging (very verbose; CI only); --explain-finding <purl> <cve> prints full range evaluation trace to stderr.
  • Determinism guarantee: findings sorted by (purl.to_string(), advisory.id) before output; snapshot_id recorded in output; same inputs + same snapshot → identical output byte-for-byte.
  • Compatibility: matching logic versioned by MATCH_ALGO_VERSION = 1; bumped when range semantics change; recorded in scan output for audit diff across algorithm versions.
  • CI integration: scs-cli scan --fail-on CRITICAL,HIGH exits non-zero on any CRITICAL/HIGH finding not suppressed by VEX/waiver; suitable for required CI step.
  • Regression harness (scs-bench): cargo bench --bench match_regression runs golden fixtures nightly; diff against stored expected outputs; alert on any new miss or new finding.

18. Phase 6 — Severity + reachability — CVSS scoring, EPSS enrichment, optional call-graph reachability (is the vulnerable symbol used?), finding prioritization

6.1 Objectives

  • Enrich every Finding with CVSS v3.1/v4.0 vector + score parsed from advisory metadata; tolerate missing scores (CVSS v2 only, or score absent).
  • Ingest EPSS probability + percentile from FIRST.org daily CSV (keyed by CVE-ID); store alongside advisory; expose in Finding.
  • Implement optional call-graph reachability pass: statically determine whether any vulnerable symbol exported by an affected component is transitively called by application code — reduces noise for library findings in large monorepos.
  • Produce deterministic, stable Finding priority order: EPSS-percentile × CVSS-base descending, then by purl (lexicographic) for ties.
  • Constraints: CVSS parsing is strict + reject malformed vectors (never silently assign score 0); EPSS ingest is best-effort (finding emitted even if EPSS absent); call-graph reachability is opt-in (--reachability flag) and marks findings reachable: Option<bool> (None = not analyzed). Scope limited to scs-core, scs-vulndb, scs-match, scs-report and a new scs-reach helper.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

scs-core additions: Cvss enum V31{vector,base_score:f32} | V40{..} | V2{..}; Epss{cve_id, probability:f32, percentile:f32, model_date:NaiveDate}; Finding gains epss: Option<Epss>, reachable: Option<bool>, priority_score: f64 (= epss_pct × cvss_base, defaulting to cvss_base if EPSS absent); Priority newtype wrapping Finding with Ord = descending priority_score then purl ascending. scs-vulndb additions: src/epss.rs downloads https://epss.cyentia.com/epss_scores-YYYY-MM-DD.csv.gz (configurable URL), decompresses with flate2, parses CSV via csv crate, stores into sled tree epss/{cve_id} as [f32;3] (prob, pct, model_date days-since-epoch); feed sync adds EPSS step after OSV/NVD, timestamp in meta/epss_date; EpssIndex::lookup(cve_id) -> Option<Epss> O(1) sled lookup. CVSS vector parsing: regex-validate AV:/AC:/PR:/UI:/S:/C:/I:/A: for v3.x, CVSS:4.0 prefix for v4; base score computed via CVSS spec formula inline (no external dep; CVSS v3.1 S6 / v4.0 S7); invariant: parsed score == advisory-provided score ± 0.05 (log mismatch, never reject). Advisory gains cvss: Option<Cvss>, epss: Option<Epss> (serialized in sled via bincode). scs-match additions: src/enrich.rs::enrich_finding(f, epss_idx) joins EPSS onto finding's advisory CVE-ID + all aliases, sets priority_score; src/prioritize.rs::sort_findings(findings) stable-sorts by Priority. scs-reach (new crate, optional): feature-gated behind workspace feature reachability, excluded from default build. Input: LLVM bitcode/MIR JSON (cargo llvm-lines or rustc --emit=mir) OR language-agnostic call-graph JSONL (node=symbol FQDN, edge=caller→callee). Structs CallGraph{nodes:Vec<Symbol>, edges:Vec<(usize,usize)>}, Symbol{fqdn:String, package:Option<Purl>}. Algorithm: BFS from application entry symbols, mark reachable nodes; Finding whose component purl matches a reachable package symbol → reachable=Some(true), else Some(false). ReachabilityAnalyzer::analyze(cg, findings) O(V+E) BFS, single-threaded post-match; observability tracing::info!(reachable_count, unreachable_count, cg_nodes, cg_edges) after BFS. scs-report additions: SARIF emits result.properties.epss_probability/epss_percentile/reachable; table adds EPSS% column + [REACHABLE]/[NOT-REACHABLE] suffix on severity cell; exit-code logic unchanged (policy gates on severity, not reachability). Test harness (scs-bench): benches/priority_sort.rs (50 000 findings, stable-sort, < 50 ms wall); tests/cvss_parse.rs (200-vector CVE-corpus roundtrip, score delta ≤ 0.05); tests/epss_ingest.rs (mock HTTP server returning fixture CSV, assert lookup correctness).

6.3 Deliverable

  • scs-match enrich pipeline produces Finding with epss, priority_score for all CVE-aliased advisories present in EPSS CSV.
  • scs-vulndb sync --epss downloads + indexes daily EPSS file; idempotent on re-run (same date = skip download).
  • scs-reach (feature-gated) marks reachable when call-graph JSONL provided; --reachability path/to/cg.jsonl CLI flag.
  • scs-report table and SARIF include EPSS% and reachability columns; scs-report --sort priority emits findings ordered by priority_score descending.
  • Operators can inspect: scscli scan --epss-date prints feed snapshot date; scscli scan --json | jq '[.findings[] | {purl, score: .priority_score}]'.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
CVSS parse unit tests 200/200 vectors parsed, score ≤ ±0.05
EPSS ingest test Mock CSV 10 000 rows → all lookups correct
Priority sort bench 50 000 findings stable-sort < 50 ms (Ryzen 5)
SARIF fields present epss_probability, reachable keys in every SARIF result
Reachability BFS 100 k node graph, BFS completes < 200 ms
Missing EPSS Finding still emitted; epss: null in JSON
Malformed CVSS vector ScsError::CvssParseError returned, not silent 0.0
Feed snapshot recorded meta/epss_date key readable; --epss-date prints date

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
fixtures/cvss/vectors_v31_200.csv 200 real CVE CVSS v3.1 vectors + reference scores parse + score within ±0.05
fixtures/cvss/vectors_v40_10.csv 10 CVSS v4.0 vectors parse succeeds
fixtures/cvss/malformed.txt 5 invalid vectors (missing fields, bad enum values) CvssParseError per line
fixtures/epss/epss_scores_sample.csv.gz 500-row EPSS snapshot all 500 rows indexed, spot-check 10
fixtures/epss/epss_bad_header.csv.gz Wrong column order EpssIngestError
fixtures/reach/callgraph_simple.jsonl 50-node graph, 2 findings reachable, 1 not reachable flags correct
fixtures/reach/callgraph_large.jsonl 100 k nodes, 200 k edges BFS < 200 ms, no OOM
fixtures/priority/findings_mixed.json 100 findings with varying EPSS+CVSS sorted order matches reference
fixtures/priority/findings_no_epss.json Findings without EPSS order by CVSS only; no panic
Regression: re-run fixtures after CVSS formula refactor; golden output SHA256 pinned in fixtures/priority/expected_order.sha256.

6.6 Pitfalls

  1. CVSS score formula drift — CVSS v3.1 spec has non-obvious f(impact) clamping; implement exactly per spec section 7.8; missing clamp causes scores 1–2 points high → priority inflation for medium vulns. Debug: compare vs NVD NVD-JSON score field.
  2. EPSS CSV date mismatch — FIRST publishes CSV at ~09:00 UTC; syncing before publish returns 404 or stale gzip; handle 404 as transient (retry with exponential backoff, cap 3 retries, then warn + continue without EPSS). Signal: epss_sync_error_count counter.
  3. EPSS keyed on CVE-ID only — GHSA/OSV advisories without a CVE alias get no EPSS; silently falls back to CVSS-only priority. Acceptable per spec; document in output field epss_source: "none".
  4. Priority sort non-determinism — f64 NaN from 0.0 × NaN EPSS fields causes partial_cmp panic in sort; guard: priority_score computed with f32::is_nan() checks → substitute 0.0 on NaN.
  5. Call-graph purl matching — symbol's package purl may differ in version from Finding.component.purl (e.g., caller compiled against 1.0.1, advisory for 1.0.0–1.0.2); match on PackageId (ecosystem + name) only, ignoring version, to avoid false negatives. Document precision tradeoff.
  6. Large call-graph OOM — 100 k node graph + adjacency list uses ~40 MB; allocate BitVec for visited set (12.5 KB), not HashSet<usize> (800 KB+). Signal: reach_graph_nodes gauge.
  7. CVSS v2 advisory — v2 vectors lack S:/PR:/UI: fields; do not attempt v3 formula on v2; store as Cvss::V2; priority uses v2 score directly; log cvss_version=2 for transparency.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • EPSS CSV decompress + parse: 500 k rows × ~40 bytes = 20 MB gzip (~2 MB compressed); target < 3 s single-threaded; use BufReader with 64 KB buffer.
  • CVSS formula: pure arithmetic, no alloc; inline into Cvss::score() -> f32; LLVM auto-vectorizes batch scoring.
  • Priority sort: sort_unstable_by is ~10% faster than sort_by but not stable; use sort_by for determinism (stable required by scan-determinism invariant).
  • enrich_finding sled lookup: O(1) but sled page faults on cold cache; warm EPSS tree before match phase with single full scan (tree.iter().count()).
  • Reachability BFS: pre-allocate adjacency list as Vec<Vec<usize>> (arena-friendly); avoid HashMap for neighbor lookup.
  • Prioritized output serialization: stream Finding out of sorted Vec directly to writer; avoid second allocation.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Finding with no CVE alias, no CVSS — priority_score = 0.0; emitted last; not filtered.
  • CVSS v4.0 supplemental metrics — ignore E:/CR:/IR:/AR: environmental metrics; use base vector only.
  • EPSS probability = 0.0 — valid; priority = 0.0 × cvss_base = 0.0; sorted to tail correctly.
  • EPSS percentile > 1.0 — treat as data error; clamp to 1.0 + emit warn!(cve_id, raw_pct).
  • Call-graph cycle — BFS visited-set prevents infinite loop; directed cycle = normal in mutual recursion.
  • Empty call-graph JSONL — zero nodes → all findings reachable: Some(false); not an error.
  • CVSS vector string with trailing whitespace — strip before parse; do not reject.
  • Concurrent enrich + sort — enrichment is single-threaded post-match; no sync needed; document as sequential contract.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
EPSS feed 404 (FIRST outage) HTTP 404 on sync, epss_sync_error counter EPSS absent from all findings Continue scan without EPSS; emit WARN: EPSS unavailable Retry next sync window (24 h cron)
CVSS parse panic Unit test gate in CI Scan aborted for advisory Return Err(ScsError::CvssParseError) Fix regex + redeploy
Priority NaN sort panic f32::is_nan guard None (guarded) Substitute 0.0 Instrument priority_nan_count counter
Call-graph OOM RSS monitor, OOM kill Reachability pass skipped Catch OutOfMemory signal; re-run without --reachability Reduce graph or add node limit flag
BFS > 5 s timeout tokio::time::timeout wrapping BFS Findings lack reachable Emit reachable: null + warning Profile graph size; enforce 500 k node cap
Postmortem: EPSS feed staleness → check meta/epss_date vs current date; alert if delta > 48 h.

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics (via metrics crate): scs_epss_rows_ingested, scs_cvss_parse_errors_total, scs_priority_nan_total, scs_reach_graph_nodes, scs_reach_bfs_duration_ms.
  • Tracing spans: epss_sync, cvss_enrich, priority_sort, reachability_bfs — all at INFO level.
  • EPSS sync job: cron daily 10:00 UTC (after FIRST publish); idempotent; skip if meta/epss_date == today.
  • Deployment: EPSS sled DB is append-only keyed by CVE-ID; rolling update safe (new scan process opens same DB).
  • Compatibility: Finding JSON schema gains optional epss, reachable, priority_score fields — additive; old consumers ignore unknowns.
  • Rollback: disable --epss-date flag → feed not joined → findings sort by CVSS only; no schema break.

19. Phase 7 — Provenance + signature verification — Sigstore/cosign, in-toto attestations, SLSA level checks, Rekor transparency-log inclusion proof, hash/integrity pinning

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement scs-provenance to verify artifact signatures via Sigstore/cosign: fetch + validate signing certificate chains from Fulcio, verify Rekor inclusion proof (transparency log entry), validate ECDSA/Ed25519 payload signatures.
  • Parse + validate in-toto Link and ITE-6 attestation envelopes (DSSE/JWT); check predicate type = SLSA Provenance.
  • Determine SLSA Build Level (0–3) for each component based on attestation predicate fields; expose ProvenanceResult { slsa_level: u8, verified: bool, rekor_log_id: Option<String> }.
  • Verify hash/integrity pinning: for every Component with hashes: Vec<Hash>, recompute from cached artifact bytes or registry manifest and compare; fail-closed on mismatch.
  • Invariant: provenance fail-closed — verified: false on ANY verification failure; never silent success.
  • Scope: scs-provenance, scs-core (Provenance type), scs-report (SARIF + JSON), scs-cli (--verify-provenance flag). Out of scope: key management, private Fulcio/Rekor instances (future phase).

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

scs-core additions: Provenance { attestation_url: Option<Url>, slsa_level: u8, rekor_log_id: Option<String>, rekor_inclusion: bool, verified: bool, failure_reason: Option<String> }; Hash { algorithm: HashAlg, value: String } (HashAlg::{Sha256,Sha512,Sha1}); Component.provenance: Option<Provenance> populated by scs-provenance pass. scs-provenance crate structure:

scs-provenance/src/
  lib.rs          — public API: verify_component(c: &mut Component, opts: &ProvenanceOpts) -> Result<()>
  cosign.rs       — Sigstore/cosign bundle parse + ECDSA verify
  rekor.rs        — Rekor inclusion proof fetch + Merkle proof verify
  fulcio.rs       — Fulcio certificate chain validation (root CA bundle embedded)
  intoto.rs       — in-toto Link + DSSE envelope parse; predicate extract
  slsa.rs         — SLSA level determination from predicate
  hash_pin.rs     — hash recompute + compare
  cache.rs        — HTTP artifact cache (content-addressed, sled-backed)
