Synthetic graph non-determinism Benchmark results vary between runs SeedableRng seed not pinned Print seed in bench output High criterion variance (CV > 5 %) Pin seed to 42 in bench; use criterion sample_size(50)
OSV-Scanner output format change Normalization breaks after tool upgrade Tool version not pinned metadata.json version vs actual installed version mismatch All diffs fail Pin tool version in metadata.json; update fixtures on tool upgrade
Criterion HTML bloat target/criterion/ → hundreds MB Many benchmarks × many samples du -sh target/criterion/ CI artifact size alert --sample-size=10 for CI; full run nightly only
Fuzz UBSan false positive cargo fuzz reports sanitizer error in dep UBSan in transitive dep UBSAN_OPTIONS=print_stacktrace=1 Sanitizer log Suppress via UBSAN_OPTIONS=suppressions=... or report upstream
100 k-node bench OOM Process killed by OOM killer Peak RSS > container limit /usr/bin/time -v RSS output bench_resolve_100k exit non-zero Reduce sample_size; profile allocations with heaptrack
Differential fixture staleness Fixtures use old feed snapshot; new CVE added Fixtures not refreshed after feed update Diff shows SCS finds CVE not in expected FN count appears 0 but FP count rises Refresh fixtures quarterly; version-stamp feed snapshot in metadata

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • bench_resolve_100k: graph stored as petgraph::Graph (adjacency list); 100 k nodes × avg degree 5 → ~500 k edges; DijkstraMap or BFS for path finding — O(V+E) acceptable; avoid HashMap with default hasher (use rustc-hash).
  • Criterion warm-up: set warm_up_time(Duration::from_secs(3)) to amortize JIT-like effects (branch predictor, CPU cache).
  • bench_match_10k: advisory index must be pre-built before benchmark loop; benchmark only match phase, not index load.
  • Fuzz throughput: each fuzz target should hit ≥ 1 k executions/sec; slow parsers (XML/pom.xml) may hit 200 exec/sec — acceptable; profile with cargo flamegraph.
  • Memory layout in synthetic graphs: pre-allocate NodeIndex vec with known capacity; avoid Vec::push realloc in hot loop.
  • Criterion black_box: wrap all bench inputs to prevent dead-code elimination; missing black_box can produce 0-ns benchmarks.
  • Advisory index load time excluded from bench_match_10k via criterion::setup; measures only satisfiability loop.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Fuzz input = empty slice (&[]): all parsers must return Err(...), not panic.
  • Fuzz input = valid lockfile with 0 dependencies: conformance diff finds 0 findings — valid; diff still passes.
  • Differential fixture where OSV-Scanner reports GHSA and SCS reports CVE for same vuln: alias normalization must equate them — test explicitly.
  • bench_resolve_100k with max-depth DAG (chain of 100 k nodes, depth = 100 k): stack overflow risk in recursive DFS — use iterative DFS with explicit stack.
  • Criterion benchmark with sample_size(1): not allowed — minimum 10; gate script rejects.
  • Conformance fixture with withdrawn advisory: SCS suppresses (withdrawn), OSV-Scanner may still report → SCS having fewer findings is not an FN for withdrawn advisories — normalize out withdrawn.
  • Fuzz with multi-GB input: libFuzzer caps at max_len=4096 by default; set max_len=1048576 for SBOM parsers; document in fuzz target.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Fuzz crash introduced by parser change cargo fuzz CI step exits non-zero Parser crate unsafe; any caller affected Block merge; isolate crashing input Fix parser; add minimized input as regression fixture
Conformance FN regression scs-bench diff exits non-zero Advisory match correctness Block merge; compare advisory alias table Audit scs-match version-range logic; expand alias table
Benchmark regression > 20 % check_thresholds.sh exits non-zero CI red; merge blocked Investigate allocation or algorithmic regression Profile with perf/flamegraph; revert suspect change
Baseline file out of sync bench_baselines.json stale after hardware change False regressions Regenerate baseline on reference hardware scs-bench update-baseline on canonical CI runner
Criterion HTML artifact missing Artifact upload step fails No performance history Non-blocking (warn only); threshold check still runs Fix artifact upload path; target/criterion/
Fuzz corpus corruption Malformed corpus files cause fuzz to exit immediately Fuzz coverage lost Re-seed corpus from known-good inputs cargo fuzz cmin on remaining corpus; recommit

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI integration: cargo fuzz run <target> -- -max_total_time=60 per target in merge CI; 30-min nightly run via cron job.
  • Conformance update protocol: when upgrading OSV-Scanner/Grype versions, re-capture all outputs; update metadata.json version fields; review fixture diffs before committing.
  • Benchmark tracking: criterion JSON artifacts archived per commit SHA; feed into dashboard (Grafana annotation on deploy); alert on 20 % wall-clock regression or 50 % RSS regression.
  • Fuzz corpus management: fuzz/corpus/ tracked in git with git-lfs if > 10 MB total; minimize before commit (cargo fuzz cmin).
  • Differential tooling versions pinned: OSV-Scanner and Grype binaries pinned in scs-bench/tools/ or via Nix flake; never use system-installed versions in CI.
  • Sanitizer profiles: [profile.fuzz] in workspace Cargo.toml — opt-level=1, debug=true, sanitize = "address,undefined"; not inherited by release builds.
  • Rollback: conformance fixtures are read-only references; rollback = revert fixture commit; no DB migration needed.
  • Observability in bench: scs-bench disables Prometheus endpoint and audit log by default to avoid measurement interference; use --with-metrics flag explicitly.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

Boundary Direction Trust Level Enforcement
Manifest/lockfile inputs inbound UNTRUSTED size-cap, schema validation, parser timeouts
Advisory feed (OSV/NVD/GHSA) inbound TRUSTED-BUT-VERIFIED feed signature, TLS, feed-id snapshot pinning
SBOM inputs (ingest path) inbound UNTRUSTED strict schema validation, reject unknown fields
Rekor transparency log inbound TRUSTED-BUT-VERIFIED inclusion proof verification, root hash
Fulcio cert chain inbound TRUSTED-BUT-VERIFIED X.509 chain, OIDC issuer allowlist
in-toto attestations inbound UNTRUSTED DSSE envelope signature verification
Policy files inbound OPERATOR-TRUSTED TOML schema, no eval, no code execution
scs-cli stdin/env inbound OPERATOR-TRUSTED env sanitization, no shell expansion
scs-vulndb local cache internal TRUSTED HMAC integrity tag on cache files
Reporting outputs (SARIF/JSON) outbound CONSUMER-TRUSTED schema-validated before emit

Attack Surface Analysis

Malformed / Adversarial Inputs

  • Manifest/lockfile parser attacks: billion-laugh TOML, deeply-nested JSON, circular package.json bundledDependencies, polyglot files crafted to confuse parser selection.
    • Mitigation: depth-cap (≤256), byte-cap (64 MB/file), parse timeout (5 s/file), serde with deny_unknown_fields where applicable, no unsafe in parsing hot paths.
    • Detection: parse_error_total{ecosystem, kind} counter; structured log {"event":"parse_limit","file":…,"limit":"depth|size|timeout"}.
    • Recovery: skip file, emit Finding of kind ParseError, continue scan.

Dependency Graph Complexity / DoS

  • Pathological lockfiles: npm with 50k packages, cyclic resolution hints, explosive transitive fan-out.
    • Mitigation: node cap (100k), edge cap (500k), cycle detection terminates traversal, BFS/DFS with visited bitset.
    • Detection: graph_node_count, graph_cycle_count metrics; alert if >50k nodes.
    • Recovery: cap reached → emit GraphTooLarge finding; partial graph is valid for already-visited nodes.

Advisory Feed Poisoning

  • Attacker compromises OSV/NVD/GHSA upstream; injects false-negative (missing ranges) or false-positive (overly broad ranges) advisories.
    • Mitigation: feed signature verification (OSV provides GPG-signed bundles); multi-feed cross-correlation (advisory must appear in ≥2 feeds to suppress false-negative risk); feed-id pinning; anomaly detection (sudden mass-withdraw spike).
    • Detection: feed_advisory_delta{feed, kind=added|removed|modified} — alert on delta >500 removals/sync.
    • Recovery: operator rollback to prior snapshot; --feed-snapshot=<id> CLI flag selects cached snapshot.

Replay Attacks on Feeds / Provenance

  • Attacker replays stale feed to hide new vulnerabilities; replays stale Rekor entry.
    • Mitigation: feed snapshot timestamp validated against wall clock (reject if >48 h stale by default, configurable); Rekor inclusion proof includes signed timestamp; Rekor checkpoint verified against known tile roots.
    • Detection: feed_staleness_seconds gauge; alert threshold configurable (default 86400 s).

Signature Verification Bypass

  • Cosign/Rekor verification skipped due to network error → silent "verified" outcome.
    • Mitigation: PROVENANCE FAIL-CLOSED invariant: any network/cert/parse error during verification → Provenance.verified = false + Finding of kind ProvenanceUnverified; never coerce error to success.
    • Detection: provenance_verification_error_total{reason} counter; provenance_verified_ratio SLO.

Typosquatting False Negatives

  • scs-threat heuristics miss a novel typosquat variant; attacker registers lod4shell after scanner is deployed.
    • Mitigation: configurable similarity threshold; operator-supplied allowlist + denylist; external blocklist feed integration; --strict-threat flag lowers threshold.
    • Detection: threat_heuristic_score_distribution histogram; operator periodic review of near-miss scores.

Resource Exhaustion (CPU/Memory)

  • Regex catastrophic backtracking in install-script heuristics; entropy scan of large binary asset.
    • Mitigation: use regex crate (linear-time DFA guarantees); file-size cap before entropy scan (1 MB default); scan timeout per-component (2 s).
    • Detection: scan_component_duration_p99; alert >500 ms p99.

Privilege Escalation

  • scs-cli runs as CI job with elevated token; an advisory match script injection (if reports are consumed by CI eval).
    • Mitigation: scanner never executes any project code; no std::process::Command on project files; SARIF/JSON outputs are data — consumer CI must not eval them.
    • Detection: policy: operator must configure CI to consume reports as data artefacts only.

Cache Poisoning (scs-vulndb local cache)

  • Attacker with filesystem write access swaps advisory cache files to suppress findings.
    • Mitigation: HMAC-SHA256 integrity tag (key derived from operator secret or machine-id); mismatch → cache invalidated, re-fetch.
    • Detection: cache_integrity_failure_total counter; alert on any nonzero value.

Operator Mistakes

  • Overly broad waiver (waive entire CRITICAL CVE range); accidental --no-provenance in production CI.
    • Mitigation: waivers require expires field (default 90 days); CLI flags affecting gate logged to audit trail; policy linter warns on broad waivers.
    • Detection: policy_waiver_expiry_approaching_total; policy_waiver_overly_broad lint warning.

Supply Chain Risk to the Scanner Itself

  • Compromised scs-* dependency (crate poisoning in cargo ecosystem).
    • Mitigation: Cargo.lock pinned; cargo deny integrated in scs-bench CI; SLSA L3 build provenance for scs releases; binary signature verification documented for operators.
    • Detection: scs-bench differential harness detects unexpected finding changes across binary versions.

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy

Class ID Symptoms Root Causes
Parser failure FM-01 ParseError finding, 0 components from a manifest malformed file, unsupported schema version, byte/depth cap exceeded
Graph construction failure FM-02 incomplete transitive graph, missing deep dependencies lockfile-manifest mismatch, cyclic dependency in resolution, node cap exceeded
Feed sync failure FM-03 stale advisory data, FeedSyncError in logs network timeout, upstream API rate limit, TLS cert error, schema change
Version-range matching bug FM-04 known-vulnerable component not flagged (false negative) off-by-one in [introduced, fixed) bound, PEP440 pre-release comparison error, Maven range parse bug
Provenance verification failure FM-05 ProvenanceUnverified findings on valid artefacts Rekor endpoint unreachable, cert chain expiry, clock skew
Threat heuristic false positive FM-06 legitimate package flagged as typosquat/confusion name edit-distance threshold too low, common prefix collision
Policy engine misconfiguration FM-07 CI gate fails unexpectedly, or passes when it should fail TOML syntax error, waiver expired silently, severity threshold mismatch
SBOM round-trip fidelity loss FM-08 SBOM parse after emit loses relationships or hashes format version mismatch, unsupported SPDX field, CycloneDX extension drop
Cache corruption FM-09 HMAC mismatch on advisory cache, re-fetch loops filesystem error, partial write, attacker tampering
Report generation failure FM-10 no SARIF/JSON output, CI job exits 0 incorrectly serialization panic, output path permission denied
Memory exhaustion FM-11 OOM kill, scan aborted graph too large, advisory index bloom filter misconfigured, rayon thread-pool over-allocation
Clock skew FM-12 feed staleness alert fires spuriously, Rekor timestamp rejected container without NTP, CI runner clock drift

FM-01 — Parser Failure

  • Detection: parse_error_total{ecosystem} nonzero; log event parse_limit or parse_schema_mismatch.
  • Automatic recovery: skip file, emit Finding{kind=ParseError, severity=Info}, continue scan.
  • Manual recovery: operator inspects file for encoding issues; --allow-parse-errors flag continues without emit.
  • Forensic artifacts: structured log with file path, byte offset, error kind, parser version.
  • Long-term mitigation: add ecosystem-specific fuzz corpus entry; regression fixture in scs-bench.

FM-02 — Graph Construction Failure

  • Detection: graph_node_count below expected; graph_cycle_count > 0; GraphTooLarge finding present.
  • Automatic recovery: cycle → skip cycle edge, emit CyclicDependency finding; cap → truncate graph at cap, emit GraphCapExceeded.
  • Manual recovery: operator splits scan into sub-scopes (workspaces) with --scope flag.
  • Forensic: dependency path to first cycle node logged; node/edge counts in scan summary JSON.
  • Long-term: tune node cap based on fleet p99; add fixture for pathological lockfiles.

FM-03 — Feed Sync Failure

  • Detection: feed_sync_error_total{feed,reason} nonzero; feed_staleness_seconds > 86400.
  • Automatic recovery: serve from last-valid snapshot (recorded in vulndb/snapshot.json); emit FeedStale warning in report.
  • Manual recovery: scs-cli db sync --force; operator checks upstream status pages (osv.dev, nvd.nist.gov, api.github.com/advisories).
  • Forensic: snapshot id + timestamp in every scan output; last sync attempt timestamp + HTTP status in logs.
  • Long-term: add secondary mirror fallback per feed; exponential backoff with jitter on sync retries.

FM-04 — Version-Range Matching Bug

  • Detection: regression discovered in scs-bench differential harness (known CVE not found against known-vulnerable fixture); match_false_negative_total counter (populated by test oracle).
  • Automatic recovery: none — match soundness invariant violation requires code fix.
  • Manual recovery: operator adds explicit deny rule in policy as workaround until fix deployed.
  • Forensic: fixture + advisory + version triple that exposes the bug; git bisect on scs-match.
  • Long-term: expand property-based tests (proptest) on [introduced, fixed) semantics per ecosystem; add failing fixture to scs-bench.

FM-05 — Provenance Verification Failure

  • Detection: provenance_verification_error_total{reason=network|cert|clock} nonzero; ProvenanceUnverified finding rate spike.
  • Automatic recovery: fail-closed → emit ProvenanceUnverified finding; operator CI gate decision based on policy (ignore-provenance-errors flag, disabled by default).
  • Manual recovery: check Rekor endpoint (https://rekor.sigstore.dev/api/v1/log); verify system clock; re-run with --provenance-timeout=30s.
  • Forensic: Rekor log index + inclusion proof bytes logged on failure.
  • Long-term: cache last-good Rekor checkpoint; allow offline verification from cached inclusion proof.

FM-06 — Threat Heuristic False Positive

  • Detection: operator files report; threat_finding_rate{kind=typosquat} histogram spike after threshold change.
  • Automatic recovery: none (conservative: report, let policy decide).
  • Manual recovery: add package to threat.allowlist in policy TOML; --threat-allowlist CLI flag.
  • Forensic: heuristic score breakdown logged per flagged package (edit-distance, entropy, age, maintainer signals).
  • Long-term: tune thresholds via scs-bench precision/recall on labeled corpus.

FM-07 — Policy Misconfiguration

  • Detection: policy_load_error_total nonzero; CI gate unexpected pass/fail; policy linter warnings in report.
  • Automatic recovery: TOML parse error → abort scan with exit code 2 (config error, distinct from gate failure exit code 1).
  • Manual recovery: scs-cli policy lint to validate TOML; check waiver expiry dates.
  • Forensic: policy file path + parse error line number in structured log.
  • Long-term: ship JSON Schema for policy TOML; editor integration for validation.

FM-08 — SBOM Round-Trip Fidelity Loss

  • Detection: scs-bench sbom-roundtrip test fails; sbom_relationship_count pre vs post emit differ.
  • Automatic recovery: none — data fidelity is invariant; emit failure causes non-zero exit.
  • Manual recovery: inspect diff with scs-report sbom-diff; identify dropped field in scs-sbom serializer.
  • Forensic: JSON diff of pre/post SBOM in scs-bench output.

FM-09 — Cache Corruption

  • Detection: cache_integrity_failure_total nonzero; HMAC mismatch log event.
  • Automatic recovery: invalidate corrupted cache file, trigger re-fetch from upstream feed.
  • Manual recovery: scs-cli db purge && scs-cli db sync.
  • Forensic: corrupted file path + expected vs actual HMAC in log.

FM-10 — Report Generation Failure

  • Detection: report output file absent; report_write_error_total nonzero; CLI exits with code 3.
  • Automatic recovery: retry to temp path, then stdout fallback.
  • Manual recovery: check output directory permissions; disk space.
  • Forensic: errno + path in structured log.

FM-11 — Memory Exhaustion

  • Detection: OOM kill in container logs; process_resident_memory_bytes exceeds limit before kill.
  • Automatic recovery: none (process killed).
  • Manual recovery: increase container memory limit; use --scope=prod to exclude dev deps; tune SCS_GRAPH_NODE_CAP env var.
  • Forensic: last graph_node_count metric sample before OOM; heap profile if --feature=heaptrack enabled.
  • Long-term: stream-process advisory index rather than full in-memory load; tiered graph paging.

FM-12 — Clock Skew

  • Detection: feed_staleness_seconds alert fires with fresh feed; Rekor timestamp rejection log event.
  • Automatic recovery: configurable --clock-skew-tolerance=300s for Rekor timestamps.
  • Manual recovery: sync system clock; configure NTP in CI runner.
  • Forensic: logged system time vs feed timestamp vs Rekor signed timestamp in scan summary.

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

Metric Kind Labels SLO Relevance
scan_duration_seconds histogram ecosystem, scope p99 < 60 s for 10k-node graph
parse_error_total counter ecosystem, kind alert if nonzero on known-good repos
graph_node_count gauge scan_id capacity planning
graph_cycle_count counter ecosystem alert if >0 in prod repos
feed_sync_duration_seconds histogram feed p99 < 30 s
feed_staleness_seconds gauge feed alert >86400 s
feed_sync_error_total counter feed, reason alert any nonzero
advisory_count gauge feed, ecosystem anomaly: drop >10% from prior sync
finding_total counter severity, ecosystem, kind dashboard; trend alerting
match_duration_seconds histogram ecosystem p99 < 10 s
provenance_verification_error_total counter reason alert nonzero
provenance_verified_ratio gauge — SLO: >95% verified on signed repos
threat_finding_total counter kind trend
policy_waiver_expiry_approaching_total gauge — alert if >0 within 7 days
cache_integrity_failure_total counter — alert any nonzero
report_write_error_total counter — alert any nonzero
process_resident_memory_bytes gauge — capacity; OOM prediction
  • Expose via Prometheus /metrics endpoint (HTTP on configurable port, default 9090) when --metrics flag set.
  • In CI mode (no long-running process): emit OpenTelemetry OTLP spans + metrics to collector sidecar.

Tracing Strategy

  • Span hierarchy: scan → parse_manifests → per-file spans; resolve_graph; sbom_emit; vulndb_sync; match_components → per-ecosystem; provenance_verify → per-component; policy_evaluate; report_write.
  • Propagate scan_id (UUID) as span baggage and structured log field for correlation.
  • Use tracing crate with tracing-opentelemetry subscriber; emit to OTLP collector.
  • Sampling: 100% for CI (short-lived); configurable rate for daemon mode.
  • Span attributes: ecosystem, component_count, advisory_count, scan_id, feed_snapshot_id.

Structured Logging

  • Format: JSON lines (tracing-subscriber with fmt::json).
  • Mandatory fields: {"timestamp","level","target","scan_id","event",…}.
  • Log levels: ERROR = invariant violations, unrecoverable errors; WARN = recoverable anomalies (stale feed, skipped file); INFO = phase boundaries, summary counts; DEBUG = per-component decisions; TRACE = version-range arithmetic.
  • Never log: raw file contents, private keys, OIDC tokens.
  • Log sink: stderr (default); file with rotation via --log-file; OTLP log exporter optional.

Dashboard Design

  • Scan Overview: scan_duration p50/p95/p99; finding_total by severity; parse_error rate.
  • Feed Health: feed_staleness_seconds per feed; advisory_count delta; sync error rate.
  • Security Signal: finding_total trend (7d); CRITICAL/HIGH finding rate; provenance_verified_ratio.
  • Threat Signals: threat_finding_total by kind; near-miss score distribution.
  • Capacity: graph_node_count p99; process_resident_memory_bytes; scan_duration vs node_count scatter.
  • Policy Health: waiver_expiry_approaching; policy_load_error rate.

Alerting Philosophy

  • Alert on actionable conditions only; no alert without runbook.
  • Severity tiers: P1 (data integrity broken: cache_integrity_failure, match false-negative oracle); P2 (security signal degraded: feed_staleness >86400 s, provenance_verification_error spike); P3 (operational warning: waiver expiry, scan_duration p99 regression).
  • Alert routing: P1 → pagerduty; P2 → slack#security-ops; P3 → slack#infra-ops.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window
Scan completion (10k-node graph) p99 < 60 s 30-day rolling
Feed freshness (all feeds) staleness < 24 h 30-day rolling, 99.5% of time
Provenance verification success (signed artefacts) >95% 30-day rolling
Advisory match soundness 0 false negatives on fixture corpus per-release gate
SBOM round-trip fidelity 100% on fixture corpus per-release gate
Report write success >99.9% 30-day rolling

Incident Response

  1. Alert fires → on-call acknowledges within SLA.
  2. Check dashboard: scan_duration spike → graph size? feed staleness → upstream status?
  3. For feed sync failure: scs-cli db status → last snapshot id + staleness; if stale, scs-cli db sync --force.
  4. For cache integrity failure: scs-cli db purge; check filesystem for external writes; escalate to security if tampering suspected.
  5. For match false-negative (oracle fires): immediate P1; freeze feed at current snapshot; open incident; patch scs-match; re-run scs-bench.
  6. Post-incident: add fixture capturing the failure; update runbook.

Runbooks

  • RB-01 Feed Sync Failure: check upstream status → retry → failover to mirror → serve stale with warning.
  • RB-02 Cache Integrity Failure: purge → re-sync → if recurring, audit filesystem ACLs; escalate if tampering.
  • RB-03 Scan OOM: reduce scope (--scope=prod); increase container memory; profile heap.
  • RB-04 CI Gate Unexpected Pass: check policy TOML for expired waivers; re-run with --explain-gate; verify feed freshness.
  • RB-05 Provenance Failures Spike: check Rekor endpoint; verify system clock; check Fulcio cert chain expiry.

Backup and Restore

  • Advisory cache (vulndb/) is reconstructible from upstream feeds; backup optional but recommended for air-gapped deployments.
  • Backup: nightly snapshot of vulndb/ directory to object storage; retain 30 days.
  • Restore test: monthly automated restore drill; scs-cli db verify confirms HMAC integrity after restore.
  • Policy files: version-controlled in operator repo; no independent backup needed.

Deployment Topology

[CI Runner / Developer Workstation]
  scs-cli (binary) ---> [local vulndb cache] ---> [OSV/NVD/GHSA upstream (sync)]
                    ---> [Rekor/Fulcio (provenance, online)]
                    ---> [policy TOML (version-controlled)]
                    ---> [SARIF/JSON output] ---> [CI artefact store]

[Daemon Mode (optional, air-gapped)]
  scs-vulndb-sync (cron) ---> [object store mirror]
  scs-cli (offline mode) ---> [local mirror only]
  • No persistent server required; scanner is a stateless CLI binary.
  • Daemon mode (optional): long-running process with HTTP API for IDE/editor integration.
  • Air-gapped: scs-cli db sync --mirror=<internal-url>; disable Rekor online checks with --offline-provenance.

Canary and Rolling-Upgrade Strategy

  • Release binary via CI pipeline with SLSA L3 provenance.
  • Canary: deploy new binary to 5% of CI runners; compare finding counts (delta) against prior binary on same repos; promote if delta within expected bounds (new advisories only).
  • Rolling upgrade: update binary in CI image; no migration step needed (cache format versioned; auto-upgrade on first run).
  • Rollback: pin CI image to prior binary version; cache format backward-compatible (old binary reads new cache with unknown fields ignored).

Migration Strategy

  • Advisory cache schema version embedded in vulndb/snapshot.json ("schema_version": N).
  • On binary upgrade: if cache schema version < current → automated migration on first db sync or db upgrade subcommand; old schema backed up before migration.
  • If migration fails: cache purged, re-sync from upstream.

Compatibility & Versioning

Semantic Versioning Policy

  • scs-cli binary and scs-* crates follow SemVer 2.0.
  • Public API surface: scs-core types, scs-cli CLI flags + exit codes, report output schemas (JSON/SARIF), policy TOML schema, advisory cache schema, SBOM emit format.
  • MAJOR bump: breaking change to any public API surface (CLI flag removal, JSON field rename, exit code semantics change, policy TOML key removal).
  • MINOR bump: additive change (new CLI flag, new JSON field, new ecosystem support, new advisory feed, new Finding kind).
  • PATCH bump: bug fix, performance improvement, no API change.
  • Pre-release (-alpha, -beta, -rc): no compatibility guarantees; not for production use.

CLI Compatibility

Surface Guarantee Deprecation Period
CLI flag names + short aliases stable across MINOR/PATCH 2 MAJOR versions
Exit codes (0=pass, 1=gate-fail, 2=config-error, 3=io-error) stable across all versions never removed
--output-format values (json, sarif, table) stable; new formats additive 2 MAJOR versions
Env var names (SCS_*) stable across MINOR/PATCH 2 MAJOR versions
Subcommand names stable; deprecation warning before removal 1 MAJOR version

JSON Report Schema Compatibility

  • Schema identified by "schema_version": N field in root of JSON output.
  • Additive fields: always; consumers MUST ignore unknown fields.
  • Renamed/removed fields: MAJOR bump only; prior schema version supported for 1 MAJOR release with deprecation warning.
  • SARIF output: tracks SARIF 2.1.0 spec; producer version in runs[].tool.driver.version.
  • JSON Schema published at https://scs.example/schemas/report/vN.json; versioned and immutable.

Advisory Cache (vulndb) Schema

Version Guaranteed Support Migration
v1 (current) indefinitely —
v2 (future) v1 readable by v2 binary auto-migrate on first sync
v3+ prior N-1 readable explicit db upgrade subcommand
  • Cache is local, operator-controlled; no cross-version wire protocol.
  • HMAC key is cache-version–aware; re-keying on schema migration.

Policy TOML Schema

  • Additive fields: always; unknown fields produce lint warning (not error) in MINOR versions; error in MAJOR.
  • Removed fields: deprecated for 1 MAJOR version (warning on load) before removal.
  • JSON Schema for policy TOML published alongside binary; scs-cli policy lint validates against bundled schema.
  • Field renames: never without MAJOR bump; old name accepted with deprecation for 1 MAJOR.

SBOM Output Compatibility

  • SPDX: emit tracks SPDX 2.3 (current); SPDX 3.0 support added as MINOR; old format emittable via --sbom-spdx-version=2.3.
  • CycloneDX: emit tracks CycloneDX 1.5; 1.6 additive; --sbom-cdx-version flag selects version.
  • SBOM ingest: accept SPDX 2.2, 2.3, 3.0-subset; CycloneDX 1.4, 1.5, 1.6; unknown fields silently ignored.
  • Round-trip: emit-then-parse preserves all components + relationships (SBOM round-trip invariant); format-version downgrade MAY drop extension fields with warning.

Wire Compatibility (Daemon / API Mode)

  • HTTP API (optional daemon mode): versioned at /v1/, /v2/ path prefix.
  • /v1/ endpoints frozen once released; no breaking changes within /v1/.
  • New capabilities added under /v2/; /v1/ supported for 1 MAJOR release after /v2/ GA.
  • OpenAPI spec published and versioned; clients MUST accept unknown fields in responses.
  • gRPC (future, MINOR): proto definitions use reserved for removed fields; proto3 unknown field passthrough.

Deprecation Policy

  • Deprecation announced in CHANGELOG + --help deprecation notice.
  • Minimum deprecation period: 1 MAJOR release cycle (expected ≥6 months).
  • Deprecated items: CLI flags produce [DEPRECATED] warning on use; JSON fields remain in output but marked "deprecated":true in schema.
  • Security-motivated removals: may skip deprecation period with advisory notice.

Rolling-Upgrade Compatibility

  • New binary reads old advisory cache (schema N-1) without re-sync; advisory coverage unchanged.
  • Old binary reads new advisory cache: unknown fields ignored; no regression in coverage (additive-only schema changes within MINOR).
  • Policy TOML written for MINOR N readable by MINOR N+1 without modification.
  • Mixed binary versions in CI fleet (during rolling upgrade): each binary uses its own local cache; no shared mutable state between runner instances; no coordination needed.
  • Downgrade (rollback): supported for 1 MINOR version; old binary ignores unknown cache fields; alert if cache schema_version > supported (prompt db upgrade or db purge).

Testing & Verification Strategy

Unit Tests — Per-Crate Invariant Coverage

Crate Focus Key Cases
scs-core type invariants Purl canonicalization, VersionReq parse roundtrip, CVSS score bounds [0.0,10.0]
scs-manifest parser correctness malformed JSON/TOML/YAML, missing fields, path traversal in names, empty manifests
scs-resolve graph invariants cycle detection, dedup, scope propagation, diamond deps, empty roots
scs-sbom round-trip fidelity SPDX→parse→emit==SPDX, CycloneDX idem, hash presence, relationship count
scs-vulndb index correctness AffectedRange half-open semantics, withdrawn advisory filtering, alias dedup
scs-match range satisfiability exact boundary versions, pre-release handling per ecosystem, withdrawn/disputed
scs-provenance fail-closed tampered bundle rejected, expired cert rejected, missing inclusion proof = fail
scs-threat signal scoring known typosquats detected, confusion threshold boundary, script entropy thresholds
scs-policy gate logic severity threshold pass/fail, VEX override, waiver expiry, license allowlist
scs-report output contract SARIF schema valid, exit codes match gate, diff shows added/removed comps
  • Every public fn: at minimum one happy-path + one adversarial-input unit test.
  • AffectedRange boundary invariant: introduced <= v < fixed tested at introduced, introduced+1, fixed-1, fixed, fixed+1 for each ecosystem version type.
  • VersionReq: property-based tests confirm parse(v.to_string()) == v for all generated Version values.

Integration Tests — Cross-Crate Pipelines

  • Fixture set: real-world lockfiles (package-lock.json, Cargo.lock, go.sum, pom.xml, requirements.txt, Gemfile.lock) committed under scs-bench/fixtures/.
  • Pipeline smoke test: manifest → resolve → sbom emit → vulndb lookup → match → policy → report for each ecosystem.
  • Advisory feed integration: mock OSV/NVD server (wiremock-rs) replayed from recorded JSON snapshots; tests are deterministic.
  • SBOM round-trip integration: emit SPDX/CycloneDX → re-ingest → assert component + relationship equality (invariant: SBOM round-trip).
  • Cross-ecosystem purl aliasing: npm + yarn resolve same purl, confirmed by graph dedup test.
  • CI fixture refresh: nightly job updates recorded advisory snapshots; diff-checked against prior run to catch silent schema changes.

Differential Testing

  • OSV-Scanner / Grype as oracles: run both tools on same fixture, compare Finding sets; discrepancies flagged for triage (not auto-fail; ecosystems differ).
  • Version-range differential: for SemVer, cross-check scs-match decisions against semver crate's matches(); for PEP 440, against packaging (Python subprocess); for Maven, against maven-version-range reference.
  • SBOM differential: Syft output on same fixture vs scs-sbom emit; component inventory overlap ≥ 90% required (known gaps documented).

Property-Based Testing (proptest / quickcheck)

  • Purl: generate arbitrary ecosystem/name/version strings → parse → serialize → re-parse == original.
  • DependencyGraph: generated random DAGs → cycle-detection always terminates; topological sort stable.
  • AffectedRange: generated (introduced, fixed, version) triples → match result consistent with manual half-open check.
  • Version ordering: generated (a, b) pairs → a < b iff b > a; transitivity holds across 10k pairs.
  • Policy: generated Finding sets → gate(pass) ∪ gate(fail) == all findings; no finding silently dropped.

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libFuzzer)

Fuzz Target Input Corpus Seed
fuzz_manifest_npm raw package-lock.json bytes real npm lockfiles
fuzz_manifest_cargo raw Cargo.lock bytes crates.io snapshots
fuzz_sbom_spdx SPDX tag-value + JSON bytes SPDX test suite
fuzz_sbom_cyclonedx CycloneDX JSON/XML bytes CycloneDX samples
fuzz_advisory_osv OSV JSON bytes OSV schema examples
fuzz_version_semver semver string bytes semver corpus
fuzz_version_pep440 PEP 440 string bytes PyPI version corpus
fuzz_provenance_bundle Sigstore bundle bytes cosign test vectors
  • CI: each fuzz target runs 60s on PR merge; nightly runs 8h; corpus committed to repo.
  • Crash triage: any panic/OOM/timeout → P0 bug; ScsError return from bad input is expected behavior.
  • Sanitizers: AddressSanitizer + UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer on fuzz builds.

Chaos and Fault Injection

  • Advisory feed unreachable: scs-vulndb returns ScsError::FeedUnavailable; scan aborts with explicit error (not silently stale).
  • Partial feed corruption: truncated JSON mid-advisory → parse error surfaced, prior valid advisories still indexed.
  • Clock skew: Rekor inclusion proof timestamp ±5 min → accepted; ±2h → rejected (fail-closed).
  • Hash mismatch injection: modify one byte of a Component hash → scs-provenance reports HashMismatch, not Verified.
  • Cycle injection: hand-crafted lockfile with circular dep reference → scs-resolve detects, reports CycleDetected, partial graph returned with error.

Deterministic Replay and Soak

  • Replay harness (scs-bench): capture (manifests + advisory snapshot) → scan.replay file; re-run 1000× → identical Finding set (scan determinism invariant).
  • Soak: 72h continuous scan loop over 50 fixture projects; measure memory growth, fd leaks, advisory index fragmentation.
  • Regression baseline: golden Finding JSON per fixture; any new/removed finding fails CI (must be explicitly updated with rationale).

Conformance and Compatibility

  • SPDX: validate emitted documents against spdx-tools-java validator (invoked via subprocess in CI).
  • CycloneDX: validate against CycloneDX schema v1.5 JSON Schema.
  • SARIF: validate against SARIF 2.1.0 JSON Schema.
  • OSV schema: validate ingested + normalized advisories against OSV JSON schema v1.
  • Sigstore: verify against cosign's own test vectors; ensure bundle format v0.2 + v0.3 both accepted.
  • SLSA provenance: test SLSA level 0/1/2/3 attestations from slsa-verifier test corpus.

Performance Regression

  • Criterion benchmarks for: manifest parse (per ecosystem), graph construction (1k/10k/100k nodes), advisory index lookup (single purl), full pipeline on reference monorepo.
  • PR gates: benchmark must not regress > 10% vs main; measured by criterion compare; results posted as PR comment.
  • Flamegraph diff: generated on perf-regression detection; attached to PR for triage.

Build & Release Engineering

Reproducible Builds

  • Cargo.lock committed; --locked flag enforced in all CI and release builds.
  • Source timestamps stripped: CARGO_SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set to last commit timestamp.
  • Deterministic artifact: same source + toolchain + Cargo.lock → byte-identical binary (verified with sha256sum across two independent CI runners).
  • Toolchain pinned: rust-toolchain.toml specifies channel (stable), version, and components (clippy, rustfmt, llvm-tools-preview).
  • Cross-compilation targets: x86_64-unknown-linux-musl (static), aarch64-unknown-linux-musl, x86_64-apple-darwin, aarch64-apple-darwin, x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.
  • Docker build image: pinned digest (FROM rust:1.78-slim@sha256:...); no latest tags.

Artifact Signing and Binary Provenance

  • Release binaries signed with cosign keyless (OIDC → Sigstore/Fulcio); signature + Rekor entry published alongside artifact.
  • Checksum file (SHA256SUMS) signed separately; consumers verify before extraction.
  • SLSA provenance: GitHub Actions generates SLSA level 3 provenance via slsa-github-generator; .intoto.jsonl attestation published to release.
  • Attestation verification: cosign verify-attestation --type slsa documented in RELEASING.md; CI re-verifies after publish.
  • Cargo publish: cargo publish --locked --no-verify (workspace crates published in dependency order); crate checksums recorded.

SBOM Generation for the Scanner Itself

  • cargo cyclonedx / cargo spdx run as release step; SBOM committed to release assets.
  • Bootstrap: scs-cli scans itself as a post-release validation step; any finding against its own deps triggers alert.
  • Dependency audit: cargo audit + cargo deny (license + ban + advisory) run in CI on every PR; block on any CVSS ≥ 7.0 unwaivered finding.

CI/CD Topology

  • PR open/push
    • ├── lint: cargo fmt --check, cargo clippy --deny warnings
    • ├── test: cargo test --locked --workspace (unit + integration)
    • ├── fuzz: cargo fuzz run <target> -- -max_total_time=60 (each target)
    • ├── bench: criterion compare vs main; post regression comment
    • ├── audit: cargo audit, cargo deny check
    • └── schema: SPDX / CycloneDX / SARIF / OSV validation
  • merge to main
    • ├── all above
    • ├── nightly fuzz (8h, corpus update PR if new coverage)
    • ├── differential vs Grype/OSV-Scanner oracles
    • └── soak (72h, scheduled weekly)
  • release tag vX.Y.Z
    • ├── reproducible build (two runners, sha256 compare)
    • ├── cross-compile matrix (5 targets)
    • ├── SBOM emit (CycloneDX + SPDX)
    • ├── artifact sign (cosign keyless + Rekor)
    • ├── SLSA provenance attestation
    • ├── cargo publish (workspace crates, dependency order)
    • └── bootstrap self-scan

Version Stamping

  • Version: CARGO_PKG_VERSION from Cargo.toml; git SHA + dirty flag embedded at compile time via build.rs → SCS_BUILD_SHA.
  • Scan output: every report includes scanner_version, build_sha, advisory_snapshot_id, advisory_snapshot_ts (feed freshness invariant).
  • Semver: workspace crates share a version; workspace Cargo.toml sets version; all member crates inherit via workspace = true.

Release Channels and Cadence

Channel Trigger Artifact Stability
nightly push to main musl static binary unstable
rc tag vX.Y.Z-rc.N all targets + SBOM pre-release
stable tag vX.Y.Z all targets + SBOM + provenance stable
patch tag vX.Y.Z-patch CVE-response fast track stable
  • LTS: latest stable + one prior minor; backport security fixes only.
  • Release automation: release-plz for changelog generation and version bumping PRs.

Supply-Chain Verification for Releases

  • cargo vet: third-party crates vetted by maintainers; supply-chain/audits.toml committed.
  • cargo deny: license allowlist (MIT/Apache-2.0/ISC/BSD); deny GPL-3.0 in non-CLI crates.
  • MSRV gate: cargo check --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu with Rust 1.78 on every PR.
  • Checksum pinning: all [patch] or git-source deps forbidden in release builds.

Performance Engineering

Performance Target Register

Derived from §4.1 Constraints Table and the Throughput/Tail-Latency targets below; each target names the phase whose Acceptance Criteria/bench gates it.

ID Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 Manifest parse throughput ≥ 50k components/s Phase 1
M2 npm lockfile parse (10k deps) p99 < 150 ms Phase 1
M3 Cargo.lock parse (5k deps) p99 < 60 ms Phase 1
M4 Full graph resolve (10k nodes) p99 < 1.5 s Phase 2
M5 Advisory index load (500k entries) p99 < 4 s Phase 4
M6 Feed sync latency, OSV full sync ≤ 5 min target / 15 min hard Phase 4
M7 Single purl advisory lookup p99 < 5 µs Phase 5
M8 Match throughput ≥ 50k component-advisory pairs/s Phase 5
M9 Sigstore bundle verify (one cert chain) p99 < 500 ms Phase 7
M10 Rekor inclusion-proof RTT p99 ≤ 2 s Phase 7
M11 Full pipeline scan (10k nodes, 500k advisories) p99 < 30 s Phase 13
M12 Peak RSS, 10k-node scan + 500k advisory index ≤ 1 GB hard limit Phase 11
M13 File descriptors per scan ≤ 128 target / 512 hard Phase 11
M14 Feed freshness staleness ≤ 24 h target / 72 h warn Phase 4

Benchmarking Strategy

  • Framework: criterion (statistical, outlier-resistant); benchmarks in scs-bench/benches/.
  • Benchmark hierarchy:
Level Scope Input Scale
micro single fn (parse, version cmp, range check) single manifest / advisory
unit single crate pipeline 100-node graph, 1k advisories
system full scan pipeline 10k-node monorepo, 500k advisories
stress adversarial 500k-node graph, 5M advisory entries
  • Fixture manifests: checked into scs-bench/fixtures/; never generated at bench time (determinism).
  • Throughput targets: manifest parse ≥ 50k components/s; advisory index lookup ≤ 5µs p99; full monorepo scan (10k nodes, 500k advisories) ≤ 30s wall on 8-core.
  • Memory target: peak RSS ≤ 1 GB for 10k-node scan with 500k advisory index loaded.

Profiling Methodology

  • CPU profiling: cargo flamegraph (perf on Linux, DTrace on macOS); flamegraph diff between main and branch on regression.
  • Instruction-level: cargo instruments -t "CPU Counters" (macOS); perf stat -e cycles,cache-misses,branch-misses (Linux).
  • Allocation profiling: heaptrack on Linux; dhat (DHAT allocator, dhat-heap.json → dhat-viewer).
  • Lock contention: mutrace (Linux); detect contended Mutex<_> in scs-vulndb index and scs-resolve graph builders.
  • Syscall overhead: strace -c / dtruss; target: < 1k syscalls per scan of a 1k-node project.

Flamegraph Workflow

1. cargo flamegraph --bench pipeline_bench -- --bench
2. Open flamegraph.svg; identify hot frames (> 5% self time)
3. Annotate: serde_json::from_str → manifest parse hot; hashbrown lookup → advisory index
4. Apply optimization (e.g., arena alloc, interning, simd parse)
5. Re-bench with criterion; confirm ≥ 10% improvement before merging
6. Store before/after SVGs as PR artifacts

Tracing Methodology

  • tracing crate with tracing-subscriber; structured spans per crate phase.
  • Span hierarchy: scan → manifest_parse → resolve → vulndb_lookup → match → provenance → threat → policy → report.
  • CI trace export: RUST_LOG=scs=trace on integration tests; spans exported to file; p99 per span checked against budget.
  • Distributed context: tracing-opentelemetry for future remote-scan mode; trace IDs propagated in report JSON.

Allocator and Memory Analysis

  • Default allocator: system; jemalloc optional feature (jemallocator) for servers/daemons.
  • Arena allocation: scs-resolve uses typed-arena for graph nodes; no per-node heap alloc during traversal.
  • String interning: Purl and package names interned via string-interner in hot paths (advisory index, graph edges).
  • Component storage: Vec<Component> pre-allocated with capacity hint from manifest file size estimate.
  • Advisory index: HashMap<Purl, Vec<Advisory>> → measured for rehash events; target: ≤ 1 rehash per 500k advisory load.

Cache and Syscall Analysis

  • File I/O: manifest files read once into Bytes (zero-copy parse where possible via nom/winnow).
  • Advisory feed: loaded from local SQLite (scs-vulndb); page cache warm = 0 disk reads on repeated scans.
  • Parallel parsing: rayon parallel iterator over manifest files; cache-line false sharing avoided by per-thread accumulator.
  • mmap: considered for large advisory SQLite DB; rejected (portability + SIGBUS risk on sparse files).
  • File descriptor budget: open fds measured per scan; target ≤ 64 concurrent fds regardless of project size.

Throughput and Tail-Latency Targets

Operation Target (p50) Target (p99) Measurement
npm lockfile parse (10k deps) < 50ms < 150ms criterion
Cargo.lock parse (5k deps) < 20ms < 60ms criterion
Advisory index load (500k entries) < 2s < 4s criterion
Single purl advisory lookup < 2µs < 5µs criterion
Full graph resolve (10k nodes) < 500ms < 1.5s criterion
Full pipeline (10k nodes, 500k advisories) < 20s < 30s integration bench
Sigstore bundle verify (one cert chain) < 200ms < 500ms criterion
  • Tail-latency analysis: criterion reports p50/p95/p99/p999; p99 > 2× p50 triggers investigation.
  • Outlier detection: criterion's built-in outlier detection; > 5% outliers logged as warning.

Scalability Analysis

Bottleneck Identification

Bottleneck Location Symptom Mitigation
Advisory index memory scs-vulndb RSS spikes > 2 GB on 5M advisories pagination + SQLite on-disk index
Graph traversal CPU scs-resolve O(V+E) DFS × advisory lookups = O(V·A) precompute purl→advisory map; batch lookup
Manifest parse I/O scs-manifest sequential file reads on large monorepos rayon parallel parse + tokio::fs async read
Sigstore network RTT scs-provenance Rekor TLS handshake per component batch inclusion-proof queries; local CT log cache
SBOM serialization scs-sbom serde_json/XML write for 100k-component SBOM streaming serializer (serde-json-stream)
SQLite WAL contention scs-vulndb concurrent feed sync + scan reads WAL mode + reader pool; write serialized

Scaling Ceilings (Single Node)

  • Advisory index: ≤ 10M entries in RAM (≈ 8 GB); beyond → SQLite on-disk with LRU page cache.
  • Dependency graph: ≤ 500k nodes comfortably; > 1M nodes requires streaming DFS + disk-backed edge store.
  • Component inventory: ≤ 1M components per SBOM emit before JSON serialization OOMs; mitigation: streaming NDJSON.
  • Concurrent scans: rayon global thread pool = num_cpus threads; memory per scan ≈ 200 MB → max 40 concurrent on 8 GB node.
  • Feed sync: OSV full corpus ≈ 500 MB JSON; NVD JSON feeds ≈ 2 GB; bandwidth + parse dominated; recommend nightly off-peak.

Horizontal Scaling

  • Scan workers: stateless (read-only advisory DB replica + read-only manifest inputs) → scale-out trivially.
  • Advisory DB: read replicas (SQLite ATTACH or PostgreSQL read replicas in server mode); write (feed sync) → single primary.
  • Sharding by ecosystem: npm advisory index shard vs Cargo vs PyPI; lookup routing by Purl.ecosystem prefix.
  • Result aggregation: per-project Finding sets independent; no cross-project coordination needed for gating.
  • CI integration: ephemeral scanner pods (Kubernetes Job per PR); no persistent state except advisory DB volume.

Vertical Scaling

  • RAM: advisory index fits in 8 GB (500k entries); 64 GB node hosts 5M entry index in RAM with headroom.
  • CPU: rayon utilizes all cores for manifest parse + graph traversal; 32-core = near-linear speedup up to I/O bound.
  • NVMe: advisory DB on NVMe → SQLite random reads ≤ 0.1ms; spinning disk degrades advisory lookup 50×.
  • NUMA: large index partitioned per NUMA node; jemallocator NUMA-aware arenas reduce cross-node traffic.

Storage Growth

Dataset Growth Rate Retention Mitigation
OSV advisory DB ~200 new advisories/day indefinite (append-only) soft-delete withdrawn; compact annually
NVD CVE feed ~50 CVE/day indefinite delta sync (modified_start_index)
GHSA feed ~100/day indefinite GraphQL paginated delta
Scan result cache per PR scan ≈ 500 KB JSON 90 days TTL eviction
Rekor CT log cache ~1M entries/day (global) project inclusion proofs only store only verified component proofs
SBOM artifacts per release ≈ 2 MB version lifetime object storage + lifecycle policy

Network Fanout

  • Feed sync: OSV (REST/GCS bucket), NVD (REST), GHSA (GraphQL) → 3 upstream connections; all idempotent; retry with exponential backoff + jitter.
  • Sigstore: Rekor inclusion proof query per signed component → batch API where available (/api/v1/log/entries/retrieve); max 100 per request.
  • Fulcio OIDC: one TLS session per signing event; connection pooled (reqwest with hyper keep-alive).
  • Registry metadata (typosquatting): npm registry / API, PyPI JSON API, crates.io API → rate-limited; respect Retry-After headers.
  • Proxy support: HTTP_PROXY / HTTPS_PROXY / NO_PROXY honored via reqwest defaults; required for air-gapped CI.

Coordination Overhead

  • No distributed consensus needed for scan (read-only); advisory DB sync uses optimistic locking (etag / If-None-Match).
  • Feed sync leader election: file lock (advisory.db-sync.lock) sufficient for single-node; Kubernetes: lease object.
  • Cache invalidation: advisory snapshot ID embedded in scan; stale cache detected by snapshot_id mismatch → re-run.
  • Finding deduplication: within a single scan, global HashSet<(Purl, AdvisoryId)> prevents duplicate Findings from diamond deps.

Scheduler Saturation

  • rayon thread pool saturates at num_cpus; CPU-bound manifest parse + graph traversal benefit fully.
  • tokio runtime (network I/O in scs-provenance, scs-vulndb sync): dedicated 4-thread runtime; separate from rayon pool.
  • Priority: provenance verification (network-bound) offloaded to tokio; graph ops (CPU-bound) stay on rayon; no cross-pool blocking.
  • Backpressure: advisory feed sync respects OS TCP send buffer; streaming JSON parse (serde-json async) avoids full-file buffering for large NVD feeds.

Metadata Scaling

  • Purl interning table: grows linearly with unique packages seen; ≤ 1M unique purls in a global enterprise scan → ≤ 100 MB interned strings.
  • Advisory alias map (CVE↔GHSA↔OSV ID): O(aliases) ≈ 3× advisory count; 5M advisories → 15M alias entries ≈ 600 MB; partitioned by ecosystem.
  • Graph edge list: average fan-out 5 deps/node; 500k-node graph → 2.5M edges → ≈ 120 MB in Vec<(u32,u32)> representation.
  • Version comparison cache: memoize Version::satisfies(req) for repeated (version, req) pairs; LRU cap 100k entries → 8 MB.

Mental Models & First Principles

The compressed insights an expert in supply-chain security holds. Each predicts a bug class. Every cited § resolves to a real heading.

MM1. A purl is the join key of the entire system. Package identity is pkg:type/namespace/name@version; every correlation (manifest ↔ advisory ↔ SBOM ↔ provenance) joins on it. Predicts: correct cross-database (OSV/NVD/GHSA) matching. Bug without it: matching on bare name → flagging the npm lodash advisory against the PyPI lodash, a wrong-ecosystem false positive. Applied: §8 (purl model), §14 (purl generation), §17 (CVE↔GHSA↔OSV correlation).

MM2. Vulnerability matching is version-range satisfiability, not string equality. "Is version V inside range R?" must be evaluated under the ecosystem's own ordering (SemVer, PEP 440, Maven). Predicts: correct match across npm ^/~, PEP 440 epochs, Maven ranges. Bug without it: lexical comparison says 1.10.0 < 1.9.0 and misses a vulnerable version. Applied: §17 (range satisfiability), ADR-009/ADR-010, §8 domain.

MM3. A lockfile is truth; a manifest is intent. When a lockfile exists, scan the resolved versions, not the declared ranges. Predicts: accurate findings for what actually ships. Bug without it: scanning the declared ^1.0.0 range either over-reports (every matching CVE) or under-reports (misses the resolved 1.2.3). Applied: §13 (lockfile reconciliation), §14 (dependency resolution), MM1.

MM4. Soundness beats completeness: zero false negatives for in-range versions is the hard invariant. A false positive wastes triage time; a false negative ships a vulnerability. Predicts: the property-based matching tests (§4.1 "Match soundness"). Bug without it: tuning the matcher to suppress noisy alerts and silently dropping a real in-range hit. Applied: §4.1 (match soundness), §17 (matching engine), §4.4 failure budget.

MM5. Reachability is the precision lever, not the detection gate. A vulnerable symbol present in the graph is not the same as called; reachability re-ranks, it must not suppress by default (MM4). Predicts: lower noise without sacrificing soundness. Bug without it: using reachability as a hard filter and dropping a finding whose call path the analysis missed. Applied: §18 (severity + reachability), Alternative Architectures, MM4.

MM6. Provenance is a chain you verify, not a flag you trust. Signature (Sigstore) + transparency-log inclusion (Rekor) + attestation (in-toto/DSSE) + SLSA level — each link checked. Predicts: resistance to forged or self-asserted attestations. Bug without it: trusting an attestation's claims without verifying its Rekor inclusion proof → accepting a fabricated provenance. Applied: §19 (provenance + signature verification), ADR-012/ADR-013.

MM7. Name-based attacks need a popularity corpus. Typosquatting is edit-distance against popular names; dependency confusion is internal/public namespace ambiguity. Predicts: detection requires the top-N package list as ground truth. Bug without it: flagging every new low-download package as a typosquat → false-positive flood. Applied: §20 (malicious-package detection), §4.2 (aho-corasick), Capacity model.

MM8. The advisory feed is a cache with a freshness SLA. A stale feed produces silent false negatives — the worst failure mode. Predicts: the need to surface StaleFeed as a first-class finding (§4.1). Bug without it: scanning against a 2-week-old feed and reporting "clean" while CVEs published yesterday go unseen. Applied: §16 (advisory ingestion), §4.1 (feed freshness SLA), §4.4.

MM9. Determinism is a feature, not a nicety. Identical inputs + identical advisory snapshot must yield bit-identical output (no timestamps, no random ordering). Predicts: clean CI diffs, stable baselines, delta scans. Bug without it: nondeterministic finding order makes every CI run a noisy diff and breaks baseline suppression. Applied: §4.1 (determinism), §22 (reporting + baseline), §23 (incremental/caching).

MM10. The SBOM is the inventory contract; conversion must be lossless. SPDX ↔ CycloneDX round-trips must preserve every component and relationship. Predicts: the 100% round-trip fidelity invariant (§4.1). Bug without it: a lossy SPDX→CycloneDX conversion drops a transitive component and a downstream scan misses it. Applied: §15 (SBOM generation/ingestion), ADR-029, §4.1 fidelity invariant.

Comparative Architecture Table

This design (multi-ecosystem resolution + purl-keyed range matching + provenance verification + malicious-package heuristics, as a deterministic CLI) versus four real supply-chain scanners. Real systems only.

Dimension This blueprint (scs) Trivy (Aqua) Grype (Anchore) OSV-Scanner (Google) Snyk
Primary focus app deps + provenance + malicious containers + IaC + app deps containers + app deps app deps (OSV-native) app deps + container + IaC
Advisory source OSV + NVD + GHSA (merged) own DB (aggregates many) own DB (Anchore feed) OSV.dev only proprietary Snyk DB
Identity / match key purl + range satisfiability purl + own matcher purl + own matcher purl + OSV ranges proprietary graph
Provenance verification Sigstore + Rekor + in-toto + SLSA limited none none limited
Malicious-package detection typosquat + dep-confusion + scripts none (CVE-focused) none none some (Snyk Advisor)
Deployment offline-capable CLI/CI CLI + server CLI CLI SaaS + CLI
Determinism bit-identical (snapshot-pinned) feed-dependent feed-dependent feed-dependent server-side
SBOM SPDX + CycloneDX emit/ingest SPDX + CycloneDX SPDX + CycloneDX (Syft) consumes SBOM CycloneDX

Trivy is a breadth-first scanner spanning containers, IaC, Kubernetes, and app dependencies with an aggregated database; it wins when one tool must cover the whole cloud-native surface. This blueprint trades that breadth for depth in app-dependency provenance and malicious-package detection, and for deterministic, offline, snapshot-pinned output (container layer scanning is deferred — see Deferred Features Register).

Grype (with Syft for SBOM) is a focused, fast container + app-dependency CVE matcher using Anchore's feed; it wins for straightforward "what CVEs are in this image" pipelines. We add provenance verification (MM6) and malicious-package heuristics (MM7) that Grype does not target, and merge three advisory sources rather than one feed.

OSV-Scanner is Google's OSV-native matcher, authoritative on the OSV schema and ranges; it wins when OSV.dev is your single source of truth. We treat OSV as one of three sources (merged with NVD CVSS and GHSA, MM1) and add CVSS/EPSS normalization (ADR-030), provenance, and policy/license compliance OSV-Scanner leaves out.

Snyk is a SaaS-first platform with a proprietary curated database, continuous monitoring, and fix PRs; it wins for teams wanting a managed service with remediation automation. We reject the SaaS model for a deterministic, offline-capable CLI (MM9) with no proprietary feed dependency, so scans are reproducible and air-gappable.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Numbers an expert computes before designing, each derived from §4.

  • C1 — Advisory index memory density. 256 MB ÷ 500 K advisories (§4.1) ≈ 512 B/advisory resident (purl-range index + CVSS); confirms the 2 M-advisory hard limit fits in the 512 MB index ceiling at ~256 B/entry compressed.
  • C2 — Dependency-graph memory. 100 K nodes × 800 B (§4.1) = 80 MB; the 500 K-node hard limit ⇒ 400 MB, inside the 512 MB→1 GB scan-RSS budget alongside the advisory index.
  • C3 — Match cost without an index. 5 K deps × 945 K advisories (NVD 230 K + GHSA 15 K + OSV 700 K, §4.1) = 4.7 B naive pairs — impossible in 15 s; the purl-keyed BTreeMap reduces this to deps × avg-advisories-per-purl (a handful), which is why C-level throughput (≥ 50 K pairs/s) suffices.
  • C4 — Per-dependency scan time. 500 deps ≤ 2 s, 5 K ≤ 15 s, 50 K ≤ 120 s (§4.1) ⇒ roughly 2.4–4 ms/dep end-to-end (parse + resolve + match) — near-linear, dominated by graph traversal cache misses (§4.2).
  • C5 — OSV full-sync rate. 500 K entries ÷ 5 min (§4.1) = ~1 670 entries/s decode+index, bounded by the ≤ 10 MB/s inbound feed cap, not CPU.
  • C6 — NVD rate-limit wall. Without an API key, 5 req/30 s = 1 req/6 s (§4.1); a delta of 600 records at 2 K/page = 300 pages ⇒ 30 min — which is why an API key (50 req/30 s, ~3 min) is operationally required for fresh NVD.
  • C7 — Provenance verification latency. Rekor p50 300 ms/component (§4.1): 5 K components serially = 25 min; with 4 async I/O workers (§4.1) and batching this drops to ~6 min, motivating parallel provenance and caching inclusion proofs.
  • C8 — File-descriptor budget. ≤ 128 FDs/scan (§4.1): manifest files + SQLite handles + Rekor TLS sockets; the 512 total hard limit caps concurrent monorepo workspace parses.
  • C9 — Disk footprint. Advisory index ≤ 2 GB (§4.1) for ~1 M advisories + JSON snapshots ⇒ ~2 KB/advisory on disk including the raw JSON retained for re-index.
  • C10 — Throughput headroom. ≥ 50 K component-advisory pairs/s (§4.1) via Rayon: a 50 K-dep monorepo with ~5 candidate advisories each = 250 K checks ⇒ ~5 s of pure matching, the rest of the 120 s p99 budget being parse + resolve + graph merge.
  • C11 — Version-comparison memo cache. LRU 100 K (version, req) pairs (§ Performance) ≈ 8 MB; hot ecosystems (npm/PyPI top-1000) hit it repeatedly (§4.2), turning O(parse) range checks into O(1) lookups.
  • C12 — Feed-staleness exposure. 24 h freshness SLA, 72 h warn (§4.1): at ~1 670 new/changed OSV entries/day (C5 basis), a feed 72 h stale could miss ~5 000 advisory updates — quantifying why StaleFeed must be a hard finding (MM8).

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each requires synthesizing ≥ 2 sections.

  1. Why does matching on package name alone produce wrong-ecosystem false positives, and what fixes it? (MM1 + §17 + §8)
  2. Why must the scanner read the lockfile rather than the manifest when both exist? (MM3 + §13 + §14)
  3. Why is "0 false negatives for in-range versions" a hard invariant while false positives are tolerable? (MM4 + §4.1 + §4.4)
  4. How is "version V in range R" decided differently for npm, PyPI, and Maven? (MM2 + ADR-009/ADR-010 + §17)
  5. Why can reachability re-rank but not suppress a finding by default? (MM5 + MM4 + §18)
  6. Trace a provenance verification from signature to SLSA level, naming each link and its failure mode. (MM6 + §19 + §4.4 Rekor timeout)
  7. What ground truth does typosquatting detection require, and what false positive appears without it? (MM7 + §20 + Capacity model)
  8. Why is a stale advisory feed the most dangerous failure mode, and how is it surfaced? (MM8 + §16 + §4.1)
  9. Why is bit-identical output a feature, and which two workflows depend on it? (MM9 + §22 + §23)
  10. What does the purl-keyed index buy over naive matching at 5 K deps × ~1 M advisories? (MM1 + Capacity C3 + §17)
  11. Why does NVD freshness effectively require an API key, quantitatively? (Capacity C6 + §4.1 + §16)
  12. How is the 1 GB scan-RSS ceiling shared between the dependency graph and the advisory index? (Capacity C1/C2 + §4.1 + §4.5)
  13. Why is container image-layer scanning deferred rather than core, and what would adding it change? (Deferred Features Register + Comparative Architecture + §1 scope)
  14. How does SBOM round-trip fidelity get tested, and what breaks downstream if it fails? (MM10 + ADR-029 + §15)
  15. Why are three advisory sources merged rather than trusting OSV alone? (MM1 + Comparative (OSV-Scanner) + ADR-030)
  16. How does provenance verification stay within latency budget for 5 K components? (Capacity C7 + §19 + §4.1 concurrency)
  17. What distinguishes this CLI's model from Snyk's SaaS, and why does determinism matter for air-gapped use? (MM9 + Comparative (Snyk) + Alternative Architectures)
  18. How does CVSS/EPSS normalization affect finding prioritization, and when is EPSS preferred? (ADR-030 + §18 + §4.1)
  19. Why does dependency-graph traversal dominate scan latency rather than matching? (Capacity C4/C10 + §4.2 + §14)
  20. What guarantees a malformed manifest cannot abort the whole scan? (§4.4 + §13 + §1 scope)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alt-A — Reachability-first scanner (call-graph gated).

deps --build call graph--> [ keep only findings whose vulnerable symbol is reachable ] --> report

Wins when: alert fatigue is the dominant problem and the languages have tractable static call graphs (Java, Go). Embodied by: Endor Labs, Snyk reachability. Rejected (as default) because: call-graph construction is language-specific and incomplete, so gating on it risks false negatives (violates MM4); reachability is used to re-rank, not suppress (MM5), and the heavier analysis is deferred.

Alt-B — Continuous SaaS monitoring service.

repos --webhook--> [ hosted service: re-scan on push, open fix PRs ] --> dashboard

Wins when: teams want zero local setup, continuous monitoring, and automated remediation PRs. Embodied by: Snyk, GitHub Dependabot. Rejected because: the blueprint targets a deterministic, offline-capable CLI/CI binary (MM9) with no proprietary feed or server dependency, so scans are reproducible and air-gappable.

Alt-C — SBOM-only (generate, don't match).

project --> [ enumerate components ] --> SBOM (SPDX/CycloneDX); matching is someone else's job

Wins when: you want a clean separation of inventory from policy, feeding SBOMs into a separate vulnerability service. Embodied by: Syft. Rejected because: the product goal is end-to-end find→match→prioritize→report; SBOM generation is one stage (§15), not the whole tool, though SBOM ingestion lets it interoperate with Syft-style producers.

Alt-D — OS/container-package-first scanner.

image --extract layers--> [ match OS packages (deb/rpm/apk) against distro CVEs ] --> report

Wins when: the primary risk surface is the base image and OS packages, not language dependencies. Embodied by: Trivy, Grype. Rejected (deferred) because: this blueprint focuses on application-dependency provenance and malicious-package detection; OCI image-layer scanning is captured in the Deferred Features Register.

Deferred Features Register

Every PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") anchor resolves here. Each row is out of the Phase-0–13 scope.

Feature Anchor Building Phase (if undeferred) Un-defer Trigger
Server / API (daemon) mode this Register row New scs-server crate IDE/CI integrations need a long-running REST/daemon endpoint
OCI container layer + image manifest scanning this Register row New scs-oci crate Users need base-image/OS-package CVEs alongside app deps (Alt-D)
ML-based maintainer-behaviour anomaly scoring this Register row New scs-ml stage Heuristic malicious-package signals (MM7) prove insufficient
eBPF runtime dependency tracing (reachability) this Register row Runtime-tracing adapter Static reachability is too imprecise and runtime evidence is required (Alt-A)
Registry mirror / private artifact repository support this Register row Registry-adapter layer Enterprises need scanning against private/internal registries
Shared NFS / network advisory index §4.3 (Scaling Model) Networked index backend Many concurrent scanner instances must share one advisory index (Capacity C1)

Glossary

Term Definition
Advisory Structured record (CVE/GHSA/OSV) describing a vulnerability in one or more package versions; contains AffectedRange list, CVSS score, fixed versions, aliases.
AffectedRange Half-open interval [introduced, fixed) within a single ecosystem; a component version v is affected iff introduced <= v < fixed (or v >= introduced when fixed is absent).
Attestation Signed metadata artifact (in-toto DSSE envelope) asserting facts (build provenance, test results, SLSA level) about a software artifact; verified by scs-provenance.
CAPEC Common Attack Pattern Enumeration and Classification; referenced as weakness classification alongside CWE in enriched advisories.
Component Single versioned package node in the dependency graph: { purl, version, hashes, licenses, source, direct: bool, scope: Scope }. Unit of analysis for vuln-matching and provenance.
Cosign Sigstore CLI + Go library for signing OCI artifacts and publishing signatures to Rekor; referenced as external verifier by scs-provenance.
CPE Common Platform Enumeration; NVD-native identifier (cpe:2.3:a:vendor:product:version:...); mapped to purl for matching in scs-vulndb.
CVSS Common Vulnerability Scoring System (v3.1/v4.0); base/temporal/environmental scores on 0.0–10.0 scale; drives Severity enum thresholds in scs-policy.
CVE Common Vulnerabilities and Exposures identifier (CVE-YYYY-NNNNN); canonical vuln ID issued by MITRE/CNAs; aliased by GHSA and OSV records.
CycloneDX SBOM standard (OWASP); JSON/XML schema for component inventory, vulnerabilities, licenses, dependencies; supported as emit/parse target in scs-sbom.
Dependency Confusion Attack (Birsan 2021): attacker publishes a package to a public registry with the same name as a private internal package at a higher version number, causing package managers to prefer it. Detected by scs-threat via registry-origin checks.
DependencyGraph DAG of Component nodes with typed edges; { roots, edges, scopes }; constructed by scs-resolve; iterated by scs-match + scs-provenance.
DSSE Dead Simple Signing Envelope (sigstore spec); JSON envelope { payload, payloadType, signatures }; wraps in-toto Link/Layout attestations.
Ecosystem Package registry namespace: cargo, npm, pypi, go, maven, gem; determines version-comparison semantics and advisory range format.
EPSS Exploit Prediction Scoring System; 0.0–1.0 probability that a CVE will be exploited in the wild within 30 days; supplements CVSS for prioritization in scs-policy.
Feed Snapshot Immutable, timestamped advisory dataset used for a scan run; identified by (source, etag/commit, timestamp); stored in scs-vulndb metadata; required for scan reproducibility.
Finding Output unit: { component, advisory, path: Vec<Purl>, severity, fixed: Option<Version>, vex_status }; carries full dep-chain to graph root.
Fulcio Sigstore CA that issues short-lived OIDC-bound code-signing certificates; certificates embedded in Sigstore signatures; verified via Rekor CT log.
GHSA GitHub Security Advisory; GitHub's advisory namespace (GHSA-xxxx-yyyy-zzzz); aliases CVE; primary feed source alongside OSV for scs-vulndb.
Hash Pinning Manifest-declared sha256/sha512 expected digest for a downloaded artifact; scs-manifest parses and scs-provenance verifies against actual fetched content.
in-toto Supply-chain integrity framework (Torres-Arias 2019); defines Link (step evidence) and Layout (policy) metadata types; scs-provenance verifies in-toto attestation chains.
License SPDX license expression (e.g., MIT, GPL-2.0-or-later, Apache-2.0 OR MIT); parsed per component; evaluated against allow/deny lists in scs-policy.
Lockfile Deterministic, fully-resolved package manifest (e.g., Cargo.lock, package-lock.json, go.sum); preferred input for scs-manifest; preferred over loose manifests for reproducibility.
Manifest Human-authored dependency declaration (Cargo.toml, package.json, requirements.txt); may contain version ranges; less precise than lockfile.
Malicious Install Script preinstall/postinstall (npm), setup.py hooks, build.rs anomalies that exfiltrate data or download payloads at install time; detected by scs-threat via static script analysis.
Match Soundness Invariant: every Component version that falls inside an advisory's AffectedRange is reported as a Finding; false negatives are defects.
OSV Open Source Vulnerability schema (Google); normalized JSON format unifying GHSA/CVE/PyPA/RustSec/etc.; primary feed format for scs-vulndb.
PackageId { ecosystem: Ecosystem, name: String }; canonical identifier stripped of version; used as index key in advisory lookups.
PEP 440 Python version specifier standard; defines epochs, pre/post/dev releases, and version operators (>=, ~=, !=, ===); implemented in scs-resolve/scs-match for PyPI.
Policy Declarative configuration (TOML) in scs-policy; contains severity thresholds, license allow/deny, VEX paths, waiver list, EPSS cutoffs, SLSA level requirements; produces gate decision.
Provenance Metadata proving where and how an artifact was built: { attestation, slsa_level, rekor_inclusion, verified: bool }; fail-closed invariant.
Purl Package URL (purl spec): pkg:<type>/<namespace>/<name>@<version>?<qualifiers>#<subpath>; canonical cross-ecosystem component identifier used throughout as primary key.
Rekor Sigstore's transparency log (Merkle tree); stores signature/attestation entries; scs-provenance verifies inclusion proofs via rekor-rs or REST API.
SARIF Static Analysis Results Interchange Format (OASIS); JSON schema for tool results; emitted by scs-report for GitHub/GitLab CI integration.
Scan Determinism Invariant: identical inputs (manifests/lockfiles) + identical advisory feed snapshot → identical finding set and report; requires canonical purl ordering, stable sort.
Scope Dependency classification: Prod (shipped), Dev (test/build tooling), Optional (feature-gated); propagated through graph; policy can gate on scope.
SBOM Software Bill of Materials; structured inventory of components, licenses, hashes, relationships; emitted as SPDX or CycloneDX by scs-sbom.
SemVer Semantic Versioning (semver.org); MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH[-pre][+build]; used by Cargo/npm ecosystems; implemented via semver crate in scs-resolve.
Sigstore Open-source project providing cosign (signing), Fulcio (CA), and Rekor (transparency log) for artifact signing and verification.
SLSA Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts; framework with levels 0–3 (4 in v1.0) encoding build integrity guarantees; scs-provenance maps attestation content to SLSA level.
SPDX Software Package Data Exchange (Linux Foundation); SBOM standard with tag-value and JSON formats; supported as emit/parse target in scs-sbom.
Typosquatting Attack: publish package with name closely resembling a popular package (reqeusts vs requests); scs-threat detects via edit-distance + phonetic similarity.
VEX Vulnerability Exploitability eXchange (OpenVEX / CSAF VEX); machine-readable statement that a known vuln does not affect a product in a specific context; consumed by scs-policy to suppress findings.
Version Parsed, ecosystem-specific version struct; supports comparison operators per ecosystem semantics; stored alongside VersionReq (range constraint).
VersionReq Version requirement/constraint; ecosystem-specific range expression (^1.2, >=1.0,<2.0, ~=3.5); evaluated by scs-resolve during constraint solving.
Withdrawn Advisory Advisory marked retracted/disputed; Advisory.withdrawn: Option<Time>; scs-match suppresses or flags findings with VEX-like status rather than treating as active.

Appendix A — File Inventory

Core type and manifest crates (scs-core, scs-manifest):

  src/lib.rs            — crate root; re-exports all public types
  src/purl.rs           — Purl newtype, parse/validate, ecosystem extraction
  src/package_id.rs     — PackageId struct, Eq/Hash/Ord impls

  src/version_req.rs    — VersionReq enum, satisfies() dispatch per ecosystem

  src/graph.rs          — DependencyGraph (petgraph wrapper), NodeIdx, edge types
  src/advisory.rs       — Advisory, AffectedRange, AliasSet structs
  src/finding.rs        — Finding struct, dep-path Vec<Purl>, VexStatus enum
  src/severity.rs       — Severity enum (Critical/High/Medium/Low/Info), Cvss struct
  src/sbom.rs           — Sbom enum (Spdx|CycloneDX), SbomComponent, SbomRelationship
  src/provenance.rs     — Provenance struct, SlsaLevel enum, RekorInclusion
  src/license.rs        — License newtype (SPDX expr), parse, copyleft detection
  src/policy.rs         — Policy config struct (severity threshold, license lists, VEX)
  src/error.rs          — ScsError enum (thiserror-derived), all crate error variants
  tests/types_test.rs   — round-trip + edge-case tests for core types

  src/lib.rs            — parser dispatch by detected ecosystem
  src/detect.rs         — filesystem heuristics to identify manifest/lockfile type
  src/npm.rs            — package.json + package-lock.json v2/v3 + yarn.lock parser
  src/cargo.rs          — Cargo.toml + Cargo.lock v3 parser, workspace support
  src/pypi.rs           — requirements.txt, setup.cfg, pyproject.toml (PEP 621) parser
  src/go.rs             — go.mod + go.sum parser, module path normalization
  src/maven.rs          — pom.xml parser (effective POM, property interpolation)
  src/gem.rs            — Gemfile + Gemfile.lock parser, gemspec basics

  src/hash.rs           — hash extraction from lockfiles, hex decode/verify helpers

  tests/fixtures/       — per-ecosystem sample manifests (real-world snapshots)
  tests/parse_test.rs   — roundtrip + edge-case coverage per parser

Graph, SBOM and vulnerability crates (scs-resolve, scs-sbom, scs-vulndb, scs-match):

scs-resolve/
  src/lib.rs            — resolve() entry point: Components -> DependencyGraph
  src/solver.rs         — constraint propagation + version selection (pubgrub adapter)

  src/lib.rs            — VulnDb trait, open/query API
